The last pair in the town had been obtained from the Ecu de France, by a gentleman who dined and supped at the Belle Étoile, and was obliged to proceed to Paris that night.

Who was the gentleman? Had he actually gone? Could he possibly be induced to wait till morning?

The gentleman was now upstairs getting his things together, and his name was Monsieur Droqville.

===Chinese===
读者可以想见，除了想躲开法律那套叫人不耐烦的盘查之外，我还有别的理由，巴不得尽早重新启程去巴黎。可想而知，当我得知当晚无论如何——好说歹说也罢、掏钱也罢——哪儿也弄不到马时，我是何等惊骇。全镇最后一对马，已被一位绅士从法兰西之盾旅馆弄到了手；那位绅士在美星旅馆用的午饭和晚饭，而且当夜非赶去巴黎不可。

那位绅士是谁？他当真走了吗？能不能劝他等到明早？

那位绅士此刻正在楼上收拾行装，他名叫德罗克维尔先生。

===Original===
I ran upstairs. I found my servant St. Clair in my room. At sight of him, for a moment, my thoughts were turned into a different channel.

“Well, St. Clair, tell me this moment who the lady is?” I demanded.

“The lady is the daughter or wife, it matters not which, of the Count de St. Alyre;﻿—the old gentleman who was so near being sliced like a cucumber tonight, I am informed, by the sword of the general whom Monsieur, by a turn of fortune, has put to bed of an apoplexy.”

“Hold your tongue, fool! The man’s beastly drunk﻿—he’s sulking﻿—he could talk if he liked﻿—who cares? Pack up my things. Which are Monsieur Droqville’s apartments?”

He knew, of course; he always knew everything.

===Chinese===
我跑上楼去，在自己房里见到了仆人圣克莱尔。一见到他，我的念头一时转到了另一条道上。

「喂，圣克莱尔，这就告诉我，那位夫人究竟是谁？」我追问道。

「那位夫人嘛，是德·圣阿利尔伯爵的女儿，或者妻子——是哪一个都无关紧要；就是今晚差一点儿像根黄瓜似的被切成片的那位老绅士——我听说，多亏时来运转，先生一杖把那位将军打成了中风，打发上了床。」

「住口，蠢货！那家伙烂醉如泥——他在赌气——他要想说话有的是话说——谁稀罕？收拾我的东西去。德罗克维尔先生住哪几间房？」

他自然知道；他从来什么都知道。

===Original===
Half an hour later Monsieur Droqville and I were travelling towards Paris, in my carriage, and with his horses. I ventured to ask the Marquis d’Harmonville, in a little while, whether the lady, who accompanied the Count, was certainly the Countess. “Has he not a daughter?”

“Yes;﻿—I believe a very beautiful and charming young lady﻿—I cannot say﻿—it may have been she, his daughter by an earlier marriage. I saw only the Count himself today.”

The Marquis was growing a little sleepy and, in a little while, he actually fell asleep in his corner. I dozed and nodded; but the Marquis slept like a top. He awoke only for a minute or two at the next posting-house, where he had fortunately secured horses by sending on his man, he told me.

===Chinese===
半个钟头后，德罗克维尔先生和我便乘着我的马车、用着他的马，向巴黎进发了。过了一会儿，我壮着胆子问德·哈蒙维尔侯爵：陪伯爵同行的那位夫人，究竟是不是伯爵夫人。「他没有女儿吗？」

「有的——我相信是位非常美丽可爱的年轻小姐——我说不准——那也许就是她，他前一次婚姻所生的女儿。我今天只见到了伯爵本人。」

侯爵渐渐有了倦意，不多时竟真的在他那个角落里睡着了。我不过打盹点头；侯爵却睡得死沉。到了下一个驿站他才醒了一两分钟——他告诉我，幸亏他先派了仆人赶在前头，才把马订妥。

===Original===
“You will excuse my being so dull a companion,” he said, “but till tonight I have had but two hours’ sleep, for more than sixty hours. I shall have a cup of coffee here; I have had my nap. Permit me to recommend you to do likewise. Their coffee is really excellent.” He ordered two cups of café noir, and waited, with his head from the window. “We will keep the cups,” he said, as he received them from the waiter, “and the tray. Thank you.”

There was a little delay as he paid for these things; and then he took in the little tray, and handed me a cup of coffee.

I declined the tray; so he placed it on his own knees, to act as a miniature table.

“I can’t endure being waited for and hurried,” he said, “I like to sip my coffee at leisure.”

I agreed. It really *was* the very perfection of coffee.

“I, like Monsieur le Marquis, have slept very little for the last two or three nights; and find it difficult to keep awake. This coffee will do wonders for me; it refreshes one so.”

===Chinese===
「恕我做了个这样乏味的旅伴，」他说，「可是到今晚为止，六十多个钟头里我只睡了两个钟头。我要在这儿喝杯咖啡；我的觉已经睡足了。请恕我奉劝您也照此办理。他们的咖啡实在好极了。」他叫了两杯黑咖啡（café noir），把头探出窗外等着。「杯子我们留下，」他从侍者手里接过咖啡时说，「托盘也留下。谢谢你。」

付这几样东西的钱时稍稍耽搁了一会儿；随后他把小托盘拿进车来，递给我一杯咖啡。

我不要托盘；他便把它放在自己膝上，权充一张小桌。

「我最受不了被人等候、被人催促，」他说，「我爱悠悠然地小口啜咖啡。」

我深表同感。这咖啡*当真*是十全十美。

「我同侯爵先生一样，这两三个晚上睡得极少，只觉得眼皮直打架。这咖啡对我必有奇效；它多提神啊。」

===Original===
Before we had half done, the carriage was again in motion.

For a time our coffee made us chatty, and our conversation was animated.

The Marquis was extremely good-natured, as well as clever, and gave me a brilliant and amusing account of Parisian life, schemes, and dangers, all put so as to furnish me with practical warnings of the most valuable kind.

In spite of the amusing and curious stories which the Marquis related, with so much point and colour, I felt myself again becoming gradually drowsy and dreamy.

Perceiving this, no doubt, the Marquis good-naturedly suffered our conversation to subside into silence. The window next him was open. He threw his cup out of it; and did the same kind office for mine, and finally the little tray flew after, and I heard it clank on the road; a valuable waif, no doubt, for some early wayfarer in wooden shoes.

===Chinese===
我们还没喝到一半，马车又开动了。

有一阵子，咖啡使我们谈兴大发，谈得热火朝天。

侯爵为人极和善，又极聪明，把巴黎的生活、圈套与危险讲得妙趣横生，处处点拨，等于给了我一大堆最切实用的忠告。

尽管侯爵讲的故事那么有滋有味、有声有色，我却觉得自己又渐渐昏昏欲睡、恍恍惚惚起来。

想必是察觉了这一点，侯爵好脾气地任由我们的谈话沉入寂静。他身旁的窗子开着。他把杯子扔了出去；又同样殷勤地替我扔了杯子，最后小托盘也跟着飞了出去，我听见它当啷一声落在路上；对哪个起早赶路、穿着木鞋的行人来说，这无疑是一件值钱的意外之财。

===Original===
I leaned back in my corner; I had my beloved souvenir﻿—my white rose﻿—close to my heart, folded, now, in white paper. It inspired all manner of romantic dreams. I began to grow more and more sleepy. But actual slumber did not come. I was still viewing, with my half-closed eyes, from my corner, diagonally, the interior of the carriage.

I wished for sleep; but the barrier between waking and sleeping seemed absolutely insurmountable; and instead, I entered into a state of novel and indescribable indolence.

The Marquis lifted his despatch-box from the floor, placed it on his knees, unlocked it, and took out what proved to be a lamp, which he hung with two hooks, attached to it, to the window opposite to him. He lighted it with a match, put on his spectacles, and taking out a bundle of letters, began to read them carefully.

We were making way very slowly. My impatience had hitherto employed four horses from stage to stage. We were in this emergency, only too happy to have secured two. But the difference in pace was depressing.

===Chinese===
我靠回自己的角落；我心爱的纪念物——那朵白玫瑰——贴着我的胸口，此刻已用白纸包起。它激起千般百种的浪漫梦想。我越来越困。然而真正的睡眠却迟迟不来。我仍旧眯着半闭的眼睛，从我的角落里斜望着马车的内部。

我渴望入睡；可醒与睡之间的那道屏障，似乎全然无法逾越；我便转而进入一种从未经历、难以言喻的懒倦状态。

侯爵把公文箱从车板上提起来，放到膝上，开了锁，取出一件东西，原来是一盏灯；灯上带着两只钩子，他把它挂到他对面的窗上，划火柴点亮，戴上眼镜，掏出一沓信，开始细细阅读。

我们的车走得非常慢。我的急性子先前一站一站都是用四匹马拉的。此番应急，能弄到两匹已是喜出望外。但这速度上的差别，实在叫人丧气。

===Original===
I grew tired of the monotony of seeing the spectacled Marquis reading, folding, and docketing, letter after letter. I wished to shut out the image which wearied me, but something prevented my being able to shut my eyes. I tried again and again; but, positively, I had lost the power of closing them.

I would have rubbed my eyes, but I could not stir my hand, my will no longer acted on my body﻿—I found that I could not move one joint, or muscle, no more than I could, by an effort of my will, have turned the carriage about.

Up to this I had experienced no sense of horror. Whatever it was, simple nightmare was not the cause. I was awfully frightened! Was I in a fit?

===Chinese===
看那位戴眼镜的侯爵一封接一封地读信、折信、编录，这单调劲儿叫我腻烦了。我想闭眼不看这令我疲倦的景象，却有什么东西不许我合上眼睛。我试了一遍又一遍；可是，千真万确，我已失去了合眼的能力。

我本想揉揉眼睛，手动不了；我的意志已不再支配我的身体——我发现自己一个关节、一块肌肉都动弹不得，正如我无论怎样运用意志，也无法叫马车掉头。

到这时为止，我还不曾感到恐惧。不管这是什么，单纯的噩梦绝非原因。我害怕极了！我这是发病了吗？

===Original===
It was horrible to see my good-natured companion pursue his occupation so serenely, when he might have dissipated my horrors by a single shake.

I made a stupendous exertion to call out but in vain; I repeated the effort again and again, with no result.

My companion now tied up his letters, and looked out of the window, humming an air from an opera. He drew back his head, and said, turning to me﻿—

“Yes, I see the lights; we shall be there in two or three minutes.”

He looked more closely at me, and with a kind smile, and a little shrug, he said, “Poor child! how fatigued he must have been﻿—how profoundly he sleeps! when the carriage stops he will waken.”

===Chinese===
眼见我这和气的旅伴如此安之若素地忙他的事——而他只消摇我一下，便能驱散我的恐惧——真是可怕。

我使出一股惊天动地的力气想喊出声，却徒劳无功；我一而再、再而三地努力，毫无结果。

我的旅伴这时把信捆好，望着窗外，哼着一段歌剧里的调子。他缩回头来，转身朝我说道——

「是的，我看见灯火了；再过两三分钟就到。」

他更凑近地看看我，带着和蔼的微笑，微微一耸肩，说道：「可怜的孩子！他一定是累坏了——睡得多沉啊！等马车一停，他就该醒了。」

===Original===
He then replaced his letters in the despatch-box, locked it, put his spectacles in his pocket, and again looked out of the window.

We had entered a little town. I suppose it was past two o’clock by this time. The carriage drew up, I saw an inn-door open, and a light issuing from it.

“Here we are!” said my companion, turning gaily to me. But I did not awake.

“Yes, how tired he must have been!” he exclaimed, after he had waited for an answer.

===Chinese===
随后他把信放回公文箱，锁上，眼镜揣进口袋，又望向窗外。

我们已进了一座小镇。我估摸这时已过了两点。马车停住，我看见一家客栈的门开了，一线灯光从门里泻出。

「到啦！」我的旅伴转过脸来，欢欢喜喜地对我说。可我没有醒。

「可不是嘛，他一定累坏了！」等了一会儿不见回答，他大声说道。

===Original===
My servant was at the carriage door, and opened it.

“Your master sleeps soundly, he is so fatigued! It would be cruel to disturb him. You and I will go in, while they change the horses, and take some refreshment, and choose something that Monsieur Beckett will like to take in the carriage, for when he awakes by-and-by, he will, I am sure, be hungry.”

He trimmed his lamp, poured in some oil; and taking care not to disturb me, with another kind smile, and another word of caution to my servant, he got out, and I heard him talking to St. Clair, as they entered the inn-door, and I was left in my corner, in the carriage, in the same state.

===Chinese===
我的仆人到了车门边，把门打开。

「你的主人睡得真沉，他实在太累了！惊动他就太狠心了。趁他们换马，你和我进去用点点心，再挑些贝克特先生爱吃的带上车；等他一会儿醒来，我敢说，他准会饿的。」

他剪了剪灯芯，添了些油；小心着不惊动我，又给了我的仆人一句嘱咐、一个和蔼的微笑，便下了车。我听见他同圣克莱尔说着话走进客栈门去；而我，被留在马车里我的角落中，还是那副样子。

## VIII A Three Minutes’ Visit / VIII 三分钟的来访

===Original===
I have suffered extreme and protracted bodily pain, at different periods of my life, but anything like that misery, thank God, I never endured before or since. I earnestly hope it may not resemble any type of death, to which we are liable. I was, indeed, a spirit in prison; and unspeakable was my dumb and unmoving agony.

The power of thought remained clear and active. Dull terror filled my mind. How would this end? Was it actual death?

You will understand that my faculty of observing was unimpaired. I could hear and see anything as distinctly as ever I did in my life. It was simply that my will had, as it were, lost its hold of my body.

===Chinese===
我一生中不同时期都受过剧烈而绵长的肉体疼痛，但那样的苦楚，感谢上帝，我从前不曾、后来也再不曾忍受过。我恳切地希望，它并不像我们任何一种可能遭受的死亡。我实在是一座囚牢中的魂灵；那种不能言、不能动的煎熬，难以言表。

思维的能力依旧清明而活跃。迟钝的恐怖充塞了我的心。这将如何收场？这就是真正的死亡吗？

你会明白，我的观察力丝毫无损。我听什么、看什么，都同平生任何时候一样清楚。只是我的意志，可以说，失去了对我身体的掌握。

===Original===
I told you that the Marquis d’Harmonville had not extinguished his carriage lamp on going into this village inn. I was listening intently, longing for his return, which might result, by some lucky accident, in awaking me from my catalepsy.

Without any sound of steps approaching, to announce an arrival, the carriage-door suddenly opened, and a total stranger got in silently, and shut the door.

The lamp gave about as strong a light as a wax-candle, so I could see the intruder perfectly. He was a young man, with a dark grey, loose surtout, made with a sort of hood, which was pulled over his head. I thought, as he moved, that I saw the gold band of a military undress cap under it; and I certainly saw the lace and buttons of a uniform, on the cuffs of the coat that were visible under the wide sleeves of his outside wrapper.

===Chinese===
我告诉过你，德·哈蒙维尔侯爵走进这家乡村客栈时，并没有吹灭车灯。我凝神谛听着，盼着他回来——说不定会有一线侥幸，把我从这僵住症（catalepsy）中惊醒。

没有任何渐近的脚步声通报来人，车门忽然开了，一个素不相识的人无声地钻进来，随手关上了门。

那盏灯大约有蜡烛般的光亮，所以我把这不速之客看得清清楚楚。他是个年轻人，穿一件深灰色的宽大外套，连着一顶兜帽，罩在头上。他一动之间，我仿佛看见帽子底下露出军便帽的金箍；我还分明看见，从他外面这件宽袖罩衣底下露出的上衣袖口上，缀着制服的绦带和纽扣。

===Original===
This young man had thick moustaches, and an imperial, and I observed that he had a red scar running upward from his lip across his cheek.

He entered, shut the door softly, and sat down beside me. It was all done in a moment; leaning toward me, and shading his eyes with his gloved hand, he examined my face closely, for a few seconds.

This man had come as noiselessly as a ghost; and everything he did was accomplished with the rapidity and decision, that indicated a well defined and prearranged plan. His designs were evidently sinister. I thought he was going to rob, and, perhaps, murder me. I lay, nevertheless, like a corpse under his hands. He inserted his hand in my breast pocket, from which he took my precious white rose and all the letters it contained, among which was a paper of some consequence to me.

===Chinese===
这个年轻人留着两撇浓髭和一撮下巴须；我还注意到，他有一道红色的疤痕，从嘴唇向上横过面颊。

他进来，轻轻关上门，在我身旁坐下。一切都在转瞬间完成；他俯身向我，用手套遮着眼睛，把我的脸凑近端详了几秒钟。

这个人来得像鬼魅一样无声；他做的每一件事，都带着那种显示出周密预定计划的速度和决断。他的来意显然不祥。我以为他要抢劫，说不定还要杀害我。然而我躺在他的手下，像一具死尸。他把手探进我胸前的口袋，从中取走了我珍贵的白玫瑰和口袋里所有的信件，其中有一份对我不无干系的文书。

===Original===
My letters he glanced at. They were plainly not what he wanted. My precious rose, too, he laid aside with them. It was evidently about the paper I have mentioned, that he was concerned; for the moment he opened it, he began with a pencil, in a small pocketbook, to make rapid notes of its contents.

This man seemed to glide through his work with a noiseless and cool celerity which argued, I thought, the training of the police-department.

He rearranged the papers, possibly in the very order in which he had found them, replaced them in my breast-pocket, and was gone.

===Chinese===
我的信他只扫了一眼。它们显然不是他要找的东西。我珍贵的玫瑰，也被他同信放在了一边。他所关切的，显然就是我提到的那份文书；因为他一展开它，便立刻掏出一个小记事本，用铅笔飞快地摘录其内容。

这个人干起事来，带着一种无声而冷静的迅捷，依我看，透着警务部门的训练有素。

他把纸页重新理好——多半正是照他发现时的原样——放回我的胸前口袋，人就不见了。

===Original===
His visit, I think, did not quite last three minutes. Very soon after his disappearance, I heard the voice of the Marquis once more. He got in, and I saw him look at me, and smile, half envying me, I fancied, my sound repose. If he had but known all!

He resumed his reading and docketing, by the light of the little lamp which had just subserved the purposes of a spy.

We were now out of the town, pursuing our journey at the same moderate pace. We had left the scene of my police visit, as I should have termed it, now two leagues behind us, when I suddenly felt a strange throbbing in one ear, and a sensation as if air passed through it into my throat. It seemed as if a bubble of air, formed deep in my ear, swelled, and burst there. The indescribable tension of my brain seemed all at once to give way; there was an odd humming in my head, and a sort of vibration through every nerve of my body, such as I have experienced in a limb that has been, in popular phraseology, asleep. I uttered a cry and half rose from my seat, and then fell back trembling, and with a sense of mortal faintness.

===Chinese===
他的来访，我想，不到三分钟。他消失后不久，我又听见了侯爵的声音。他上了车，我看见他望着我微笑；我想，他是半带艳羡我这酣畅的安眠。他哪里知道底细！

他就着那盏小灯的灯光，重新读信、编录——这盏灯方才还为一个密探效过劳。

这时我们已出了镇子，仍以同样的中等速度赶路。我所谓的「警务来访」发生的地方，已被我们抛在身后两里格；忽然，我感到一只耳朵里一阵奇异的搏动，还有一种空气穿耳而入、直通喉咙的感觉。仿佛一个气泡在耳朵深处形成、胀大，在那里破掉了。我脑中那难以言喻的紧张，仿佛霎时间松开了；头里嗡嗡作响，全身上下每一条神经都掠过一阵麻颤，就像俗话说的「腿睡麻了」的时候那样。我喊出一声，从座位上半撑起来，随即颤抖着跌回去，带着一种致命的虚弱感。

===Original===
The Marquis stared at me, took my hand, and earnestly asked if I was ill. I could answer only with a deep groan.

Gradually the process of restoration was completed; and I was able, though very faintly, to tell him how very ill I had been; and then to describe the violation of my letters, during the time of his absence from the carriage.

“Good heaven!” he exclaimed, “the miscreant did not get at my dispatch-box?”

I satisfied him, so far as I had observed, on that point. He placed the box on the seat beside him, and opened and examined its contents very minutely.

===Chinese===
侯爵愕然望着我，握住我的手，恳切地问我是不是病了。我只能报以一声深深的呻吟。

复原的过程渐渐完成；我总算能够——尽管气息微弱——告诉他我方才病得多么厉害；接着又描述了他离开马车的那段时间里，我的信件如何被人翻看了。

「天哪！」他失声道，「那恶棍没动我的公文箱吧？」

就我所见，我在这一点上使他放了心。他把箱子放到身旁的座位上，打开来，把里面的东西极仔细地查了一遍。

===Original===
“Yes, undisturbed; all safe, thank heaven!” he murmured. “There are half-a-dozen letters here, that I would not have some people read, for a great deal.”

He now asked with a very kind anxiety all about the illness I complained of. When he had heard me, he said﻿—

“A friend of mine once had an attack as like yours as possible. It was on board-ship, and followed a state of high excitement. He was a brave man like you; and was called on to exert both his strength and his courage suddenly. An hour or two after, fatigue overpowered him, and he appeared to fall into a sound sleep. He really sank into a state which he afterwards described so, that I think it must have been precisely the same affection as yours.”

“I am happy to think that my attack was not unique. Did he ever experience a return of it?”

“I knew him for years after, and never heard of any such thing. What strikes me is a parallel in the predisposing causes of each attack. Your unexpected, and gallant hand-to-hand encounter, at such desperate odds, with an experienced swordsman, like that insane colonel of dragoons, your fatigue, and, finally, your composing yourself, as my other friend did, to sleep.”

===Chinese===
「还好，没被动过；都安然无恙，谢天谢地！」他喃喃道，「这里面有半打信，出多大的价钱我也不愿让某些人读到。」

接着他又满怀善意地细细询问我所诉说的病况。听我讲完，他说——

「我有个朋友，从前发过一次病，同您这次像得不能再像。那是在船上，事先正处在高度亢奋之中。他同您一样是个勇士；突然之间被迫拿出全部的力气和胆量。一两个钟头之后，疲劳压倒了他，他看上去沉入了酣睡。他实际上是陷入了一种状态——他后来描述得那么清楚，我想必定同您这毛病一模一样。」

「想到我这病并非独一份，我倒高兴。他后来复发过吗？」

「此后我同他相识多年，从未听说过那样的事。叫我注意的是，两次发病的诱因如出一辙：您那场出乎意料的、英勇的白刃相搏——同那样一个老练的剑客、那个疯子龙骑兵上校，以那样悬殊的形势相搏；您的疲劳；最后，您又同我那位朋友一样，安顿自己入睡。」

===Original===
“I wish,” he resumed, “one could make out who that *coquin* was, who examined your letters. It is not worth turning back, however, because we should learn nothing. Those people always manage so adroitly. I am satisfied, however, that he must have been an agent of the police. A rogue of any other kind would have robbed you.”

I talked very little, being ill and exhausted, but the Marquis talked on agreeably.

“We grow so intimate,” said he, at last, “that I must remind you that I am not, for the present, the Marquis d’Harmonville, but only Monsieur Droqville; nevertheless, when we get to Paris, although I cannot see you often, I may be of use. I shall ask you to name to me the hotel at which you mean to put up; because the Marquis being, as you are aware, on his travels, the Hotel d’Harmonville is, for the present, tenanted only by two or three old servants, who must not even see Monsieur Droqville. That gentleman will, nevertheless, contrive to get you access to the box of Monsieur le Marquis, at the Opera; as well, possibly, as to other places more difficult; and so soon as the diplomatic office of the Marquis d’Harmonville is ended, and he at liberty to declare himself, he will not excuse his friend, Monsieur Beckett, from fulfilling his promise to visit him this autumn at the Château d’Harmonville.”

===Chinese===
「我但愿，」他又说，「能查个明白，那个翻看您信件的*无赖*（coquin）究竟是谁。不过，掉头回去不值得，我们什么也查不出来。那种人行事永远滴水不漏。可我确信，他必定是警方的密探。换了别的任何一类恶棍，早就把您抢了。」

我病着，又精疲力竭，话说得很少；侯爵却谈锋甚健，娓娓动听。

「我们越来越亲近了，」他终于说，「那我必须提醒您：眼下的我，不是德·哈蒙维尔侯爵，只是德罗克维尔先生。不过到了巴黎，虽然我不能常同您见面，或许还用得上我。我要请您告诉我，您打算下榻哪家旅馆；因为如您所知，侯爵正在旅途中，德·哈蒙维尔府上眼下只住着两三个老仆人，他们连德罗克维尔先生也见不得。尽管如此，那位先生自有办法让您进歌剧院里侯爵先生的包厢；说不定还能进另外几处更难进的地方；而一旦德·哈蒙维尔侯爵的外交差事办完，得以恢复本来身份，他是不会放过他的朋友贝克特先生的——贝克特先生答应过今年秋天到德·哈蒙维尔城堡拜访他，这诺言必须兑现。」

===Original===
You may be sure I thanked the Marquis.

The nearer we got to Paris, the more I valued his protection. The countenance of a great man on the spot, just then, taking so kind an interest in the stranger whom he had, as it were, blundered upon, might make my visit ever so many degrees more delightful than I had anticipated.

Nothing could be more gracious than the manner and looks of the Marquis; and, as I still thanked him, the carriage suddenly stopped in front of the place where a relay of horses awaited us, and where, as it turned out, we were to part.

===Chinese===
你尽可相信，我对侯爵千恩万谢。

离巴黎越近，我越觉得他的照拂可贵。一位大人物当时正在当地，对这样一位——可以说是误打误撞结识的——外乡人如此善意地垂青，足以使我此行的乐趣，比我原先预料的平添不知多少倍。

侯爵的态度和神情再温文不过了；我还在道谢，马车忽然停在一处换马的地方——后来才知道，我们就要在那里分手了。

## IX Gossip and Counsel / IX 闲话与忠告

===Original===
My eventful journey was over, at last. I sat in my hotel window looking out upon brilliant Paris, which had, in a moment, recovered all its gaiety, and more than its accustomed bustle. Everyone has read of the kind of excitement that followed the catastrophe of Napoleon, and the second restoration of the Bourbons. I need not, therefore, even if, at this distance, I could, recall and describe my experiences and impressions of the peculiar aspect of Paris, in those strange times. It was, to be sure, my first visit. But, often as I have seen it since, I don’t think I ever saw that delightful capital in a state, pleasurably, so excited and exciting.

I had been two days in Paris, and had seen all sorts of sights, and experienced none of that rudeness and insolence of which others complained, from the exasperated officers of the defeated French army.

I must say this, also. My romance had taken complete possession of me; and the chance of seeing the object of my dream, gave a secret and delightful interest to my rambles and drives in the streets and environs, and my visits to the galleries and other sights of the metropolis.

I had neither seen nor heard of Count or Countess, nor had the Marquis d’Harmonville made any sign. I had quite recovered the strange indisposition under which I had suffered during my night journey.

===Chinese===
我这趟多事的旅程总算结束了。我坐在旅馆窗前，眺望着光辉灿烂的巴黎——它转眼间已恢复了全部的欢快，还添了不止平常的喧闹。拿破仑覆亡、波旁王朝第二次复辟之后那种举国骚动的情景，人人都读到过。因此，即便隔着这么多年我还回想得起、描绘得出，我也无须追述我在那些奇异时日里对巴黎特殊面貌的亲历与观感。这的确是我头一回到访。可后来虽屡屡重见，我不记得再见过这座可爱的都城处于这样一种令人愉悦的、如此亢奋而又如此激荡人心的状态。

我到巴黎已经两天，各处景致都看过了，却不曾领教别人抱怨过的那种吃了败仗的法国军官怒气冲冲的粗鲁无礼。

还有一句也得说：我的罗曼史已把我完全占据；只要有可能撞见我梦中的那个人，我在街巷郊野的漫步驱车、在都城画廊名胜的游览，便都平添了一种秘而不宣的、甜滋滋的兴味。

伯爵和伯爵夫人我既没见着，也没听到消息；德·哈蒙维尔侯爵也不曾有半点表示。夜路上害的那场古怪的不适，我已完全复原了。

===Original===
It was now evening, and I was beginning to fear that my patrician acquaintance had quite forgotten me, when the waiter presented me the card of “Monsieur Droqville;” and, with no small elation and hurry, I desired him to show the gentleman up.

In came the Marquis d’Harmonville, kind and gracious as ever.

“I am a night-bird at present,” said he, so soon as we had exchanged the little speeches which are usual. “I keep in the shade, during the daytime, and even now I hardly ventured to come in a close carriage. The friends for whom I have undertaken a rather critical service, have so ordained it. They think all is lost, if I am known to be in Paris. First let me present you with these orders for my box. I am so vexed that I cannot command it oftener during the next fortnight; during my absence, I had directed my secretary to give it for any night to the first of my friends who might apply, and the result is, that I find next to nothing left at my disposal.”

I thanked him very much.

===Chinese===
这时已是傍晚，我正开始担心我那位贵族相识把我忘了个干净，侍者却给我递上了「德罗克维尔先生」的名片；我按捺不住心头的高兴和忙乱，立刻请他把这位先生请上楼来。

进来的是德·哈蒙维尔侯爵，一如往常那般和蔼可亲。

照例的寒暄一完，他便说：「我眼下是只夜鸟儿。白天我藏在不亮的地方，就是现在，也只敢关着马车窗来。托我办这桩相当紧要的差事的朋友们，如此吩咐了下来。他们认为，一旦让人知道我在巴黎，全盘皆输。先让我把这几张我包厢的入场券送给您。真叫我不痛快，往后两个星期我没法更多地支配它；我不在的期间，曾吩咐秘书：无论哪一晚，先来申请的朋友就先给；结果是，我发现留给自己支配的几乎一无所剩。」

我对他千恩万谢。

===Original===
“And now, a word, in my office of Mentor. You have not come here, of course, without introductions?”

I produced half-a-dozen letters, the addresses of which he looked at.

“Don’t mind these letters,” he said. “I will introduce you. I will take you myself from house to house. One friend at your side is worth many letters. Make no intimities, no acquaintances, until then. You young men like best to exhaust the public amusements of a great city, before embarrassing yourself with the engagements of society. Go to all these. It will occupy you, day and night, for at least three weeks. When this is over, I shall be at liberty, and will myself introduce you to the brilliant but comparatively quiet routine of society. Place yourself in my hands; and in Paris remember, when once in society, you are always there.”

I thanked him very much, and promised to follow his counsels implicitly.

===Chinese===
「现在，以我良师（Mentor）的身份说一句话。您当然不会是毫无引荐信就来的吧？」

我掏出半打介绍信来，他看了看信封上的称呼。

「这些信不必在意，」他说，「我来引荐您。我亲自带您挨家挨户去拜会。身边有一位朋友，胜过多少封信。在那之前，切勿深交，也不要结识什么人。你们年轻人最喜欢先把大城市的公共玩乐尝个遍，再来让社交的义务绊住手脚。这些尽管都去。够您日夜消遣，至少三个星期。完了之后，我就得空了，亲自引您进入社交界那华彩而相对清静的常规。把自己交到我手里；还要记住，在巴黎，一旦进了社交界，就终身都在里面了。」

我感激不尽，答应对他的一应指点言听计从。

===Original===
He seemed pleased, and said﻿—

“I shall now tell you some of the places you ought to go to. Take your map, and write letters or numbers upon the points I will indicate, and we will make out a little list. All the places that I shall mention to you are worth seeing.”

In this methodical way, and with a great deal of amusing and scandalous anecdote, he furnished me with a catalogue and a guide, which, to a seeker of novelty and pleasure, was invaluable.

“In a fortnight, perhaps in a week,” he said, “I shall be at leisure to be of real use to you. In the meantime, be on your guard. You must not play; you will be robbed if you do. Remember, you are surrounded, here, by plausible swindlers and villains of all kinds, who subsist by devouring strangers. Trust no one but those you know.”

===Chinese===
他显得很高兴，说道——

「我这就告诉您几个该去的地方。拿出您的地图，在我指出的地点上标上字母或号码，咱们列一张小单子。我向您提到的这些地方，都值得一去。」

就这样有条不紊，又夹着大量妙趣横生、骇人听闻的轶闻，他给我编成了一份目录和一份指南；对一个寻求新奇与享乐的人，这实在是无价之宝。

「过两个星期，也许一个星期，」他说，「我就得空，能真正帮上您的忙了。在此之前，务必警惕。您不可以赌；一赌就要被抢。记住，您在这里，四面八方都是满口圆谎的骗子和各色恶棍，他们靠吞吃外乡人过活。除了相识的人，谁也别信。」

===Original===
I thanked him again, and promised to profit by his advice. But my heart was too full of the beautiful lady of the Belle Étoile, to allow our interview to close without an effort to learn something about her. I therefore asked for the Count and Countess de St. Alyre, whom I had had the good fortune to extricate from an extremely unpleasant row in the hall of the inn.

Alas! he had not seen them since. He did not know where they were staying. They had a fine old house only a few leagues from Paris; but he thought it probable that they would remain, for a few days at least, in the city, as preparations would, no doubt, be necessary, after so long an absence, for their reception at home.

“How long have they been away?”

“About eight months, I think.”

“They are poor, I think you said?”

===Chinese===
我又道了谢，答应从他的忠告里得益。可我心里满盛着美星旅馆那位美丽的夫人，总不能眼看着这次会面收场，而不想法子打听到她的一点消息。于是我便问起居·圣阿利尔伯爵和伯爵夫人来——我曾有幸把他们从客栈门厅一场极不体面的吵闹里解救出来。

唉！他后来没有再见过他们。他不知道他们下落何处。他们在离巴黎只有几里格的地方有一座漂亮的古老宅邸；不过他想，他们多半会在城里至少再待几天，因为出门这么久，家里总得先作些准备，才好迎接他们回去。

「他们出门多久了？」

「大约八个月，我想。」

「我记得您说过，他们不算阔？」

===Original===
“What *you* would consider poor. But, Monsieur, the Count has an income which affords them the comforts, and even the elegancies of life, living as they do, in a very quiet and retired way, in this cheap country.”

“Then they are very happy?”

“One would say they *ought* to be happy.”

“And what prevents?”

“He is jealous.”

“But his wife﻿—she gives him no cause?”

“I am afraid she does.”

“How, Monsieur?”

“I always thought she was a little too﻿—a *great deal* too﻿—”

“Too *what*, Monsieur?”

===Chinese===
「是按*您的*标准算穷。可是先生，伯爵有一份进项，够他们过得舒舒服服，甚至不乏雅致——既然他们在这个物价低廉的国度里，过的是那么清静深居的日子。」

「那么他们很幸福了？」

「换了谁都要说，他们*理应*幸福。」

「那是什么妨碍了他们呢？」

「他吃醋。」

「可他的妻子——总没有给他口实吧？」

「恐怕恰恰有。」

「怎么讲，先生？」

「我一向觉得她有点太——*太*得厉害——」

「太*什么*，先生？」

===Original===
“Too handsome. But although she has remarkably fine eyes, exquisite features, and the most delicate complexion in the world, I believe that she is a woman of probity. You have never seen her?”

“There was a lady, muffled up in a cloak, with a very thick veil on, the other night, in the hall of the Belle Étoile, when I broke that fellow’s head who was bullying the old Count. But her veil was so thick I could not see a feature through it.” My answer was diplomatic, you observe. “She may have been the Count’s daughter. Do they quarrel?”

“Who, he and his wife?”

“Yes.”

“A little.”

“Oh! and what do they quarrel about?”

===Chinese===
「太漂亮。不过，尽管她有出众的眼睛、精致的五官和世上最娇嫩的肤色，我相信她是个品行端正的女人。您从未见过她？」

「前几夜在美星旅馆的门厅里，倒有一位夫人，裹着斗篷，罩着厚厚的面纱——正是我敲破那个欺侮老伯爵的家伙的脑袋的时候。只是她那面纱太厚，我连一个五官也没能看透。」你瞧，我的回答颇有外交辞令。「她也许就是伯爵的女儿。他们夫妻吵架吗？」

「谁，他和他妻子？」

「是。」

「吵一点。」

「哦！他们为什么吵呢？」

===Original===
“It is a long story; about the lady’s diamonds. They are valuable﻿—they are worth, La Perelleuse says, about a million of francs. The Count wishes them sold and turned into revenue, which he offers to settle as she pleases. The Countess, whose they are, resists, and for a reason which, I rather think, she can’t disclose to him.”

“And pray what is that?” I asked, my curiosity a good deal piqued.

“She is thinking, I conjecture, how well she will look in them when she marries her second husband.”

“Oh?﻿—yes, to be sure. But the Count de St. Alyre is a good man?”

“Admirable, and extremely intelligent.”

===Chinese===
「说来话长；为的是夫人的钻石。那些钻石很值钱——据拉佩勒勒斯（La Perelleuse）说，约值一百万法郎。伯爵想叫她卖掉，折成进项，并许诺随她的意思支配。钻石是伯爵夫人的，她不肯，而且——我颇疑心——是为着一个不便对他明说的缘故。」

「请问是什么缘故？」我问，好奇心被大大勾了起来。

「我猜呀，她是在盘算：等她嫁第二任丈夫的时候，戴上它们该有多好看。」

「哦？——是，可不是嘛。可德·圣阿利尔伯爵是位好人吧？」

「好得很，而且极有才智。」

===Original===
“I should wish so much to be presented to the Count: you tell me he’s so﻿—”

“So agreeably married. But they are living quite out of the world. He takes her now and then to the Opera, or to a public entertainment; but that is all.”

“And he must remember so much of the old regime, and so many of the scenes of the revolution!”

“Yes, the very man for a philosopher, like you! And he falls asleep after dinner; and his wife don’t. But, seriously, he has retired from the gay and the great world, and has grown apathetic; and so has his wife; and nothing seems to interest her now, not even﻿—her husband!”

The Marquis stood up to take his leave.

“Don’t risk your money,” said he. “You will soon have an opportunity of laying out some of it to great advantage. Several collections of really good pictures, belonging to persons who have mixed themselves up in this Bonapartist restoration, must come within a few weeks to the hammer. You can do wonders when these sales commence. There will be startling bargains! Reserve yourself for them. I shall let you know all about it. By the by,” he said, stopping short as he approached the door, “I was so near forgetting. There is to be, next week, the very thing you would enjoy so much, because you see so little of it in England﻿—I mean a *bal masqué*, conducted, it is said, with more than usual splendour. It takes place at Versailles﻿—all the world will be there; there is such a rush for cards! But I think I may promise you one. Good night! Adieu!”

===Chinese===
「我真巴不得有人把我引见给伯爵：您说他那么——」

「婚配得那么美满。不过他们过着完全与世隔绝的日子。他偶尔带她去去歌剧院，或赴个公共娱乐；仅此而已。」

「再说，旧制度的种种、大革命的一幕幕，他该记得多少啊！」

「正是，像您这样的哲人，找他正合适！而且他饭后要打瞌睡；他的夫人不睡。不过说正经的，他已从浮华的上流大世界里退了出来，变得心灰意懒；他的夫人也一样；如今似乎没有什么能提起她的兴致了，连——她的丈夫也不例外！」

侯爵站起身来告辞。

「别拿您的钱去冒险，」他说，「您很快就有机会把其中一部分花在刀刃上。好几批真正的好画，主人都是卷进这场波拿巴复辟里的人物，不出几个星期就要上拍卖槌。开拍之时，您大可大展身手。会有惊人的便宜货！养精蓄锐等着吧。一切内情我会通知您。对了，」他走到门口忽然站住，「我差点忘了。下星期有一件您准会尽兴的事，因为这种场面在英国难得一见——我指的是一场*化装舞会*（bal masqué）；据说筹办得比往常更加华丽。地点在凡尔赛——全巴黎的人都要去；入场券抢破头！不过我想，我可以给您弄到一张。晚安！再会！」

## X The Black Veil / X 黑纱

===Original===
Speaking the language fluently and with unlimited money, there was nothing to prevent my enjoying all that was enjoyable in the French capital. You may easily suppose how two days were passed. At the end of that time, and at about the same hour, Monsieur Droqville called again.

Courtly, good-natured, gay, as usual, he told me that the masquerade ball was fixed for the next Wednesday, and that he had applied for a card for me.

===Chinese===
我法语说得流利，钱又多得花不完，在法兰西首都，凡是赏心乐事，没有一样拦得住我。这两天是怎么过的，你们不难想象。到了第二天头上，也差不多是同一时辰，德罗克维尔先生（Monsieur Droqville）又来拜访了。

他还是老样子，温文尔雅、和蔼可亲、兴致勃勃，告诉我说化装舞会定在下星期三，他已经替我申请了一张入场券。

===Original===
How awfully unlucky. I was so afraid I should not be able to go.

He stared at me for a moment with a suspicious and menacing look which I did not understand, in silence, and then inquired, rather sharply.

“And will Monsieur Beckett be good enough to say, why not?”

I was a little surprised, but answered the simple truth: I had made an engagement for that evening with two or three English friends, and did not see how I could.

===Chinese===
真是倒霉透顶。我还一直生怕自己去不成呢。

他默不作声地盯了我片刻，目光里含着一种我捉摸不透的疑忌与威吓，随即相当尖刻地质问道：

「贝克特先生可否赏个脸，说说为什么去不成？」

我有点吃惊，但还是照实答了：那天晚上我已约了两三位英国朋友小聚，实在想不出怎样才能脱身。

===Original===
“Just so! You English, wherever you are, always look out for your English boors, your beer and ‘*bifstek*’; and when you come here, instead of trying to learn something of the people you visit, and pretend to study, you are guzzling, and swearing, and smoking with one another, and no wiser or more polished at the end of your travels than if you had been all the time carousing in a booth at Greenwich.”

He laughed sarcastically, and looked as if he could have poisoned me.

“There it is,” said he, throwing the card on the table. “Take it or leave it, just as you please. I suppose I shall have my trouble for my pains; but it is not usual when a man, such as I, takes trouble, asks a favour, and secures a privilege for an acquaintance, to treat him so.”

===Chinese===
「正是如此！你们英国人，不管走到哪儿，永远只寻摸你们英国的老粗，抱着你们的啤酒和『牛排』（bifstek）不放；到了这里，也不设法了解一点东道国百姓的情形——嘴上还假装考察——反倒凑在一处大吃大喝、骂骂咧咧、吞云吐雾，一趟旅行下来，学问没长进，气度没修琢，倒好像整日泡在格林尼治的凉棚里喝得烂醉一般。」

他讥诮地笑了笑，那副神情，仿佛恨不得把我毒死才好。

「在这儿，」他说着，把那张卡片往桌上一摔。「要不要随你的便。看来我这一番心血是白费了；不过，像我这样的人，肯劳神费力、开口求人、替一位相识谋下这份体面，人家却这样待他，天下可没有这样的礼数。」

===Original===
This was astonishingly impertinent!

I was shocked, offended, penitent. I had possibly committed unwittingly a breach of good-breeding, according to French ideas, which almost justified the brusque severity of the Marquis’s undignified rebuke.

In a confusion, therefore, of many feelings, I hastened to make my apologies, and to propitiate the chance friend who had showed me so much disinterested kindness.

I told him that I would, at any cost, break through the engagement in which I had unluckily entangled myself; that I had spoken with too little reflection, and that I certainly had not thanked him at all in proportion to his kindness and to my real estimate of it.

“Pray say not a word more; my vexation was entirely on your account; and I expressed it, I am only too conscious, in terms a great deal too strong, which, I am sure, your good nature will pardon. Those who know me a little better are aware that I sometimes say a good deal more than I intend; and am always sorry when I do. Monsieur Beckett will forget that his old friend, Monsieur Droqville, has lost his temper in his cause, for a moment, and﻿—we are as good friends as before.”

===Chinese===
这话放肆得骇人！

我又惊又恼，又觉愧疚。说不定我不曾留意，按法国人的规矩失了礼数，侯爵这番疾言厉色固然有失体面，倒也几乎情有可原。

于是我百感交集、心慌意乱，赶忙赔不是，巴结讨好这位萍水相逢却对我如此无私关照的朋友。

我对他说，无论如何我也要挣脱那桩不幸应下的纠葛；说我方才出言欠考虑；说他的一番好意，以及我心里对它的真正珍重，实在远不是我刚才那点谢忱所能相称的。

「请再别提一个字了；我这恼怒，完完全全是为你着想；我心里再清楚不过，方才的话确实说得太重，我相信以你的好性儿必定会原谅的。稍微同我相熟一点的人都知道，我有时说起话来总要大大超出本意，过后也总是懊悔。贝克特先生一定不会计较他的老朋友德罗克维尔先生方才为了他的事动了一时火气——咱们还跟从前一样是好朋友。」

===Original===
He smiled like the Monsieur Droqville of the Belle Étoile, and extended his hand, which I took very respectfully and cordially.

Our momentary quarrel had left us only better friends.

The Marquis then told me I had better secure a bed in some hotel at Versailles, as a rush would be made to take them; and advised my going down next morning for the purpose.

I ordered horses accordingly for eleven o’clock; and, after a little more conversation, the Marquis d’Harmonville bid me good night, and ran down the stairs with his handkerchief to his mouth and nose, and, as I saw from my window, jumped into his close carriage again and drove away.

===Chinese===
他微微一笑，又是美星旅馆（Belle Étoile）那位德罗克维尔先生的笑模样，向我伸出手来；我恭恭敬敬、诚诚恳恳地握住了。

这一场口角，反倒叫我们交情更笃。

接着侯爵对我说，我最好赶紧在凡尔赛哪家旅馆订下一个铺位，因为到时必定人人抢着要；又劝我次日一早便赶去把这事办妥。

我于是吩咐十一点备马；又略谈了几句，德·哈蒙维尔侯爵便向我道了晚安，一手用手帕捂着口鼻奔下楼去；我从窗口望见他又跳上那辆密闭的马车，驱车而去。

===Original===
Next day I was at Versailles. As I approached the door of the Hotel de France, it was plain that I was not a moment too soon, if, indeed, I were not already too late.

A crowd of carriages were drawn up about the entrance, so that I had no chance of approaching except by dismounting and pushing my way among the horses. The hall was full of servants and gentlemen screaming to the proprietor, who, in a state of polite distraction, was assuring them, one and all, that there was not a room or a closet disengaged in his entire house.

I slipped out again, leaving the hall to those who were shouting, expostulating, wheedling, in the delusion that the host might, if he pleased, manage something for them. I jumped into my carriage and drove, at my horses’ best pace, to the Hotel du Reservoir. The blockade about this door was as complete as the other. The result was the same. It was very provoking, but what was to be done? My postillion had, a little officiously, while I was in the hall talking with the hotel authorities, got his horses, bit by bit, as other carriages moved away, to the very steps of the inn door.

===Chinese===
第二天我到了凡尔赛。走近法兰西旅馆（Hôtel de France）大门时，一望便知我来得已经不算早，甚至恐怕已经迟了。

大群马车堵在入口四周，我根本无法靠近，只得下车，从马匹中间挤过去。门厅里挤满了仆役和绅士，正冲着店主人大呼小叫；店主一副彬彬有礼的手忙脚乱，向他们一一保证：他这整座客栈里，连一间客房、一间小橱屋都腾不出来了。

我又悄悄退了出来，把门厅留给那些还在喊叫、争辩、软磨硬泡的人——他们糊涂地以为，店主只要愿意，总还能替他们想出办法。我跳上马车，催着马以最快的速度赶到雷泽尔瓦旅馆（Hôtel du Réservoir）。这家门口的围堵同方才那家一样水泄不通，结果也一模一样。真是气人，可又有什么办法？方才我在门厅里同旅馆管事们交涉的时候，我那骑马的跟车马夫倒有点自作主张，趁着旁的马车挪动，一点一点把车挪到了客栈门口的台阶跟前。

===Original===
This arrangement was very convenient so far as getting in again was concerned. But, this accomplished, how were we to get on? There were carriages in front, and carriages behind, and no less than four rows of carriages, of all sorts, outside.

I had at this time remarkably long and clear sight, and if I had been impatient before, guess what my feelings were when I saw an open carriage pass along the narrow strip of roadway left open at the other side, a barouche in which I was certain I recognized the veiled Countess and her husband. This carriage had been brought to a walk by a cart which occupied the whole breadth of the narrow way, and was moving with the customary tardiness of such vehicles.

===Chinese===
就再上车而言，这安排倒是方便之至。可是上了车，又怎么走得动呢？前头是马车，后头也是马车，外头还足足排着四行各式各样的马车。

我那时候目力极佳，望得又远又清。先前我已经等得心焦，此刻又望见一辆敞篷马车沿着另一侧仅存的一条窄道驶来——那是一辆四轮马车（barouche），我认定车里坐的正是那位戴黑纱的伯爵夫人和她的丈夫——你可想而知我心里是什么滋味。那辆车被一辆大车逼得只能缓步而行，那大车占尽了窄道的全部宽度，正以这类车辆素来那副慢吞吞的劲儿挪动着。

===Original===
I should have done more wisely if I had jumped down on the *trottoir*, and run round the block of carriages in front of the barouche. But, unfortunately, I was more of a Murat than a Moltke, and preferred a direct charge upon my object to relying on *tactique*. I dashed across the back seat of a carriage which was next mine, I don’t know how; tumbled through a sort of gig, in which an old gentleman and a dog were dozing; stepped with an incoherent apology over the side of an open carriage, in which were four gentlemen engaged in a hot dispute; tripped at the far side in getting out, and fell flat across the backs of a pair of horses, who instantly began plunging and threw me head foremost in the dust.

To those who observed my reckless charge without being in the secret of my object I must have appeared demented. Fortunately, the interesting barouche had passed before the catastrophe, and covered as I was with dust, and my hat blocked, you may be sure I did not care to present myself before the object of my quixotic devotion.

===Chinese===
倘若我当时跳下人行道（trottoir），绕到那辆四轮马车前头去，本会更明智些。可惜，我天生更像缪拉（Murat）而不像毛奇（Moltke），宁肯朝着目标正面冲锋，也不肯倚仗「战术」（tactique）。我不知怎么居然蹿上了紧挨我车的那辆马车的后座；又一头滚过一辆双轮轻马车——车里一位老先生和一条狗正在打盹；再语无伦次地道歉着跨过一辆敞篷马车的车帮——车里四位先生正吵得不可开交；从那头下车时又绊了一下，正正扑倒在一对马的背上，那两匹马立刻暴跳起来，把我头朝前甩进了尘土里。

在那些眼看我这场鲁莽冲锋、却不知我意之所指的人眼里，我一定像个失了心疯的。所幸这场灾祸发生之前，那辆教人心痒的四轮马车已经驶了过去；我浑身尘土，帽子也砸瘪了，你可以料到，我自然不愿这副模样去到那位害我唐吉诃德式痴心妄想的人儿面前现身。

===Original===
I stood for a while amid a storm of *sacré*-ing, tempered disagreeably with laughter; and in the midst of these, while endeavouring to beat the dust from my clothes with my handkerchief, I heard a voice with which I was acquainted call, “Monsieur Beckett.”

I looked and saw the Marquis peeping from a carriage-window. It was a welcome sight. In a moment I was at his carriage side.

“You may as well leave Versailles,” he said; “you have learned, no doubt, that there is not a bed to hire in either of the hotels; and I can add that there is not a room to let in the whole town. But I have managed something for you that will answer just as well. Tell your servant to follow us, and get in here and sit beside me.”

===Chinese===
我站在一片「该死的」（sacré）咒骂声的风暴里，其间还不痛快地掺着阵阵哄笑；正这么着，我一边用手帕掸衣裳上的土，一边听得一个熟悉的嗓音喊道：「贝克特先生。」

我抬头一看，侯爵正从一扇车窗里向外张望。这真是求之不得的景象。转眼间我已到了他的马车旁。

「你不如离开凡尔赛吧，」他说，「你无疑已经打听到，那两家旅馆连一个铺位都租不到了；我还可以再告诉你，全城也再没有一间空房。不过我替你另想了个法子，一样顶用。叫你的仆人跟上来，上这儿来，坐在我旁边。」

===Original===
Fortunately an opening in the closely-packed carriages had just occurred, and mine was approaching.

I directed the servant to follow us; and the Marquis having said a word to his driver, we were immediately in motion.

“I will bring you to a comfortable place, the very existence of which is known to but few Parisians, where, knowing how things were here, I secured a room for you. It is only a mile away, and an old comfortable inn, called Le Dragon Volant. It was fortunate for you that my tiresome business called me to this place so early.”

===Chinese===
幸而密密匝匝的车阵里恰好空出一道口子，我的车正挨近过来。

我吩咐仆人跟上来；侯爵对车夫嘱咐了一句，我们立刻动身了。

「我带你去一个舒舒服服的地方，连巴黎人也少有人知道世上有这么一处。我料到这里会客满，早替你订好了一间房。离这儿不过一英里，是一家又老又舒适的客栈，名叫飞龙旅馆（Le Dragon Volant）。亏得我那桩烦人的公事，这么早就把我召到这儿来，真是你的运气。」

===Original===
I think we had driven about a mile-and-a-half to the further side of the palace when we found ourselves upon a narrow old road, with the woods of Versailles on one side, and much older trees, of a size seldom seen in France, on the other.

We pulled up before an antique and solid inn, built of Caen stone, in a fashion richer and more florid than was ever usual in such houses, and which indicated that it was originally designed for the private mansion of some person of wealth, and probably, as the wall bore many carved shields and supporters, of distinction also. A kind of porch, less ancient than the rest, projected hospitably with a wide and florid arch, over which, cut in high relief in stone, and painted and gilded, was the sign of the inn. This was the Flying Dragon, with wings of brilliant red and gold, expanded, and its tail, pale green and gold, twisted and knotted into ever so many rings, and ending in a burnished point barbed like the dart of death.

===Chinese===
我想我们顺着宫殿另一侧驶了约莫一英里半，便上了一条狭窄的老路：一侧是凡尔赛的林木，另一侧是更加古老的树，那般粗大，在法国也属罕见。

我们在一家古朴而坚实的客栈前停了下来。客栈以卡昂石（Caen stone）筑成，式样比这类房舍惯常的气派更华丽、更繁复，可见它当初本是照着哪位有钱人的私邸营造的；墙上又有那么多雕花的盾徽和护兽，恐怕主人的门第还不止于有钱而已。一座门廊，年代比其余部分稍晚，顶着一道宽阔华美的拱，好客地探出街来；拱上是一方高浮雕的石雕，敷彩贴金，正是客栈的招牌：一条飞龙，红金二色的双翼灿然张开，淡绿间金的龙尾扭结成不知多少个环，末梢收作一支光亮亮的尖，带着倒刺，活像死亡掷出的标枪。

===Original===
“I shan’t go in﻿—but you will find it a comfortable place; at all events better than nothing. I would go in with you, but my incognito forbids. You will, I daresay, be all the better pleased to learn that the inn is haunted﻿—I should have been, in my young days, I know. But don’t allude to that awful fact in hearing of your host, for I believe it is a sore subject. Adieu. If you want to enjoy yourself at the ball take my advice, and go in a domino. I think I shall look in; and certainly, if I do, in the same costume. How shall we recognize one another? Let me see, something held in the fingers﻿—a flower won’t do, so many people will have flowers. Suppose you get a red cross a couple of inches long﻿—you’re an Englishman﻿—stitched or pinned on the breast of your domino, and I a white one? Yes, that will do very well; and whatever room you go into keep near the door till we meet. I shall look for you at all the doors I pass; and you, in the same way, for me; and we *must* find each other soon. So that is understood. I can’t enjoy a thing of that kind with any but a young person; a man of my age requires the contagion of young spirits and the companionship of someone who enjoys everything spontaneously. Farewell; we meet tonight.”

===Chinese===
「我不进去了——你会发觉这地方挺舒适；无论如何，总比没有强。我本想陪你进去，怎奈我隐姓埋名，多有不便。这客栈闹鬼——你想必听了会更高兴——我知道，搁在我年轻的时候，准会喜出望外。不过在店主听得见的地方，千万别提这桩可怕的事，我相信那是他心头的疮疤。再会。你若想在舞会上尽兴，就听我一句劝，穿一件化装斗篷（domino）去。我也许会去转转；真去的话，自然也作同样打扮。咱们怎么相认呢？让我想想——手指间捏点什么——花不成，拿花的人太多。这么着：你缀一枚两英寸长的红十字——你是英国人——缝上也行，别上也好，缀在斗篷胸前；我缀一枚白的，如何？好，这就成了；还有，你无论进哪个厅，先守在门边，等我们碰了头再说。我每过一道门都会留意找你；你也照样找我；咱们*准能*很快碰上。就这么说定了。跟年轻人以外的人在一处，我享受不来这种玩意儿；像我这个年纪的人，需要的是青春兴致的感染，需要一个看什么都自然而然津津有味的人做伴。再会；今晚见。」

===Original===
By this time I was standing *on* the road; I shut the carriage-door; bid him goodbye; and away he drove.

===Chinese===
这时我已经站*在路上*了；我关上车门，同他道了别，他便驱车而去。

## XI The Dragon Volant / XI 飞龙旅馆

===Original===
I took one look about me.

The building was picturesque; the trees made it more so. The antique and sequestered character of the scene, contrasted strangely with the glare and bustle of the Parisian life, to which my eye and ear had become accustomed.

Then I examined the gorgeous old sign for a minute or two. Next I surveyed the exterior of the house more carefully. It was large and solid, and squared more with my ideas of an ancient English hostelrie, such as the Canterbury pilgrims might have put up at, than a French house of entertainment. Except, indeed, for a round turret, that rose at the left flank of the house, and terminated in the extinguisher-shaped roof that suggests a French château.

===Chinese===
我先朝四周望了一眼。

房舍景致如画，树木更添几分。这里古朴幽僻的气象，同我耳目早已惯了的巴黎生活的喧嚣与光彩，恰成奇异的对照。

接着，我把那块华丽的老招牌端详了一两分钟，又把屋子的外观看得更仔细些。它高大坚实，倒更合我心中古老英国客店的模样——坎特伯雷朝圣者投宿的那一类——而不大像法国的游乐之所。只是屋子左翼凸起一座圆塔，塔顶收成灭火器似的尖锥，这才叫人想起法国的城堡来。

===Original===
I entered and announced myself as Monsieur Beckett, for whom a room had been taken. I was received with all the consideration due to an English milord, with, of course, an unfathomable purse.

My host conducted me to my apartment. It was a large room, a little sombre, panelled with dark wainscoting, and furnished in a stately and sombre style, long out of date. There was a wide hearth, and a heavy mantelpiece, carved with shields, in which I might, had I been curious enough, have discovered a correspondence with the heraldry on the outer walls. There was something interesting, melancholy, and even depressing in all this. I went to the stone-shafted window, and looked out upon a small park, with a thick wood, forming the background of a château, which presented a cluster of such conical-topped turrets as I have just now mentioned.

The wood and château were melancholy objects. They showed signs of neglect, and almost of decay; and the gloom of fallen grandeur, and a certain air of desertion hung oppressively over the scene.

===Chinese===
我走进店内，报上姓名，说是贝克特先生，房间已替我订下了。他们待我礼数周全，俨然一位英国爵爷（milord）——自然，还假定我带着一只深不可测的钱袋。

店主领我到我的房间。那是一间大屋，微微有些幽暗，镶着深色护壁板，家具气派而沉郁，式样早已过时。壁炉宽大，炉台厚重，上面雕着盾徽——我若好奇心再重些，本可以发现它们与外墙上纹章的对应。这一切里透着一种有趣、忧郁、甚至叫人压抑的东西。我走到石砌窗棂的窗前，向外望去，是一座小园林，尽头一片密林，衬出一座城堡的背影，城堡上攒着一簇簇圆锥顶的塔楼，正是我方才提到的那种。

林木和城堡都是愁惨的景致。它们露出荒于打理、近乎颓败的光景；繁华零落的阴郁，加上一种遭人遗弃的神气，沉沉地压在这一片景色上。

===Original===
I asked my host the name of the château.

“That, Monsieur, is the Château de la Carque,” he answered.

“It is a pity it is so neglected,” I observed. “I should say, perhaps, a pity that its proprietor is not more wealthy?”

“Perhaps so, Monsieur.”

“*Perhaps?*”—I repeated, and looked at him. “Then I suppose he is not very popular.”

“Neither one thing nor the other, Monsieur,” he answered; “I meant only that we could not tell what use he might make of riches.”

===Chinese===
我问店主那座城堡的名字。

「回先生，那是拉卡尔克城堡（Château de la Carque）。」他答道。

「可惜荒成这样，」我说，「也许该说，可惜主人不够有钱？」

「也许是的，先生。」

「*也许*？」——我重复了一遍，望着他。「那么我猜他不大得人心吧。」

「也不是这个，也不是那个，先生，」他答道，「我的意思只是，他有了钱会派什么用场，我们说不好。」

===Original===
“And who is he?” I inquired.

“The Count de St. Alyre.”

“Oh! The Count! You are quite sure?” I asked, very eagerly.

It was now the innkeeper’s turn to look at me.

“*Quite* sure, Monsieur, the Count de St. Alyre.”

===Chinese===
「他是哪一位？」我问。

「圣阿利尔伯爵。」

「哦！伯爵！你拿得准么？」我问得非常急切。

这回轮到店主打量我了。

「拿得*很*准，先生，圣阿利尔伯爵。」

===Original===
“Do you see much of him in this part of the world?”

“Not a great deal, Monsieur; he is often absent for a considerable time.”

“And is he poor?” I inquired.

“I pay rent to him for this house. It is not much; but I find he cannot wait long for it,” he replied, smiling satirically.

“From what I have heard, however, I should think he cannot be very poor?” I continued.

“They say, Monsieur, he plays. I know not. He certainly is not rich. About seven months ago, a relation of his died in a distant place. His body was sent to the Count’s house here, and by him buried in Père la Chaise, as the poor gentleman had desired. The Count was in profound affliction; although he got a handsome legacy, they say, by that death. But money never seems to do him good for any time.”

===Chinese===
「这一带你们常见得着他么？」

「不常见，先生；他往往一走就是好些日子。」

「那么他穷么？」我问。

「我这店房的租金就是付给他的。数目不大；可我发现他等不得几天。」他答道，嘴角挂着讥诮的笑。

「不过照我听说的，他总不至于很穷吧？」我接着问。

「人家说他赌钱。我不晓得。他确实不富。约莫七个月前，他的一位亲戚死在外地，灵柩运到伯爵这里的宅子，由他安葬在拉雪兹神父公墓（Père la Chaise），也算全了那位可怜的先生临终的心愿。伯爵悲痛欲绝——虽说这一死，据说他还得了一注可观的遗产。可是钱到他手里，从来好不了多久。」

===Original===
“He is old, I believe?”

“Old? we call him the ‘Wandering Jew,’ except, indeed, that he has not always the five sous in his pocket. Yet, Monsieur, his courage does not fail him. He has taken a young and handsome wife.”

“And, she?” I urged—

“Is the Countess de St. Alyre.”

“Yes; but I fancy we may say something more? She has attributes?”

“Three, Monsieur, three, at least most amiable.”

“Ah! And what are they?”

“Youth, beauty, and﻿—diamonds.”

===Chinese===
「他上了年纪了吧，我想？」

「年纪？我们都管他叫『流浪的犹太人』——只是比起那位来，他兜里未必总揣着五个苏。可是先生，他的胆气倒不输人：娶了一位年轻貌美的夫人。」

「那么她呢？」我追问——

「就是圣阿利尔伯爵夫人。」

「是；不过我想总还能多说几句吧？她总有几点可说的品性？」

「三点，先生，三点，而且都极可爱。」

「啊！哪三点？」

「年轻，美貌，还有——钻石。」

===Original===
I laughed. The sly old gentleman was foiling my curiosity.

“I see, my friend,” said I, “you are reluctant﻿—”

“To quarrel with the Count,” he concluded. “True. You see, Monsieur, he could vex me in two or three ways; so could I him. But, on the whole, it is better each to mind his business, and to maintain peaceful relations; you understand.”

It was, therefore, no use trying, at least for the present. Perhaps he had nothing to relate. Should I think differently, by-and-by, I could try the effect of a few Napoleons. Possibly he meant to extract them.

The host of the Dragon Volant was an elderly man, thin, bronzed, intelligent, and with an air of decision, perfectly military. I learned afterwards that he had served under Napoleon in his early Italian campaigns.

===Chinese===
我笑了。这狡黠的老先生存心不让我问出个所以然来。

「我明白了，我的朋友，」我说，「你不愿——」

「同伯爵闹翻。」他替我把话说完。「正是。您瞧，先生，他能从两三处整治我；我也能回敬他。不过总的说来，还是各人守各人的营生、和和气气为好；您懂的。」

那么，至少眼下再问也是无用。或许他本来也没有什么可讲的。往后我要是改了主意，不妨试试几枚拿破仑金币（Napoleon）的效力。说不定他正是等着榨出这几枚金币呢。

飞龙旅馆的店主是位上了年纪的人，瘦削，面孔黝黑，目光精明，带着一种斩钉截铁的气概，十足军人做派。后来我才得知，他早年曾在拿破仑麾下征战意大利。

===Original===
“One question, I think you may answer,” I said, “without risking a quarrel. Is the Count at home?”

“He has many homes, I conjecture,” said the host evasively. “But﻿—but I think I may say, Monsieur, that he is, I believe, at present staying at the Château de la Carque.”

I looked out of the window, more interested than ever, across the undulating grounds to the château, with its gloomy background of foliage.

“I saw him today, in his carriage at Versailles,” I said.

“Very natural.”

“Then his carriage and horses and servants are at the château?”

“The carriage he puts up here, Monsieur, and the servants are hired for the occasion. There is but one who sleeps at the château. Such a life must be terrifying for Madame the Countess,” he replied.

===Chinese===
「有一个问题，我想你总可以回答，」我说，「不致惹起争端。伯爵在家么？」

「他的家多着呢，我猜，」店主含糊其辞。「不过——不过我想可以说，先生，他这会儿，据我相信，正住在拉卡尔克城堡。」

我望向窗外，隔着起伏的园地望向那座城堡，和它身后阴沉的树影，兴致比先前更浓了。

「我今天在凡尔赛还见过他，在他的马车里。」我说。

「那很自然。」

「那么他的马车、马匹和仆役都在城堡里了？」

「马车寄存在我这里，先生，仆役是临时雇下的。睡在城堡里的只有一个人。这样的日子，对伯爵夫人一定可怕极了。」他答道。

===Original===
“The old screw!” I thought. “By this torture, he hopes to extract her diamonds. What a life! What fiends to contend with﻿—jealousy and extortion!”

The knight having made this speech to himself, cast his eyes once more upon the enchanter’s castle, and heaved a gentle sigh﻿—a sigh of longing, of resolution, and of love.

What a fool I was! and yet, in the sight of angels, are we any wiser as we grow older? It seems to me, only, that our illusions change as we go on; but, still, we are madmen all the same.

===Chinese===
「老吝啬鬼！」我心想。「他是要用这种酷刑榨她的钻石。过的什么日子！要同什么样的恶魔周旋——嫉妒和勒索！」

这位骑士自言自语完毕，又把目光投向那座魔法师的城堡，轻轻叹了口气——一声渴望的、决意的、又是爱情的叹息。

我那时多傻啊！然而在天使眼里，我们年纪大了就真的更聪明么？依我看，不过是我们的妄想随路改换了模样；可我们照旧是疯子，一点没变。

===Original===
“Well, St. Clair,” said I, as my servant entered, and began to arrange my things. “You have got a bed?”

“In the cockloft, Monsieur, among the spiders, and, *par ma foi!* the cats and the owls. But we agree very well. *Vive la bagatelle!*”

“I had no idea it was so full.”

“Chiefly the servants, Monsieur, of those persons who were fortunate enough to get apartments at Versailles.”

“And what do you think of the Dragon Volant?”

===Chinese===
「喂，圣克莱尔（St. Clair），」我的仆人进来收拾东西，我说道，「你弄到床铺了？」

「在阁楼里，先生，跟蜘蛛们做伴，还——*说真的*（par ma foi!）——跟猫和猫头鹰做伴。不过我们处得很好。*管它呢，乐呵就行*（Vive la bagatelle!）！」

「没想到店里竟住得这么满。」

「多半是仆人，先生，都是那些运气好、在凡尔赛找到了住处的人家的仆人。」

「那你觉得飞龙旅馆怎么样？」

===Original===
“The Dragon Volant! Monsieur; the old fiery dragon! The devil himself, if all is true! On the faith of a Christian, Monsieur, they say that diabolical miracles have taken place in this house.”

“What do you mean? *Revenants?*”

“Not at all, sir; I wish it was no worse. *Revenants?* No! People who have *never* returned﻿—who vanished, before the eyes of half-a-dozen men, all looking at them.”

“What do you mean, St. Clair? Let us hear the story, or miracle, or whatever it is.”

“It is only this, Monsieur, that an ex-master-of-the-horse of the late king, who lost his head﻿—Monsieur will have the goodness to recollect, in the revolution﻿—being permitted by the Emperor to return to France, lived here in this hotel, for a month, and at the end of that time vanished, visibly, as I told you, before the faces of half-a-dozen credible witnesses! The other was a Russian nobleman, six feet high and upwards, who, standing in the centre of the room, downstairs, describing to seven gentlemen of unquestionable veracity, the last moments of Peter the Great, and having a glass of eau-de-vie in his left hand, and his *tasse de café*, nearly finished, in his right, in like manner vanished. His boots were found on the floor where he had been standing; and the gentleman at his right, found, to his astonishment, his cup of coffee in his fingers, and the gentleman at his left, his glass of eau-de-vie﻿—”

===Chinese===
「飞龙旅馆！先生；那条老火龙！要是传闻全真，简直就是魔鬼本人！凭基督徒的信义说，先生，人家讲，这座房子里出过邪魔的奇迹。」

「什么意思？是*还魂鬼*（revenants）么？」

「绝不是，先生；但愿只是还魂鬼就好了。还魂鬼？不！是些*从此再没有回来*的人——当着半打人的眼睛，众目睽睽，凭空不见了。」

「你到底什么意思，圣克莱尔？把这事讲来听听，管它是传闻还是奇迹。」

「事情是这样，先生：先王的一位御马总管——那位先王丢了脑袋，先生谅必记得，是在革命里丢的——蒙皇帝恩准回到法国，就在这家客栈里住了一个月，一个月期满，就这般当着半打可靠证人的面，眼睁睁地失踪了！另一位是俄国贵族，六英尺还多的高个子，站在楼下大堂正中，正向七位绝无虚言的绅士描述彼得大帝临终的情形，左手端着一杯烧酒（eau-de-vie），右手一杯将尽的咖啡（tasse de café），也就这么不见了。他站过的地方，地板上留着他那双靴子；他右手边的那位绅士，吃惊地发现自己指间捏着那杯咖啡，左手边的，捏着那杯烧酒——」

===Original===
“Which he swallowed in his confusion,” I suggested.

“Which was preserved for three years among the curious articles of this house, and was broken by the curé while conversing with Mademoiselle Fidone in the housekeeper’s room; but of the Russian nobleman himself, nothing more was ever seen or heard! *Parbleu!* when *we* go out of the Dragon Volant, I hope it may be by the door. I heard all this, Monsieur, from the postillion who drove us.”

“Then it *must* be true!” said I, jocularly: but I was beginning to feel the gloom of the view, and of the chamber in which I stood; there had stolen over me, I know not how, a presentiment of evil; and my joke was with an effort, and my spirit flagged.

===Chinese===
「慌乱之中一口吞了？」我插嘴道。

「那杯酒在这客栈的稀奇物件里供奉了三年，后来本堂神父在管家婆房里同菲多娜小姐（Mademoiselle Fidone）闲谈，失手打了；至于那位俄国贵族本人，从此再没有人见过、听过！*见鬼*（Parbleu!）！等*我们*走出飞龙旅馆的时候，我指望还是打大门里走。这些话，先生，都是赶我们来的那位骑马的跟车马夫讲给我听的。」

「那*一定*是真的了！」我打趣道。可是不觉之间，那片景色的阴郁、我立身的这间屋子的阴郁，已经悄悄爬上我的心头；一种不祥的预感不知怎的缠住了我；我的玩笑是硬挤出来的，我的兴致也蔫了。

## XII The Magician / XII 魔术师

===Original===
No more brilliant spectacle than this masked ball could be imagined. Among other salons and galleries, thrown open, was the enormous perspective of the “Grande Galerie des Glaces,” lighted up on that occasion with no less than four thousand wax candles, reflected and repeated by all the mirrors, so that the effect was almost dazzling. The grand suite of salons was thronged with masques, in every conceivable costume. There was not a single room deserted. Every place was animated with music, voices, brilliant colours, flashing jewels, the hilarity of extemporized comedy, and all the spirited incidents of a cleverly sustained masquerade. I had never seen before anything, in the least, comparable to this magnificent fête. I moved along, indolently, in my domino and mask, loitering, now and then, to enjoy a clever dialogue, a farcical song, or an amusing monologue, but, at the same time, keeping my eyes about me, lest my friend in the black domino, with the little white cross on his breast, should pass me by.

===Chinese===
再没有比这场化装舞会更辉煌的场面，叫人想都想不出来。在一间接一间敞开的沙龙和画廊中间，有一道望不到头的恢弘透视——「镜厅」（Grande Galerie des Glaces），那一晚点着不下四千支蜡烛，烛光又被四面的镜子层层映照，几乎耀人眼目。整座连绵成套的大厅里挤满了戴假面的人，装束千奇百怪，无所不有。没有一间屋子是冷清的。处处是音乐、人语、绚烂的色彩、闪烁的珠宝、即兴喜剧的哄笑，还有一场经营得巧妙的化装舞会上种种意趣盎然的插曲。这样的盛会，我生平见所未见。我裹着化装斗篷（domino）、戴着假面，懒洋洋地随人流走动，不时驻足，赏一段机智的对答、一首滑稽的歌，或一段逗趣的独白，眼睛却同时不闲着，唯恐我那位胸前缀小白十字的黑斗篷朋友与我错身而过。

===Original===
I had delayed and looked about me, specially, at every door I passed, as the Marquis and I had agreed; but he had not yet appeared.

While I was thus employed, in the very luxury of lazy amusement, I saw a gilded sedan chair, or, rather, a Chinese palanquin, exhibiting the fantastic exuberance of “Celestial” decoration, borne forward on gilded poles by four richly-dressed Chinese; one with a wand in his hand marched in front, and another behind; and a slight and solemn man, with a long black beard, a tall fez, such as a dervish is represented as wearing, walked close to its side. A strangely-embroidered robe fell over his shoulders, covered with hieroglyphic symbols; the embroidery was in black and gold, upon a variegated ground of brilliant colours. The robe was bound about his waist with a broad belt of gold, with cabalistic devices traced on it, in dark red and black; red stockings, and shoes embroidered with gold, and pointed and curved upward at the toes, in Oriental fashion, appeared below the skirt of the robe. The man’s face was dark, fixed, and solemn, and his eyebrows black, and enormously heavy﻿—he carried a singular-looking book under his arm, a wand of polished black wood in his other hand, and walked with his chin sunk on his breast, and his eyes fixed upon the floor. The man in front waved his wand right and left to clear the way for the advancing palanquin, the curtains of which were closed; and there was something so singular, strange, and solemn about the whole thing, that I felt at once interested.

I was very well pleased when I saw the bearers set down their burden within a few yards of the spot on which I stood.

===Chinese===
每逢经过一道门，我都照侯爵同我约定的那样，特意流连片刻，四下张望；可他始终没有露面。

我正这样无所事事、优哉游哉地消遣着，忽见一顶镀金的肩舆——不如说是一顶中国轿辇，通体是「天朝」式穷奢极侈的装潢，由四个衣饰华丽的中国人抬着镀金轿杠前行；一人手持长杖走在前头，另有一人殿后；还有一个瘦削而肃穆的人，蓄着长长的黑须，头上一顶高高的毡帽——就是图画里苦行僧戴的那一种——紧傍着轿子步行。一件绣工怪异的长袍披在他肩上，满是符箓似的记号；绣花黑金二色，衬着斑斓绚烂的底色。长袍腰间束一条宽阔的金带，带上描着暗红与黑色的秘符；红袜子，金绣、尖头、鞋尖上翘的鞋，东方样式，露出在袍裾之下。这人面色黝黑、呆板而肃穆，两道眉毛又黑又浓得出奇——腋下夹着一本模样古怪的书，另一只手拄着一根乌木磨光的杖，下巴抵着胸口，两眼盯着地面走着。前头那人左右挥动长杖，为行进的轿辇开路；轿帘垂得严严实实。整个场面透着那样一种奇异、古怪而肃穆的气派，我顿时起了兴致。

见抬轿的人把轿子落在离我立足处不过几码远的地方，我心里着实高兴。

===Original===
The bearers and the men with the gilded wands forthwith clapped their hands, and in silence danced round the palanquin a curious and half frantic dance, which was yet, as to figures and postures, perfectly methodical. This was soon accompanied by a clapping of hands and a *ha-ha*-ing, rhythmically delivered.

While the dance was going on a hand was lightly laid on my arm, and, looking round, a black domino with a white cross stood beside me.

“I am so glad I have found you,” said the Marquis; “and at this moment. This is the best group in the rooms. *You* must speak to the wizard. About an hour ago I lighted upon them, in another salon, and consulted the oracle, by putting questions. I never was more amazed. Although his answers were a little disguised it was soon perfectly plain that he knew every detail about the business, which no one on earth had heard of but myself, and two or three other men, about the most cautious persons in France. I shall never forget that shock. I saw other people who consulted him, evidently as much surprised, and more frightened than I. I came with the Count St. Alyre and the Countess.”

===Chinese===
轿夫和执金杖的人们随即拍起手来，默不作声地绕着轿辇跳起一种古怪的、半带颠狂的舞，然而队形与身法却又井然有法。不一会儿，四周又应和起一阵拍手声和有板有眼的「哈哈」声。

舞正跳着，一只手轻轻搭上我的手臂；回头一看，一件缀白十字的黑斗篷立在我身旁。

「真高兴找着了你，」侯爵说，「还赶得这么巧。这是全场最妙的一班人马。*你*务必去问问那位术士。约莫一个钟头前，我在另一间沙龙撞见他们，问了几句，求了一回神谕（the oracle）。我从来没有那样吃惊过。他的回答虽略加掩饰，可很快就明明白白：那桩事的每一个细节他全知道——普天之下，除了我和另外两三个人，都是全法国顶顶精细谨慎的人——再没有旁人听说过。那一下震惊，我一辈子忘不了。我还看见别人去问，显然同我一样吃惊，却比我更害怕。我是同圣阿利尔伯爵和伯爵夫人一道来的。」

===Original===
He nodded toward a thin figure, also in a domino. It was the Count.

“Come,” he said to me, “I’ll introduce you.”

I followed, you may suppose, readily enough.

The Marquis presented me, with a very prettily-turned allusion to my fortunate intervention in his favour at the Belle Étoile; and the Count overwhelmed me with polite speeches, and ended by saying, what pleased me better still:

“The Countess is near us, in the next salon but one, chatting with her old friend the Duchesse d’Argensaque; I shall go for her in a few minutes; and when I bring her here, she shall make your acquaintance; and thank you, also, for your assistance, rendered with so much courage when we were so very disagreeably interrupted.”

===Chinese===
他朝一个瘦削的身影点点头；那人也裹着斗篷。正是伯爵。

「来，」他对我说，「我给你引见。」

你可想见，我跟着走过去有多爽快。

侯爵替我引见时，还十分得体地提了一句我在美星旅馆（Belle Étoile）侥幸为他解围的事；伯爵一迭声的客气话把我淹没，末了更说了一句叫我更受用的话：

「伯爵夫人就在近旁，隔一间沙龙，正同她的老朋友阿尔让萨克公爵夫人（Duchesse d’Argensaque）叙话；我过几分钟就去接她来；等我把她带来，就让她认识认识你，也谢谢你那一臂之力——我们那次被人搅得多败兴，亏得你挺身而出，那样有胆量。」

===Original===
“You must, positively, speak with the magician,” said the Marquis to the Count de St. Alyre, “you will be so much amused. *I* did so; and, I assure you, I could not have anticipated such answers! I don’t know what to believe.”

“Really! Then, by all means, let us try,” he replied.

We three approached, together, the side of the palanquin, at which the black-bearded magician stood.

A young man, in a Spanish dress, who, with a friend at his side, had just conferred with the conjuror, was saying, as he passed us by:

“Ingenious mystification! Who is that in the palanquin? He seems to know everybody!”

===Chinese===
「你务必，一定，去同那位魔术师谈谈，」侯爵对圣阿利尔伯爵说，「保管有趣得很。*我*方才问过了；我向你担保，那样的回答，我做梦也料不着！都不知道该信什么了。」

「当真！那么，务必让我们也试试。」他答道。

我们三人便一同走向轿辇的一侧，那黑须魔术师站立的地方。

一个西班牙装束的年轻人，身边跟着一位朋友，刚同那术士问过话，擦过我们身边时说道：

「巧妙的骗局！轿子里坐的是谁？他好像谁都认得！」

===Original===
The Count, in his mask and domino, moved along, stiffly, with us, toward the palanquin. A clear circle was maintained by the Chinese attendants, and the spectators crowded round in a ring.

One of these men﻿—he who with a gilded wand had preceded the procession﻿—advanced, extending his empty hand, palm upward.

“Money?” inquired the Count.

“Gold,” replied the usher.

The Count placed a piece of money in his hand; and I and the Marquis were each called on in turn to do likewise as we entered the circle. We paid accordingly.

The conjuror stood beside the palanquin, its silk curtain in his hand; his chin sunk, with its long, jet-black beard, on his chest; the outer hand grasping the black wand, on which he leaned; his eyes were lowered, as before, to the ground; his face looked absolutely lifeless. Indeed, I never saw face or figure so moveless, except in death.

===Chinese===
伯爵戴着假面、裹着斗篷，同我们一起，步子僵僵地朝轿辇走去。中国侍役们维持出一圈空场，看客们围成一圈，挤作一环。

这些人中的一个——就是执金杖走在队伍前头的那个——迎上前来，伸出手掌，掌心朝天。

「要钱？」伯爵问。

「要金子。」引座人答道。

伯爵往他掌中放了一枚钱；我和侯爵进圈时，也依次被示意照办。我们便都付了。

术士立在轿辇旁，手里捏着丝帘；蓄着漆黑长须的下巴抵在胸前；外侧那只手攥着那根乌木杖，倚着它；两眼仍如先前一样垂视地面；那张脸看上去竟毫无生气。真的，除了死人，我从未见过这样纹丝不动的脸和身形。

===Original===
The first question the Count put, was﻿—

“Am I married, or unmarried?”

The conjuror drew back the curtain quickly, and placed his ear toward a richly-dressed Chinese, who sat in the litter; withdrew his head, and closed the curtain again; and then answered﻿—

“Yes.”

The same preliminary was observed each time, so that the man with the black wand presented himself, not as a prophet, but as a medium; and answered, as it seemed, in the words of a greater than himself.

===Chinese===
伯爵问的头一个问题是——

「我是结了婚，还是没结婚？」

术士迅疾掀开轿帘，把耳朵凑向一位衣饰华贵的中国人——那人坐在轿中——随即缩回头，重新掩上轿帘，然后答道——

「结了。」

这一套仪节每问一回都要行一遍，于是那执乌杖的人便不以预言家、而以通灵者的身份出现；他的回答，看去像是转述一位比他更高的存在的话。

===Original===
Two or three questions followed, the answers to which seemed to amuse the Marquis very much; but the point of which I could not see, for I knew next to nothing of the Count’s peculiarities and adventures.

“Does my wife love me?” asked he, playfully.

“As well as you deserve.”

“Whom do I love best in the world?”

“Self.”

“Oh! That I fancy is pretty much the case with everyone. But, putting myself out of the question, do I love anything on earth better than my wife?”

“Her diamonds.”

“Oh!” said the Count.

The Marquis, I could see, laughed.

===Chinese===
接着又问了两三个问题，那些回答似乎让侯爵乐不可支；其中的妙处我却看不出，因为对伯爵的脾性和经历，我几乎一无所知。

「我的妻子爱我么？」他戏谑地问。

「按你该得的那样爱。」

「普天之下，我最爱的是谁？」

「自己。」

「哦！这个嘛，我看人人大抵如此。可是撇开我自己不谈，这世上可有什么，我爱得胜过我的妻子？」

「她的钻石。」

「哦！」伯爵说。

我瞧得出，侯爵笑了。

===Original===
“Is it true,” said the Count, changing the conversation peremptorily, “that there has been a battle in Naples?”

“No; in France.”

“Indeed,” said the Count, satirically, with a glance round. “And may I inquire between what powers, and on what particular quarrel?”

“Between the Count and Countess de St. Alyre, and about a document they subscribed on the 25th July, 1811.”

The Marquis afterwards told me that this was the date of their marriage settlement.

The Count stood stock-still for a minute or so; and one could fancy that they saw his face flushing through his mask.

Nobody, but we two, knew that the inquirer was the Count de St. Alyre.

===Chinese===
「是不是真的，」伯爵不由分说地岔开话头，「那不勒斯打了一仗？」

「不；在法国。」

「当真，」伯爵讥诮地说，眼睛四下一扫。「那么可否请教，是哪两方之间的事，又为着哪一桩特别的仇隙？」

「在圣阿利尔伯爵与伯爵夫人之间，为着他们 1811 年 7 月 25 日签立的一纸文书。」

侯爵后来告诉我，那正是他们订立婚约的日子。

伯爵直挺挺地立了一分钟上下；人们简直可以想见，透过假面，看得见他的脸在发红。

除了我们两个，谁也不知道发问的人就是圣阿利尔伯爵。

===Original===
I thought he was puzzled to find a subject for his next question; and, perhaps, repented having entangled himself in such a colloquy. If so, he was relieved; for the Marquis, touching his arm, whispered﻿—

“Look to your right, and see who is coming.”

I looked in the direction indicated by the Marquis, and I saw a gaunt figure stalking toward us. It was not a masque. The face was broad, scarred, and white. In a word, it was the ugly face of Colonel Gaillarde, who, in the costume of a corporal of the Imperial Guard, with his left arm so adjusted as to look like a stump, leaving the lower part of the coat-sleeve empty, and pinned up to the breast. There were strips of very real sticking-plaster across his eyebrow and temple, where my stick had left its mark, to score, hereafter, among the more honourable scars of war.

===Chinese===
我想他一时竟找不出下一句该问什么；说不定还后悔陷进了这样一场对答。若真如此，倒有人替他解了围；因为侯爵碰了碰他的手臂，低声道——

「朝你右边看，瞧瞧谁来了。」

我朝侯爵指的方向望去，看见一个瘦长的身影正大步朝我们走来。那不是戴假面的。一张阔脸，布满疤痕，煞白。一句话，正是盖亚尔上校那张丑脸。他扮的是帝国近卫军一个下士的行头，左臂装束成断桩的模样——军衣袖子的下半截空荡荡的，别到了胸口。眉骨和太阳穴上贴着几条货真价实的膏药，那正是我的手杖留下的印记，往后要在他那许多光荣的战疤里记上一笔。

## XIII The Oracle Tells Me Wonders / XIII 神谕对我说出奇迹

===Original===
I forgot for a moment how impervious my mask and domino were to the hard stare of the old campaigner, and was preparing for an animated scuffle. It was only for a moment, of course; but the Count cautiously drew a little back as the gasconading corporal, in blue uniform, white vest, and white gaiters﻿—for my friend Gaillarde was as loud and swaggering in his assumed character as in his real one of a colonel of dragoons﻿—drew near. He had already twice all but got himself turned out of doors for vaunting the exploits of Napoleon le Grand, in terrific mock-heroics, and had very nearly come to handgrips with a Prussian hussar. In fact, he would have been involved in several sanguinary rows already, had not his discretion reminded him that the object of his coming there at all, namely, to arrange a meeting with an affluent widow, on whom he believed he had made a tender impression, would not have been promoted by his premature removal from the festive scene, of which he was an ornament, in charge of a couple of gendarmes.

===Chinese===
有那么一瞬，我忘了自己的假面和斗篷足以挡住这老行伍锥子似的逼视，正准备着大干一场。当然，只是一瞬；可当那个吹牛皮的下士——蓝军装、白背心、白绑腿——走近时，伯爵还是小心地退开了些。我这位盖亚尔朋友，扮起角色来同他本来的龙骑兵上校身份一样大嗓门、一样神气活现。他已经两次差一点因为用吓死人的假英雄腔吹捧拿破仑大帝（Napoleon le Grand）的功业而被轰出门去，还差一点同一个普鲁士骠骑兵真刀真枪地干起来。说实在的，若不是他的精明提醒他，此行的目的——同一个阔寡妇安排会面，他自信已在那寡妇心头留下了温柔缠绵的印象——若是被两名宪兵半路押出这场有他增光的盛会，可就全然无益了，他早已卷进好几场血战了。

===Original===
“Money! Gold! Bah! What money can a wounded soldier like your humble servant have amassed, with but his sword-hand left, which, being necessarily occupied, places not a finger at his command with which to scrape together the spoils of a routed enemy?”

“No gold from him,” said the magician. “His scars frank him.”

“*Bravo, Monsieur le prophète! Bravissimo!* Here I am. Shall I begin, *mon sorcier*, without further loss of time, to question your﻿—”

Without waiting for an answer, he commenced, in stentorian tones.

===Chinese===
「钱！金子！呸！像鄙人这样一个伤兵，只剩一条使剑的手，能攒下什么钱？这条手又一时闲不下来，连一根指头都腾不出，教他拿什么去捡溃敌丢下的财物？」

「他的金子不收，」魔术师说，「他的伤疤就是通行证。」

「*好极了，预言家先生（Monsieur le prophète）！妙极了（Bravissimo）！*我来了。还等什么，*我的术士（mon sorcier）*，这就开始问你的——」

他不等人回答，便扯开洪钟般的嗓门问了起来。

===Original===
After half-a-dozen questions and answers, he asked﻿—

“Whom do I pursue at present?”

“Two persons.”

“Ha! Two? Well, who are they?”

“An Englishman, whom, if you catch, he will kill you; and a French widow, whom, if you find, she will spit in your face.”

“*Monsieur le magicien* calls a spade a spade, and knows that his cloth protects him. No matter! Why do I pursue them?”

“The widow has inflicted a wound on your heart, and the Englishman a wound on your head. They are each separately too strong for you; take care your pursuit does not unite them.”

“Bah! How could that be?”

“The Englishman protects ladies. He has got that fact into your head. The widow, if she sees, will marry him. It takes some time, she will reflect, to become a colonel, and the Englishman is unquestionably young.”

===Chinese===
问过六个来回之后，他问——

「我眼下在追捕谁？」

「两个人。」

「哈！两个？好，是谁？」

「一个英国人，你若抓住他，他会要你的命；一个法国寡妇，你若找到她，她会啐你的脸。」

「*魔术师先生*（Monsieur le magicien）说话直来直去，也知道身上这行头护得住他。没关系！我为什么追他们？」

「寡妇在你心上留了一处伤，英国人在你头上留了一处伤。他们哪一个，你都单打独斗地惹不起；小心别让你的追逐把他俩凑成了一对。」

「呸！那怎么可能？」

「那英国人保护女人。这一条已经进了你的脑袋。那寡妇若看在眼里，就会嫁给他。她心里会盘算：熬成上校总要些年月，而这英国人又年轻得无可争辩。」

===Original===
“I will cut his cock’s-comb for him,” he ejaculated with an oath and a grin; and in a softer tone he asked, “Where is she?”

“Near enough to be offended if you fail.”

“So she ought, by my faith. You are right, *Monsieur le prophète*! A hundred thousand thanks! Farewell!” And staring about him, and stretching his lank neck as high as he could, he strode away with his scars, and white waistcoat and gaiters, and his bearskin shako.

===Chinese===
「看我不铰掉他的鸡冠！」他咒骂一声，咧嘴一笑，又放软了声调问：「她在哪儿？」

「近得很——你要是办砸了，她一气就在近旁。」

「她也该生气，说实话。你说得对，*预言家先生*！千恩万谢！再会！」说着他四下张望，把那细长的脖子伸得老高，带着他的伤疤、白背心白绑腿和熊皮高筒军帽，大步流星地去了。

===Original===
I had been trying to see the person who sat in the palanquin. I had only once an opportunity of a tolerably steady peep. What I saw was singular. The oracle was dressed, as I have said, very richly, in the Chinese fashion. He was a figure altogether on a larger scale than the interpreter, who stood outside. The features seemed to me large and heavy, and the head was carried with a downward inclination! the eyes were closed, and the chin rested on the breast of his embroidered pelisse. The face seemed fixed, and the very image of apathy. Its character and pose seemed an exaggerated repetition of the immobility of the figure who communicated with the noisy outer world. This face looked bloodred; but that was caused, I concluded, by the light entering through the red silk curtains. All this struck me almost at a glance; I had not many seconds in which to make my observation. The ground was now clear, and the Marquis said, “Go forward, my friend.”

I did so. When I reached the magician, as we called the man with the black wand, I glanced over my shoulder to see whether the Count was near.

No, he was some yards behind; and he and the Marquis, whose curiosity seemed to be, by this time, satisfied, were now conversing generally upon some subject of course quite different.

I was relieved, for the sage seemed to blurt out secrets in an unexpected way; and some of mine might not have amused the Count.

===Chinese===
我一直想看清轿辇里坐着的人。只有一次，得着一个还算稳当的窥视机会。所见很奇特。那位神谕一身中国式装束，如我所说，华贵之至。他的身量比立在轿外的通译整整大了一圈。那五官在我看来阔大厚重，脑袋微微俯垂着！双眼闭合，下巴抵在绣花皮袍的胸前。那张脸僵着，全然一副麻木的形容，神情与姿态，仿佛把外面那位与喧嚷尘世交通者的纹丝不动又放大了一层。这张脸红得像血；可我断定，那是红绸轿帘透进的光染出来的。这一切几乎一眼扫过；容我观察的不过几秒钟。这会儿场子空了出来，侯爵说：「上前吧，我的朋友。」

我便上前。走到那位我们唤作魔术师的黑杖人跟前时，我回头瞥了一眼，看伯爵在不在近旁。

不在，他在几码开外；他和侯爵——后者的好奇心到这会儿看来已经满足——正泛泛地谈着别的什么话头。

我松了口气，因为这高人开口就会出人意料地漏出隐私；我的某些隐私，未必能让伯爵听了开心。

===Original===
I thought for a moment. I wished to test the prophet. A Church-of-England man was a rara avis in Paris.

“What is my religion?” I asked.

“A beautiful heresy,” answered the oracle instantly.

“A heresy?﻿—and pray how is it named?”

“Love.”

“Oh! Then I suppose I am a polytheist, and love a great many?”

“One.”

“But, seriously,” I asked, intending to turn the course of our colloquy a little out of an embarrassing channel, “have I ever learned any words of devotion by heart?”

“Yes.”

“Can you repeat them?”

“Approach.”

I did, and lowered my ear.

===Chinese===
我想了想，要考一考这位预言家。英国国教徒在巴黎可是凤毛麟角（rara avis）。

「我信的是什么教？」我问。

「一个美丽的异端。」神谕应声而答。

「异端？——那么请问它叫什么名字？」

「爱。」

「哦！那我大概是个多神教徒，爱着许多位了？」

「只有一位。」

「不过，说正经的，」我问，想把我们这场对答从尴尬的河道里稍稍拨开，「我可曾背熟过什么祷词？」

「背熟过。」

「你能复述一遍么？」

「凑近来。」

我便凑近，侧过了耳朵。

===Original===
The man with the black wand closed the curtains, and whispered, slowly and distinctly, these words, which, I need scarcely tell you, I instantly recognized:

> “*I may never see you more; and, oh! that I could forget you! go﻿—farewell﻿—for God’s sake, go!*”

I started as I heard them. They were, you know, the last words whispered to me by the Countess.

Good Heaven! How miraculous! Words heard, most assuredly, by no ear on earth but my own and the lady’s who uttered them, till now!

I looked at the impassive face of the spokesman with the wand. There was no trace of meaning, or even of a consciousness that the words he had uttered could possibly interest me.

===Chinese===
那执黑杖的人掩上轿帘，缓慢而清晰地低声念出下面这几句话——不必我说，我立刻就听出来了：

> *「我也许再也见不到你了；啊！但愿我能忘了你！走吧——别了——看在上帝分上，走吧！」*

我一听，浑身一震。你知道，这正是伯爵夫人对我耳语的最后几个字。

老天爷！何等的神迹！这些话，直到此刻，天底下确凿无疑除了我自己的耳朵和说这话的夫人的耳朵，再没有第三只耳朵听过！

我看着执杖代言人那张无动于衷的脸。没有一丝会意的痕迹，甚至没有一点意识——意识到他刚说出口的话可能与我相干。

===Original===
“What do I most long for?” I asked, scarcely knowing what I said.

“Paradise.”

“And what prevents my reaching it?”

“A black veil.”

Stronger and stronger! The answers seemed to me to indicate the minutest acquaintance with every detail of my little romance, of which not even the Marquis knew anything! And I, the questioner, masked and robed so that my own brother could not have known me!

“You said I loved someone. Am I loved in return?” I asked.

“Try.”

I was speaking lower than before, and stood near the dark man with the beard, to prevent the necessity of his speaking in a loud key.

===Chinese===
「我最渴望的是什么？」我问，几乎不知道自己在说什么。

「乐园。」

「是什么拦着我到不了那里？」

「一重黑纱。」

一浪高过一浪！这些回答在我看来表明，对我这桩小小罗曼司的每一个细节，他都了如指掌——连侯爵都对此一无所知！而我这个发问的人，假面斗篷裹得亲兄弟也认不出来！

「你说过我爱着一个人。人家也爱不爱我？」我问。

「试试看。」

我的声音越说越低，凑近那黑须的阴沉汉子站着，省得他非扬声作答不可。

===Original===
“Does anyone love me?” I repeated.

“Secretly,” was the answer.

“Much or little?” I inquired.

“Too well.”

“How long will that love last?”

“Till the rose casts its leaves.”

“The rose﻿—another allusion!”

“Then﻿—darkness!” I sighed. “But till then I live in light.”

“The light of violet eyes.”

===Chinese===
「有没有人爱我？」我又问。

「暗中爱着。」回答是。

「爱得深还是浅？」

「深得过了头。」

「这爱能持续多久？」

「直到玫瑰落尽花瓣。」

「玫瑰——又是一句暗语！」

「那么——便是黑暗了！」我叹道。「可在那之前，我活在光里。」

「紫罗兰色眼睛的光。」

===Original===
Love, if not a religion, as the oracle had just pronounced it, is, at least, a superstition. How it exalts the imagination! How it enervates the reason! How credulous it makes us!

All this which, in the case of another, I should have laughed at, most powerfully affected me in my own. It inflamed my ardour, and half crazed my brain, and even influenced my conduct.

The spokesman of this wonderful trick﻿—if trick it were﻿—now waved me backward with his wand, and as I withdrew, my eyes still fixed upon the group, by this time encircled with an aura of mystery in my fancy; backing toward the ring of spectators, I saw him raise his hand suddenly, with a gesture of command, as a signal to the usher who carried the golden wand in front.

===Chinese===
爱情，纵然算不上神谕方才宣布的那种宗教，至少也是一种迷信。它多么抬举想象力！多么瓦解理性！多么叫人轻信！

这一切若落在别人身上，我早就笑了；落在自己身上，却把我撼动得厉害。它烧旺了我的热望，把我的脑袋搅得半疯，甚至左右了我的行事。

这场绝妙戏法——如果当真是戏法——的代言人，这时用杖一挥，示意我退后；我一面退，一面仍盯着这一群人，此刻他们在我心目中已罩上一圈神秘的光晕；正退向看客围成的人环时，我见他突然抬手，做了个号令的姿势，那是给前头执金杖的引座人的信号。

===Original===
The usher struck his wand on the ground, and, in a shrill voice, proclaimed; “The great Confu is silent for an hour.”

Instantly the bearers pulled down a sort of blind of bamboo, which descended with a sharp clatter, and secured it at the bottom; and then the man in the tall fez, with the black beard and wand, began a sort of dervish dance. In this the men with the gold wands joined, and finally, in an outer ring, the bearers, the palanquin being the centre of the circles described by these solemn dancers, whose pace, little by little, quickened, whose gestures grew sudden, strange, frantic, as the motion became swifter and swifter, until at length the whirl became so rapid that the dancers seemed to fly by with the speed of a mill-wheel, and amid a general clapping of hands, and universal wonder, these strange performers mingled with the crowd, and the exhibition, for the time at least, ended.

===Chinese===
引座人把杖往地上一顿，尖声唱道：「大圣孔夫（Confu）静默一个钟头。」

轿夫们立刻放下一面竹帘，哗啦一声脆响落到底，在下沿拴牢；于是那戴高毡帽、黑须执杖的人跳起一种苦行僧的舞。执金杖的人们加入进来，最后，抬轿的也在外圈起舞，轿辇便成了这一圈圈肃穆舞者画出的圆心。舞步渐渐加快，动作变得突兀、古怪、颠狂，旋转越急越疾，最后那旋涡快到舞者仿佛磨盘般飞掠而过；在一片齐声拍手和满场惊叹之中，这班古怪的艺人没入人群，这场展演，至少在眼下，收了场。

===Original===
The Marquis d’Harmonville was standing not far away, looking on the ground, as one could judge by his attitude and musing. I approached, and he said:

“The Count has just gone away to look for his wife. It is a pity she was not here to consult the prophet; it would have been amusing, I daresay, to see how the Count bore it. Suppose we follow him. I have asked him to introduce you.”

With a beating heart, I accompanied the Marquis d’Harmonville.

===Chinese===
德·哈蒙维尔侯爵正立在不远处，低头望着地上，看那姿态像是出了神。我走上前去，他说：

「伯爵刚走开，找他的夫人去了。可惜她方才不在这儿问问那位预言家；看看伯爵怎么受着，想必会有趣。咱们跟上吧。我已经请他把你引见给伯爵夫人。」

我心头突突地跳，跟着德·哈蒙维尔侯爵去了。

## XIV Mademoiselle de la Vallière / XIV 德·拉瓦利埃小姐

===Original===
We wandered through the salons, the Marquis and I. It was no easy matter to find a friend in rooms so crowded.

“Stay here,” said the Marquis, “I have thought of a way of finding him. Besides, his jealousy may have warned him that there is no particular advantage to be gained by presenting you to his wife, I had better go and reason with him; as you seem to wish an introduction so very much.”

This occurred in the room that is now called the “Salon d’Apollon.” The paintings remained in my memory, and my adventure of that evening was destined to occur there.

===Chinese===
我和侯爵在各个厅堂里穿行。在这样人满为患的屋子里找一位朋友，可不是件容易事。

「在这儿等着，」侯爵说，「我想出一个找他的法子了。再说，他的嫉妒心或许也已提醒了他：把你引见给他夫人，没什么特别的好处。我还是去找他说说理；既然你这么想让人引见。」

这一幕发生在如今唤作「阿波罗厅」（Salon d'Apollon）的屋子里。那几幅画一直留在我的记忆里，而我那晚的奇遇，也注定要在那里发生。

===Original===
