I sat down upon a sofa; and looked about me. Three or four persons beside myself were seated on this roomy piece of gilded furniture. They were chatting all very gaily; all﻿—except the person who sat next me, and she was a lady. Hardly two feet interposed between us. The lady sat apparently in a reverie. Nothing could be more graceful. She wore the costume perpetuated in Collignan’s full-length portrait of Mademoiselle de la Vallière. It is, as you know, not only rich, but elegant. Her hair was powdered, but one could perceive that it was naturally a dark brown. One pretty little foot appeared, and could anything be more exquisite than her hand?

It was extremely provoking that this lady wore her mask, and did not, as many did, hold it for a time in her hand.

I was convinced that she was pretty. Availing myself of the privilege of a masquerade, a microcosm in which it is impossible, except by voice and allusion, to distinguish friend from foe, I spoke﻿—

===Chinese===
我在一张沙发上坐下，四下打量。这张宽敞的镀金家具上，除我之外还坐着三四个人。他们谈得正欢——唯独挨着我坐的那位例外，而且是位女士。我们中间隔着还不足两英尺。女士显然正出了神，那姿态说不出的优雅。她穿的，正是科利尼昂（Collignan）那幅德·拉瓦利埃小姐（Mademoiselle de la Vallière）全身画像传下来的那身装束。如你所知，那不仅华贵，而且雅致。她的头发扑了粉，可还看得出本是深褐色。一只秀气的小脚露了出来，而她的那双手——还有比那更精致的么？

恼人的是，这位女士戴着假面，不像许多人那样，且把它拿在手里晾上一会儿。

我认定她是美的。化装舞会自有它的特权——那是个小天地，除非凭嗓音和言语关窍，谁也辨不出敌友——我便开了口——

===Original===
“It is not easy, Mademoiselle, to deceive me,” I began.

“So much the better for Monsieur,” answered the mask, quietly.

“I mean,” I said, determined to tell my fib, “that beauty is a gift more difficult to conceal than Mademoiselle supposes.”

“Yet Monsieur has succeeded very well,” she said in the same sweet and careless tones.

“I see the costume of this, the beautiful Mademoiselle de la Vallière, upon a form that surpasses her own; I raise my eyes, and I behold a mask, and yet I recognise the lady; beauty is like that precious stone in the *Arabian Nights*, which emits, no matter how concealed, a light that betrays it.”

“I know the story,” said the young lady. “The light betrayed it, not in the sun, but in darkness. Is there so little light in these rooms, Monsieur, that a poor glowworm can show so brightly. I thought we were in a luminous atmosphere, wherever a certain countess moved?”

===Chinese===
「小姐要想骗过我，可不容易。」我开了腔。

「那对先生只有更好。」假面平静地答道。

「我的意思是，」我说，打定主意要撒这个谎，「美是天赋的一件礼物，要比小姐以为的更难遮掩。」

「可先生遮掩得就很成功。」她还是那样甜美而满不在乎的声调。

「我看见美丽的德·拉瓦利埃小姐的这身穿戴，套在一副胜过她本人的身段上；我抬起眼睛，看见的是一副假面，然而我还是认出了这位女士；美就像《一千零一夜》（Arabian Nights）里那块宝石，无论怎样掩藏，总要放出光来，泄露自己。」

「这故事我知道，」年轻女士说。「那光出卖它，不是在太阳底下，而是在黑暗里。这几间屋子里的光就这么少么，先生，以致一只可怜的萤火虫也显得这样亮？我还以为，只要某位伯爵夫人往哪里一动，哪里便是一片光明呢？」

===Original===
Here was an awkward speech! How was I to answer? This lady might be, as they say some ladies are, a lover of mischief, or an intimate of the Countess de St. Alyre. Cautiously, therefore, I inquired,

“What countess?”

“If you know me, you must know that she is my dearest friend. Is she not beautiful?”

“How can I answer, there are so many countesses.”

“Everyone who knows me, knows who my best beloved friend is. You don’t know me?”

“That is cruel. I can scarcely believe I am mistaken.”

===Chinese===
这话可不好接！我怎么回答呢？这位女士说不定正如人家说的某些女士那样，是个促狭鬼，要么就是圣阿利尔伯爵夫人的贴心密友。于是我小心地探问：

「哪位伯爵夫人？」

「你既然认得我，就一定知道她是我最亲爱的朋友。她美不美？」

「这教我怎么答？伯爵夫人多得是。」

「凡认得我的，都知道我最心爱的朋友是谁。你认不得我？」

「这可太狠心了。我几乎不能相信自己认错了。」

===Original===
“With whom were you walking, just now?” she asked.

“A gentleman, a friend,” I answered.

“I saw him, of course, a friend; but I think I know him, and should like to be certain. Is he not a certain marquis?”

Here was another question that was extremely awkward.

“There are so many people here, and one may walk, at one time, with one, and at another with a different one, that﻿—”

“That an unscrupulous person has no difficulty in evading a simple question like mine. Know then, once for all, that nothing disgusts a person of spirit so much as suspicion. You, Monsieur, are a gentleman of discretion. I shall respect you accordingly.”

===Chinese===
「你方才同谁一起走路？」她问。

「一位绅士，一位朋友。」我答。

「我当然看见了，一位朋友；可我好像认得他，想拿准一点。他不是某位侯爵么？」

又是一个叫人为难已极的问题。

「这儿人这么多，人有时同这个走，有时又同那个走，以致——」

「以致一个没有心肝的人，要回避我这么个小问题，毫不费事。那你听着，只说这一遍：再没有比猜疑更叫有血性的人恶心的了。你，先生，是位有分寸的绅士。我自然也要照此敬重你。」

===Original===
“Mademoiselle would despise me, were I to violate a confidence.”

“But you don’t deceive me. You imitate your friend’s diplomacy. I hate diplomacy. It means fraud and cowardice. Don’t you think I know him. The gentleman with the cross of white ribbon on his breast. I know the Marquis d’Harmonville perfectly. You see to what good purpose your ingenuity has been expended.”

“To that conjecture I can answer neither yes nor no.”

“You need not. But what was your motive in mortifying a lady?”

“It is the last thing on earth I should do.”

“You affected to know me, and you don’t; through caprice or listlessness or curiosity you wished to converse, not with a lady, but with a costume. You admired, and you pretend to mistake me for another. But who is quite perfect? Is truth any longer to be found on earth?”

===Chinese===
「小姐若是叫我泄露别人的信任，才会看轻我呢。」

「可你骗不了我。你不过学你那位朋友的圆滑。我恨圆滑。圆滑就是欺诈加怯懦。你以为我不认得他。胸前佩白绶带十字的那位绅士。德·哈蒙维尔侯爵，我认得一清二楚。你瞧，你的机灵就是这么个用处。」

「这个猜想，我既不能答是，也不能答否。」

「不必答。可你存心羞辱一位女士，是何居心？」

「那是我天底下最不肯做的一件事。」

「你装作认得我，其实并不；你出于任性，或无聊，或好奇，想攀谈的并非一位女士，而是一身衣裳。你心里赞叹着，嘴上却装作把我错认成别人。可谁又十全十美呢？这世上还有实话么？」

===Original===
“Mademoiselle has formed a mistaken opinion of me.”

“And you also of me; you find me less foolish than you supposed. I know perfectly whom you intend amusing with compliments and melancholy declamation, and whom, with that amiable purpose, you have been seeking.”

“Tell me whom you mean,” I entreated.

“Upon one condition.”

“What is that?”

“That you will confess if I name the lady.”

===Chinese===
「小姐对我成见差了。」

「你对我也是一样；你发现我不如你想的那么傻。你想拿恭维和哀艳的调子去逗哪一位开心，又为着这番好意一直在寻哪一位，我全清楚。」

「告诉我你说的是谁。」我恳求道。

「有一个条件。」

「什么条件？」

「我说出那位女士的名字，你就得认账。」

===Original===
“You describe my object unfairly.” I objected. “I can’t admit that I proposed speaking to any lady in the tone you describe.”

“Well, I shan’t insist on that; only if I name the lady, you will promise to admit that I am right.”

“*Must* I promise?”

“Certainly not, there is no compulsion; but your promise is the only condition on which I will speak to you again.”

I hesitated for a moment; but how could she possibly tell? The Countess would scarcely have admitted this little romance to anyone; and the mask in the La Vallière costume could not possibly know who the masked domino beside her was.

“I consent,” I said, “I promise.”

“You must promise on the honour of a gentleman.”

“Well, I do; on the honour of a gentleman.”

===Chinese===
「你把我的用心说得不公道，」我抗议道。「我不能承认，我打算用你说的那种口吻同哪位女士说话。」

「好吧，这一点我不坚持；只要我说出那位女士，你须答应承认我说对了。」

「*一定*要答应么？」

「当然不必，没人强迫你；不过你答应了，我才会再同你说话。」

我迟疑了片刻；可她怎么可能说得出呢？伯爵夫人决不至于把这桩小小的罗曼司讲给任何人；而这位拉瓦利埃装束的假面，也决计无从知道身旁这个戴假面的斗篷究竟是谁。

「我依你，」我说，「我答应。」

「你得凭绅士的名誉起誓。」

「好，我凭绅士的名誉起誓。」

===Original===
“Then this lady is the Countess de St. Alyre.” I was unspeakably surprised; I was disconcerted; but I remembered my promise, and said﻿—

“The Countess de St. Alyre *is*, unquestionably, the lady to whom I hoped for an introduction tonight; but I beg to assure you also on the honour of a gentleman, that she has not the faintest imaginable suspicion that I was seeking such an honour, nor, in all probability, does she remember that such a person as I exists. I had the honour to render her and the Count a trifling service, too trifling, I fear, to have earned more than an hour’s recollection.”

“The world is not so ungrateful as you suppose; or if it be, there are, nevertheless, a few hearts that redeem it. I can answer for the Countess de St. Alyre, she never forgets a kindness. She does not show all she feels; for she is unhappy, and cannot.”

“Unhappy! I feared, indeed, that might be. But for all the rest that you are good enough to suppose, it is but a flattering dream.”

“I told you that I am the Countess’s friend, and being so I must know something of her character; also, there are confidences between us, and I may know more than you think, of those trifling services of which you suppose the recollection is so transitory.”

===Chinese===
「那么这位女士，就是圣阿利尔伯爵夫人。」我惊得无以言表；我心里发慌；可我想起了自己的承诺，便说——

「圣阿利尔伯爵夫人*确实*毫无疑问，正是我今晚盼着有人引见的那位女士；不过我也请小姐相信，我凭绅士的名誉担保，她对我竟怀着这样的盼头，连最细微的疑心也不曾有过；而且十之八九，她根本不记得世上还有我这样一个人。我曾有幸为她 和伯爵效过一点微劳，只怕太微不足道，配不上比一个钟头更长久的记挂。」

「世人不像你想的那样忘恩负义；即便真是，也总有几颗心替世人赎罪。圣阿利尔伯爵夫人，我敢担保，从不忘记人家的好。她的心事不肯全露出来；因为她不快活，也露不出来。」

「不快活！我倒真怕是这样。至于小姐好意设想的其余种种，那不过是一场讨人喜欢的梦。」

「我说过我是伯爵夫人的朋友，既然是朋友，她的性子我总该知道几分；再说，我们之间素有私语，你说记得那样短促的那些微劳，我知道的恐怕比你以为的多。」

===Original===
I was becoming more and more interested. I was as wicked as other young men, and the heinousness of such a pursuit was as nothing, now that self-love and all the passions that mingle in such a romance, were roused. The image of the beautiful Countess had now again quite superseded the pretty counterpart of La Vallière, who was before me. I would have given a great deal to hear, in solemn earnest, that she did remember the champion who, for her sake, had thrown himself before the sabre of an enraged dragoon, with only a cudgel in his hand, and conquered.

===Chinese===
我的兴致越来越浓。我同别的年轻人一样坏；如今自尊心，连同搅在这种罗曼司里的一切情欲，都已被撩起，这桩追逐在天理上的罪过，便算不得什么了。美丽伯爵夫人的影像，这会儿又完全盖过了眼前这位俊俏的拉瓦利埃翻版。若能听见她郑重其事地亲口说，她当真记得那位骑士——为她只身挡在暴怒的龙骑兵马刀之前，手里只一根木棍，还得胜了——我情愿付出很大的代价。

===Original===
“You say the Countess is unhappy,” said I. “What causes her unhappiness?”

“Many things. Her husband is old, jealous, and tyrannical. Is not that enough? Even when relieved from his society, she is lonely.”

“But you are her friend?” I suggested.

“And you think one friend enough?” she answered; “she has one alone, to whom she can open her heart.”

“Is there room for another friend?”

“Try.”

“How can I find a way?”

“She will aid you.”

“How?”

She answered by a question. “Have you secured rooms in either of the hotels of Versailles?”

===Chinese===
「你说伯爵夫人不快活，」我说，「是什么叫她不快活？」

「许多事。她丈夫又老、又妒、又专横。这不就够了么？即便躲开了他的陪伴，她也还是孤独的。」

「可你不是她的朋友么？」我提醒道。

「你以为一个朋友就够了？」她答道，「能让她敞开心扉的，只有一个。」

「还容得下另一个朋友么？」

「试试看。」

「路子怎么找呢？」

「她会帮你。」

「怎么帮？」

她用一个问题作答：「凡尔赛那两家旅馆，你订到房间了么？」

===Original===
“No, I could not. I am lodged in the Dragon Volant, which stands at the verge of the grounds of the Château de la Carque.”

“That is better still. I need not ask if you have courage for an adventure. I need not ask if you are a man of honour. A lady may trust herself to you, and fear nothing. There are few men to whom the interview, such as I shall arrange, could be granted with safety. You shall meet her at two o’clock this morning in the Park of the Château de la Carque. What room do you occupy in the Dragon Volant?”

I was amazed at the audacity and decision of this girl. Was she, as we say in England, hoaxing me?

“I can describe that accurately,” said I. “As I look from the rear of the house, in which my apartment is, I am at the extreme right, next the angle; and one pair of stairs up, from the hall.”

“Very well; you must have observed, if you looked into the park, two or three clumps of chestnut and lime-trees, growing so close together as to form a small grove. You must return to your hotel, change your dress, and, preserving a scrupulous secrecy, as to why or where you go, leave the Dragon Volant, and climb the park-wall, unseen; you will easily recognize the grove I have mentioned; there you will meet the Countess, who will grant you an audience of a few minutes, who will expect the most scrupulous reserve on your part, and who will explain to you, in a few words, a great deal which *I* could not so well tell you here.”

I cannot describe the feeling with which I heard these words. I was astounded. Doubt succeeded. I could not believe these agitating words.

===Chinese===
「没有，订不到。我住在飞龙旅馆，就在拉卡尔克城堡园林的边上。」

「那更好。你的胆量够不够闯一场奇遇，我不必问。你是不是个重荣誉的人，我也不必问。一位女士把自己托付给你，尽可什么都不怕。这样一场会面，像我安排的这样，能放心应允的人可不多。今儿凌晨两点，你在拉卡尔克城堡园林同她见面。你在飞龙旅馆住的是哪间房？」

这姑娘的大胆与决断叫我吃惊。她该不会是像我们英国人说的，在耍我吧？

「这个我说得分毫不差，」我说。「从我房间所在的屋子后边望，我在最靠右的位置，紧挨着墙角；从门厅上去，一层楼梯。」

「很好；你若朝园林里张望过，一定见过两三丛栗树和菩提树，长得极密，合成一小片树丛。你必须回旅馆去，换掉这身衣裳，无论为什么去、往哪里去，都守口如瓶，人不知鬼不觉地离开飞龙旅馆，翻过园林的墙；我说的那片树丛，你一认便知；在那里你会见到伯爵夫人，她准你几分钟的会面，指望你这边慎之又慎，还会用寥寥几句话，向你说明许多*我*在这里不便说、也说不好的事。」

我听着这些话时的心情，难以描摹。我怔住了。接着起了疑。这样叫人心摇神荡的话，我不能相信。

===Original===
“Mademoiselle will believe that if I only dared assure myself that so great a happiness and honour were really intended for me, my gratitude would be as lasting as my life. But how dare I believe that Mademoiselle does not speak, rather from her own sympathy or goodness, than from a certainty that the Countess de St. Alyre would concede so great an honour?”

“Monsieur believes either that I am not, as I pretend to be, in the secret which he hitherto supposed to be shared by no one but the Countess and himself, or else that I am cruelly mystifying him. That I am in her confidence, I swear by all that is dear in a whispered farewell. By the last companion of this flower!” and she took for a moment in her fingers the nodding head of a white rosebud that was nestled in her bouquet. “By my own good star, and hers﻿—or shall I call it our ‘*belle étoile*?’ Have I said enough?”

“Enough?” I repeated, “more than enough﻿—a thousand thanks.”

“And being thus in her confidence, I am clearly her friend; and being a friend would it be friendly to use her dear name so; and all for sake of practising a vulgar trick upon you﻿—a stranger?”

“Mademoiselle will forgive me. Remember how very precious is the hope of seeing, and speaking to the Countess. Is it wonderful, then, that I should falter in my belief? You have convinced me, however, and will forgive my hesitation.”

===Chinese===
「小姐应当相信，我只要敢确信这般幸福与荣幸真是为我备下的，我的感激必与性命同长。可我怎敢相信，小姐这番话不是出于自己的热心与好意，而是确知圣阿利尔伯爵夫人肯赐下这般的荣幸呢？」

「先生要么不信我真如我所显示的那样，与他共享着他本以为除伯爵夫人和他本人外再无人知的那个秘密；要么就是疑心我在狠心地作弄他。我确在她心里占着一份，这一点，我凭一声耳语道别中一切珍贵的东西起誓。凭这朵花的最后一位伴侣！」她一时用手指托住花束里一朵垂着头的白玫瑰蕾。「凭我的福星，也凭她的——或者，要不要叫它作我们的『美丽星』（belle étoile）？我说得够了么？」

「够了？」我重复道，「何止是够——千恩万谢。」

「我既在她心里占着一份，自然就是她的朋友；既是朋友，又怎么会拿她亲爱的名字这样用，全为着对一个陌生人——耍一套下流的把戏？」

「还望小姐见谅。想想吧，能见到伯爵夫人、同她说话，这指望于我是何等珍贵。我会迟疑不敢相信，又有什么可怪呢？不过你已说服了我，还望你原谅我的迟疑。」

===Original===
“You will be at the place I have described, then, at two o’clock?”

“Assuredly,” I answered.

“And Monsieur, I know, will not fail, through fear. No, he need not assure me; his courage is already proved.”

“No danger, in such a case, will be unwelcome to me.”

“Had you not better go now, Monsieur, and rejoin your friend?”

“I promised to wait here for my friend’s return. The Count de St. Alyre said that he intended to introduce me to the Countess.”

===Chinese===
「那么，两点钟，你一定到我说的地方？」

「一定。」我答。

「先生我也知道，不会因为害怕失约。不，不必向我担保；他的胆量早已证过了。」

「这样一桩事里，什么危险于我都是受欢迎的。」

「先生现在不如就去，回到你朋友那儿去？」

「我答应过在这儿等我的朋友回来。圣阿利尔伯爵说过，他要把我引见给伯爵夫人呢。」

===Original===
“And Monsieur is so simple as to believe him?”

“Why should I not?”

“Because he is jealous and cunning. You will see. He will never introduce you to his wife. He will come here and say he cannot find her, and promise another time.”

“I think I see him approaching, with my friend. No﻿—there is no lady with him.”

“I told you so. You will wait a long time for that happiness, if it is never to reach you except through his hands. In the meantime, you had better not let him see you so near me. He will suspect that we have been talking of his wife; and that will whet his jealousy and his vigilance.”

===Chinese===
「先生竟这样老实，信了他？」

「为什么不信？」

「因为他又妒又狡。你瞧着吧。他决不会把你引见给他夫人。他会到这儿来，说找不着她，答应改天再说。」

「我想我看见他来了，同我的朋友一道。不——他身边没有女士。」

「我怎么说来着。这桩幸福若非经他的手不能到你身上，你就有得等了。这会儿，你最好不要让他瞧见你挨我这么近。他会疑心我们在谈他妻子；那可要磨利他的妒心和警觉了。」

===Original===
I thanked my unknown friend in the mask, and withdrawing a few steps, came, by a little “circumbendibus,” upon the flank of the Count.

I smiled under my mask, as he assured me that the Duchesse de la Roqueme had changed her place, and taken the Countess with her; but he hoped, at some very early time, to have an opportunity of enabling her to make my acquaintance.

I avoided the Marquis d’Harmonville, who was following the Count. I was afraid he might propose accompanying me home, and had no wish to be forced to make an explanation.

I lost myself quickly, therefore, in the crowd, and moved, as rapidly as it would allow me, toward the Galerie des Glaces, which lay in the direction opposite to that in which I saw the Count and my friend the Marquis moving.

===Chinese===
我谢过这位不相识的假面朋友，退开几步，绕了个小小的「弯子」，转到伯爵的侧翼。

我在假面底下暗笑，听他向我担保说，拉罗克姆公爵夫人（Duchesse de la Roqueme）换了地方，把伯爵夫人也带走了；但他希望，过不了多久，便能有机会让她认识认识我。

我避开了跟着伯爵走来的德·哈蒙维尔侯爵。我怕他要提议陪我回家，而我不愿被迫解释一番。

于是我很快没入人流，朝着镜厅方向尽快挤去——那正好同我望见伯爵和侯爵朋友所走的方向相反。

## XV Strange Story of the Dragon Volant / XV 飞龙旅馆怪谈

===Original===
These fêtes were earlier in those days, and in France, than our modern balls are in London. I consulted my watch. It was a little past twelve.

It was a still and sultry night; the magnificent suite of rooms, vast as some of them were, could not be kept at a temperature less than oppressive, especially to people with masks on. In some places the crowd was inconvenient, and the profusion of lights added to the heat. I removed my mask, therefore, as I saw some other people do, who were as careless of mystery as I. I had hardly done so, and began to breathe more comfortably, when I heard a friendly English voice call me by my name. It was Tom Whistlewick, of the —﻿th Dragoons. He had unmasked, with a very flushed face, as I did. He was one of those Waterloo heroes, new from the mint of glory, whom, as a body, all the world, except France, revered; and the only thing I knew against him, was a habit of allaying his thirst, which was excessive, at balls, fêtes, musical parties, and all gatherings, where it was to be had, with champagne; and, as he introduced me to his friend, Monsieur Carmaignac, I observed that he spoke a little thick. Monsieur Carmaignac was little, lean, and as straight as a ramrod. He was bald, took snuff, and wore spectacles; and, as I soon learned, held an official position.

===Chinese===
那时候，在法国，这类盛会开得比我们如今伦敦的舞会早。我看了看表，刚过十二点。

那是个闷热无风的夜晚；这一连串豪华的厅堂，有几间大得惊人，可温度怎么也压不下来，闷得叫人喘不过气，戴着假面的人尤其难挨。有些地方拥挤不堪，满堂灯火又添着热气。于是我摘下假面——我看见另外一些人也不在乎什么秘密，照摘不误。我刚摘下，正开始呼吸得舒畅些，忽听一个友好的英国嗓音唤我的名字。是第——龙骑兵团的汤姆·惠斯尔威克（Tom Whistlewick）。他满脸通红，也像我一样摘了假面。他属于滑铁卢的英雄一代，刚刚从光荣的造币厂里新鲜出炉，全世界——法国除外——都把他们奉若神明；而据我所知，他唯一的不良嗜好，是在舞会、游园会、音乐晚会，以及一切弄得到酒喝的聚会上，拿香槟浇他那过分的渴。他把我介绍给他的朋友卡马尼亚克先生（Monsieur Carmaignac）时，我听出他的舌头已经有些大了。卡马尼亚克先生个子瘦小，腰板却直得像根通条。他秃顶，吸鼻烟，戴眼镜；而且我很快就知道，他在官府里担任着差事。

===Original===
Tom was facetious, sly, and rather difficult to understand, in his present pleasant mood. He was elevating his eyebrows and screwing his lips oddly, and fanning himself vaguely with his mask.

After some agreeable conversation, I was glad to observe that he preferred silence, and was satisfied with the role of listener, as I and Monsieur Carmaignac chatted; and he seated himself, with extraordinary caution and indecision, upon a bench, beside us, and seemed very soon to find a difficulty in keeping his eyes open.

“I heard you mention,” said the French gentleman, “that you had engaged an apartment in the Dragon Volant, about half a league from this. When I was in a different police department, about four years ago, two very strange cases were connected with that house. One was of a wealthy émigré, permitted to return to France, by the Em﻿—by Napoleon. He vanished. The other﻿—equally strange﻿—was the case of a Russian of rank and wealth. He disappeared just as mysteriously.”

===Chinese===
汤姆这时兴致正好，说话诙谐促狭，不大容易听懂。他眉毛一挑一挑，嘴唇一瘪一瘪，神气古怪，拿假面漫不经心地扇着风。

愉快地聊了一阵之后，我高兴地发现他宁可安静，甘当听众，听我和卡马尼亚克先生交谈；他小心翼翼、犹犹豫豫地在旁边一张长凳上坐下，不多时便觉得眼皮直打架了。

「我听见您说起，」这位法国绅士说，「您在飞龙旅馆订了房间，离这儿约半里格。四年前我在另一个警务部门时，有两桩极古怪的案子同那座宅子有牵连。一桩是一位富有的逃亡贵族（émigré），蒙皇——蒙拿破仑恩准回到法国。他失踪了。另一桩同样离奇，是一位有身份、有钱的俄国人。他消失得也一样神秘。」

===Original===
“My servant,” I said, “gave me a confused account of some occurrences, and, as well as I recollect he described the same persons﻿—I mean a returned French nobleman, and a Russian gentleman. But he made the whole story so marvellous﻿—I mean in the supernatural sense﻿—that, I confess, I did not believe a word of it.”

“No, there was nothing supernatural; but a great deal inexplicable,” said the French gentleman. “Of course there may be theories; but the thing was never explained, nor, so far as I know, was a ray of light ever thrown upon it.”

“Pray let me hear the story,” I said. “I think I have a claim, as it affects my quarters. You don’t suspect the people of the house?”

“Oh! it has changed hands since then. But there seemed to be a fatality about a particular room.”

===Chinese===
「我的仆人，」我说，「也向我讲过一桩糊涂事，我记得他说的正是这两个人——我是说，一位归国的法国贵族，一位俄国先生。可他把整个故事讲得神乎其神——我是说，神怪意义上的——恕我直言，我一个字也没信。」

「不，那里面没有什么超自然的东西；只是有许多说不清的地方，」这位法国绅士说。「当然，理论可以有几套；可事情从来没有解释清楚过，而且据我所知，也从来没有透进过一线光亮。」

「请把故事讲给我听吧，」我说。「我想我有这个权利，它牵涉到我的住处。您不怀疑店家里的人么？」

「哦！店家早就换过手了。不过那间特定的屋子，似乎带着一股命里的邪气。」

===Original===
“Could you describe that room?”

“Certainly. It is a spacious, panelled bedroom, up one pair of stairs, in the back of the house, and at the extreme right, as you look from its windows.”

“Ho! Really? Why, then, I have got the very room!” I said, beginning to be more interested﻿—perhaps the least bit in the world, disagreeably. “Did the people die, or were they actually spirited away?”

“No, they did not die﻿—they disappeared very oddly. I’ll tell you the particulars﻿—I happen to know them exactly, because I made an official visit, on the first occasion, to the house, to collect evidence; and although I did not go down there, upon the second, the papers came before me, and I dictated the official letter despatched to the relations of the people who had disappeared; they had applied to the government to investigate the affair. We had letters from the same relations more than two years later, from which we learned that the missing men had never turned up.”

===Chinese===
「您能把那间屋子描述一下么？」

「当然。那是一间宽敞的、装着护壁板的卧室，上去一层楼梯，在屋子背面，从窗户望出去，在最靠右的一头。」

「嗬！当真？那么，我住的正是那间屋！」我说，兴味越发浓了——也许还有那么一丝丝，不祥的意味。「那些人是死了呢，还是真被摄走了？」

「不，他们没有死——他们消失得非常古怪。我把细节讲给您听——我恰好知道得确切，因为头一桩案子，我专程到那宅子里做过一次公务查访，收集证词；第二桩我虽没有去，案卷却送到了我的手里，发给失踪者亲属的那封公函，也是由我口授的；是那些亲属呈请政府查办此案的。两年多以后，我们还收到同一些亲属的来信，从信里得知，失踪的人始终没有下落。」

===Original===
He took a pinch of snuff, and looked steadily at me.

“Never! I shall relate all that happened, so far as we could discover. The French noble, who was the Chevalier Château Blassemare, unlike most émigrés, had taken the matter in time, sold a large portion of his property before the revolution had proceeded so far as to render that next to impossible, and retired with a large sum. He brought with him about half a million of francs, the greater part of which he invested in the French funds; a much larger sum remained in Austrian land and securities. You will observe then that this gentleman was rich, and there was no allegation of his having lost money, or being, in any way, embarrassed. You see?”

I assented.

“This gentleman’s habits were not expensive in proportion to his means. He had suitable lodgings in Paris; and for a time, society, the theatres, and other reasonable amusements, engrossed him. He did not play. He was a middle-aged man, affecting youth, with the vanities which are usual in such persons; but, for the rest, he was a gentle and polite person, who disturbed nobody﻿—a person, you see, not likely to provoke an enmity.”

===Chinese===
他捏了一撮鼻烟，目不转睛地看着我。

「始终没有！我们把查明的一切都讲给您听。那位法国贵族，沙托·布拉塞马尔骑士（Chevalier Château Blassemare），与多数逃亡贵族不同，事情赶在了头里：大革命还没闹到叫人变卖家产近乎不可能的地步，他就卖掉了大部分产业，带着一大笔钱退了出来。他随身带回约五十万法郎，大部分投在法国公债里；更大的一笔留在奥地利的地产和证券上。您看得出，这位绅士是有钱人，没有任何传闻说他亏过钱，也没有说他手头短过。您明白？」

我点头称是。

「这位绅士的用度，比他的家底要节俭。他在巴黎住着相称的寓所；有一阵子，社交、戏园子和别的正当消遣占满了他的日子。他不赌。他是个中年人，偏要充年轻，带着这类人惯有的虚荣；不过其余方面，他是个和气的、有礼的人，谁也不招惹——您看，是个不像会结仇的人。」

===Original===
“Certainly not,” I agreed.

“Early in the summer of 1811, he got an order permitting him to copy a picture in one of these salons, and came down here, to Versailles, for the purpose. His work was getting on slowly. After a time he left his hotel, here, and went, by way of change, to the Dragon Volant: there he took, by special choice, the bedroom which has fallen to you by chance. From this time, it appeared, he painted little; and seldom visited his apartments in Paris. One night he saw the host of the Dragon Volant, and told him that he was going into Paris, to remain for a day or two, on very particular business; that his servant would accompany him, but that he would retain his apartments at the Dragon Volant, and return in a few days. He left some clothes there, but packed a portmanteau, took his dressing-case, and the rest, and, with his servant behind his carriage, drove into Paris. You observe all this, Monsieur?”

“Most attentively,” I answered.

“Well, Monsieur, as soon as they were approaching his lodgings, he stopped the carriage on a sudden, told his servant that he had changed his mind; that he would sleep elsewhere that night, that he had very particular business in the north of France, not far from Rouen, that he would set out before daylight on his journey, and return in a fortnight. He called a fiacre, took in his hand a leather bag which, the servant said, was just large enough to hold a few shirts and a coat, but that it was enormously heavy, as he could testify, for he held it in his hand, while his master took out his purse to count thirty-six Napoleons, for which the servant was to account when he should return. He then sent him on, in the carriage; and he, with the bag I have mentioned, got into the fiacre. Up to that, you see, the narrative is quite clear.”

“Perfectly,” I agreed.

===Chinese===
「自然不像。」我同意。

「1811 年初夏，他领到一张许可，可以临摹这些沙龙里的一幅画，为这个目的来到这儿，凡尔赛。他的画进展缓慢。过了一阵，他离开了这里的旅馆，为换换环境，搬到飞龙旅馆去：在那里，他特意挑中了那间卧室——如今恰好落到您手里。从这时起，看来他作画就少了；巴黎的寓所也难得去了。一天晚上，他见到飞龙旅馆的店主，告诉店主说他要进巴黎去，为着一桩极要紧的事，盘桓一两天；他的仆人随行，但飞龙旅馆的房间他要留着，过几天就回来。他留下几件衣裳，收拾了一只旅行提箱，带上梳妆匣和其他物件，仆人坐在马车后面，一路进了巴黎。这些您都听清了，先生？」

「聚精会神。」我答。

「好，先生，马车快到他的寓所时，他突然叫停车，对仆人说自己改了主意；那晚要另找地方睡；他在法兰西北部，离鲁昂（Rouen）不远，有非常要紧的差事，天不亮就要动身，两周后回来。他唤了一辆出租马车（fiacre），手里提一只皮袋——仆人说，那袋子刚够装几件衬衫和一件外衣，却重得出奇，这一点他可以作证，因为他亲手提过：他的主人掏出钱袋，数了三十六枚拿破仑金币，这笔钱仆人回来须交账。随后他打发仆人乘原来的马车继续赶路；他自己提着我说的那只袋子，上了出租马车。到这里为止，您看，事情一清二楚。」

「再清楚不过。」我同意。

===Original===
“Now comes the mystery,” said Monsieur Carmaignac. “After that, the Count Château Blassemare was never more seen, so far as we can make out, by acquaintance or friend. We learned that the day before the Count’s stockbroker had, by his direction, sold all his stock in the French funds, and handed him the cash it realized. The reason he gave him for this measure tallied with what he said to his servant. He told him that he was going to the north of France to settle some claims, and did not know exactly how much might be required. The bag, which had puzzled the servant by its weight, contained, no doubt, a large sum in gold. Will Monsieur try my snuff?”

He politely tendered his open snuffbox, of which I partook, experimentally.

“A reward was offered,” he continued, “when the inquiry was instituted, for any information tending to throw a light upon the mystery, which might be afforded by the driver of the fiacre ‘employed on the night of’ (so-and-so), ‘at about the hour of half-past ten, by a gentleman, with a black-leather travelling-bag in his hand, who descended from a private carriage, and gave his servant some money, which he counted twice over.’ About a hundred-and-fifty drivers applied, but not one of them was the right man. We did, however, elicit a curious and unexpected piece of evidence in quite another quarter. What a racket that plaguey harlequin makes with his sword!”

“Intolerable!” I chimed in.

===Chinese===
「谜就从这里开始，」卡马尼亚克先生说。「从那以后，据我们查明，沙托·布拉塞马尔伯爵再没有让任何熟人或朋友见过。我们得知，前一天，伯爵的证券经纪人奉他的指示，把他名下的法国公债全部抛售，把变现的现款交到了他手里。他向经纪人说明这样做的理由，同他对仆人说的正对得上。他说要去法兰西北部清理几宗债权，需要多少，一时说不好。那只叫仆人纳闷的沉重的袋子，装的无疑是一大笔金子。先生尝尝我的鼻烟？」

他客气地递上打开的鼻烟盒，我尝了一点试试。

「立案查办的时候，」他接着说，「悬了赏，征求一切有助于照亮这个谜团的线索——比如『某夜』（某年某月某日）『十点半前后，由一位手提黑皮旅行袋的先生雇车，此人从一辆私人马车上下来，给了仆人一些钱，数了两遍』——寻找当时的那位车夫。前来应征的车夫约有一百五十个，可惜没一个对得上号。不过，我们在完全另一个方向上，倒是引出了一件离奇而出人意料的证词。那个讨厌的丑角（harlequin）拿剑敲得真吵！」

「吵死人了！」我附和道。

===Original===
The harlequin was soon gone, and he resumed.

“The evidence I speak of, came from a boy, about twelve years old, who knew the appearance of the Count perfectly, having been often employed by him as a messenger. He stated that about half-past twelve o’clock, on the same night﻿—upon which you are to observe, there was a brilliant moon﻿—he was sent, his mother having been suddenly taken ill, for the *sage femme* who lived within a stone’s throw of the Dragon Volant. His father’s house, from which he started, was a mile away, or more, from that inn, in order to reach which he had to pass round the park of the Château de la Carque, at the site most remote from the point to which he was going. It passes the old churchyard of St. Aubin, which is separated from the road only by a very low fence, and two or three enormous old trees. The boy was a little nervous as he approached this ancient cemetery; and, under the bright moonlight, he saw a man whom he distinctly recognised as the Count, whom they designated by a sobriquet which means ‘the man of smiles.’ He was looking rueful enough now, and was seated on the side of a tombstone, on which he had laid a pistol, while he was ramming home the charge of another.

“The boy got cautiously by, on tiptoe, with his eyes all the time on the Count Château Blassemare, or the man he mistook for him; his dress was not what he usually wore, but the witness swore that he could not be mistaken as to his identity. He said his face looked grave and stern; but though he did not smile, it was the same face he knew so well. Nothing would make him swerve from that. If that were he, it was the last time he was seen. He has never been heard of since. Nothing could be heard of him in the neighbourhood of Rouen. There has been no evidence of his death; and there is no sign that he is living.”

===Chinese===
丑角不一会儿就走开了，他又接着讲。

「我说的这份证词，出自一个十二岁上下的男孩，他常给伯爵当信差，认得他的相貌认得极熟。他陈述说，同一夜十二点半前后——您要注意，那晚月色极亮——他母亲突然病了，打发他去请产婆（sage femme），产婆的住处离飞龙旅馆不过一箭之地。他动身的那所父亲的家，离那家客栈有一英里开外，要到那里，他必须绕着拉卡尔克城堡的园林走，从离目的地最远的一头绕过去。路上要经过圣奥班（St. Aubin）的老教堂墓地，墓地同大路只隔着一道很矮的栅栏和两三棵巨大的老树。男孩走近这座古墓园时心里有点发毛；明晃晃的月光下，他看见一个人，一眼就认出是伯爵——那一带的人给他起了个诨名，意思是『笑脸人』。这会儿他一脸愁相，坐在一方墓碑边上，碑上搁着一支手枪，他正往另一支里捣实火药。

「男孩蹑手蹑脚、小心翼翼地走了过去，眼睛一直盯着沙托·布拉塞马尔伯爵——或者他误认成伯爵的那个人；那人穿的不是平日的衣裳，可证人一口咬定，人绝不会认错。他说那人的脸显得又沉又严厉；可虽然不笑，还是他熟悉至极的那张脸。任凭什么也不能叫他改口。如果那真是他，那就是人们最后一次见到他。此后再无音讯。鲁昂一带也打听过，杳无音信。没有他死亡的凭证；也没有他还活着的迹象。」

===Original===
“That certainly is a most singular case,” I replied; and was about to ask a question or two, when Tom Whistlewick who, without my observing it, had been taking a ramble, returned, a great deal more awake, and a great deal less tipsy.

“I say, Carmaignac, it is getting late, and I must go; I really must, for the reason I told you﻿—and, Beckett, we must soon meet again.”

“I regret very much, Monsieur, my not being able at present to relate to you the other case, that of another tenant of the very same room﻿—a case more mysterious and sinister than the last﻿—and which occurred in the autumn of the same year.”

“Will you both do a very good-natured thing, and come and dine with me at the Dragon Volant tomorrow?”

So, as we pursued our way along the Galerie des Glaces, I extracted their promise.

===Chinese===
「这实在是一桩再离奇不过的案子，」我答道，正要再问上一两句，汤姆·惠斯尔威克——我一时没留神，他出去转了一圈——回来了，人清醒了许多，酒也醒了大半。

「我说，卡马尼亚克，时候不早了，我得走了；真的必须走，就是我跟你说的那个缘故——还有，贝克特，咱们得尽快再聚。」

「非常遗憾，先生，眼下不能把另一桩案子也讲给您听——正是同一间屋子的另一位房客——那桩案子比这桩更神秘、更凶险——就发生在同一年秋天。」

「两位可肯赏光做件好事，明天到飞龙旅馆来同我吃饭？」

于是，我们沿着镜厅一路走去，我从他们口中讨来了这个承诺。

===Original===
“By Jove!” said Whistlewick, when this was done; “look at that pagoda, or sedan chair, or whatever it is, just where those fellows set it down, and not one of them near it! I can’t imagine how they tell fortunes so devilish well. Jack Nuffles﻿—I met him here tonight﻿—says they are gipsies﻿—where are they, I wonder? I’ll go over and have a peep at the prophet.”

I saw him plucking at the blinds, which were constructed something on the principle of Venetian blinds; the red curtains were inside; but they did not yield, and he could only peep under one that did not come quite down.

When he rejoined us, he related: “I could scarcely see the old fellow, it’s so dark. He is covered with gold and red, and has an embroidered hat on like a mandarin’s; he’s fast asleep; and, by Jove, he smells like a polecat! It’s worth going over only to have it to say. Fiew! pooh! oh! It *is* a perfume. Faugh!”

===Chinese===
「老天！」事情敲定之后，惠斯尔威克说，「瞧那座宝塔，还是轿椅子，反正就是那玩意儿，还搁在那伙人放下的地方，附近连个人影也没有！真不明白他们的卦怎么算得那么鬼灵。杰克·纳弗尔斯（Jack Nuffles）——今晚在这儿碰见的——说他们是吉卜赛人——人都上哪儿去了呢？我过去瞧瞧那位预言家。」

我看见他去拉那竹帘——那帘子造得有点像百叶窗；红绸帘在里层；可帘子纹丝不动，他只能从一面没有完全放到底的帘子底下往里窥。

他回到我们跟前，说道：「那老家伙我几乎看不清，里头黑得很。他裹着金色和红色的衣裳，戴一顶绣花帽，活像个中国大官；他睡得正香；而且，老天在上，他浑身一股臭鼬味！单为回去有得可说，就值得走这一遭。哟！呸！哦！这*可真*是香水呢。呸！」

===Original===
Not caring to accept this tempting invitation, we got along slowly toward the door. I bid them good night, reminding them of their promise. And so found my way at last to my carriage; and was soon rolling slowly toward the Dragon Volant, on the loneliest of roads, under old trees, and the soft moonlight.

What a number of things had happened within the last two hours! what a variety of strange and vivid pictures were crowded together in that brief space! What an adventure was before me!

The silent, moonlighted, solitary road, how it contrasted with the many-eddied whirl of pleasure from whose roar and music, lights, diamonds and colours, I had just extricated myself.

The sight of lonely Nature at such an hour, acts like a sudden sedative. The madness and guilt of my pursuit struck me with a momentary compunction and horror. I wished I had never entered the labyrinth which was leading me, I knew not whither. It was too late to think of that now; but the bitter was already stealing into my cup; and vague anticipations lay, for a few minutes, heavy on my heart. It would not have taken much to make me disclose my unmanly state of mind to my lively friend, Alfred Ogle, nor even to the milder ridicule of the agreeable Tom Whistlewick.

===Chinese===
这番诱人的邀约我们无意领受，便慢慢朝门口挪去。我同他们道了晚安，又提醒了一遍他们的承诺。就这样，我终于找到了自己的马车；不久便沿着一条再僻静不过的路，在老树和柔亮的月光下，朝飞龙旅馆缓缓驶去。

短短两个钟头里，发生了多少事啊！那短短一段时光里，挤进了多少幅光怪陆离、鲜活如画的图景！摆在我面前的，又是怎样一场奇遇！

这条寂静的、月光朗照的、冷清清的路，同我方才抽身而出的那股欢娱的旋涡恰成对照——那吼声与音乐、灯火、钻石与色彩的万千回流。

这种时辰里孤独的大自然，看上一眼，就像服下一剂猛然的安神药。我这桩追逐的疯狂与罪愆，一时叫我又悔又惧。我但愿自己从没踏进这座不知引我何往的迷宫。现在想这些已经太迟；可苦味早已悄悄渗进我的杯中；一时之间，模糊的预感沉沉压在我心头。只消再添一点点，我就会把自己这副没出息的心境，向我的活宝朋友阿尔弗雷德·奥格尔（Alfred Ogle）和盘托出，甚至向那位较温和、却也免不了要取笑人的汤姆·惠斯尔威克吐露。

## XVI The Parc of the Château de la Carque / XVI 拉卡尔克城堡园林

===Original===
There was no danger of the Dragon Volant’s closing its doors on that occasion till three or four in the morning. There were quartered there many servants of great people, whose masters would not leave the ball till the last moment, and who could not return to their corners in the Dragon Volant, till their last services had been rendered.

I knew, therefore, I should have ample time for my mysterious excursion without exciting curiosity by being shut out.

And now we pulled up under the canopy of boughs, before the sign of the Dragon Volant, and the light that shone from its hall-door.

===Chinese===
那天晚上，飞龙旅馆绝无三四点钟之前关门的道理。馆里住着许多大人物的仆役，主人们不熬到舞会最后一刻不肯走，他们也就得伺候到最后一差办完，才回得了自己在飞龙旅馆的角落。

所以我知道，我这趟神秘的夜游尽有时间，不至因为被关在门外而惹人起疑。

这时，我们在枝叶交覆的穹顶下停了车，停在飞龙旅馆的招牌底下，停在门厅透出的灯光跟前。

===Original===
I dismissed my carriage, ran up the broad staircase, mask in hand, with my domino fluttering about me, and entered the large bedroom. The black wainscoting and stately furniture, with the dark curtains of the very tall bed, made the night there more sombre.

An oblique patch of moonlight was thrown upon the floor from the window to which I hastened. I looked out upon the landscape slumbering in those silvery beams. There stood the outline of the Château de la Carque, its chimneys, and many turrets with their extinguisher-shaped roofs black against the soft grey sky. There, also, more in the foreground, about midway between the window where I stood, and the château, but a little to the left, I traced the tufted masses of the grove which the lady in the mask had appointed as the trysting-place, where I and the beautiful Countess were to meet that night.

I took the bearings of this gloomy bit of wood, whose foliage glimmered softly at top in the light of the moon.

You may guess with what a strange interest and swelling of the heart I gazed on the unknown scene of my coming adventure.

===Chinese===
我打发走马车，一手拿着假面，斗篷在身边飘拂，奔上宽阔的楼梯，进了那间大卧室。黑沉沉的护壁板、气派的家具，加上那张高得出奇的黑帐大床，把那里的夜色衬得愈发幽暗。

一道斜斜的月光洒在地板上，我几步奔到窗前。向外望去，景色正沉睡在银辉里。拉卡尔克城堡的轮廓立在那里，烟囱和一座座灭火器顶的塔楼，衬着柔和的灰天，显得漆黑。还有，更近处，大约在我站立的窗户同城堡之间的半道，略偏左一点，我认出了那丛团团簇簇的树影——假面女士指定的幽会之地，当夜我同美丽的伯爵夫人相会的地方。

我记准了这小片阴沉沉的树林的方位，树梢的叶子在月光里泛着柔和的微光。

你可以想见，我望着这即将上演我的奇遇的陌生场景时，心头怀着怎样一种奇异的兴味，怎样一阵激荡。

===Original===
But time was flying, and the hour already near. I threw my robe upon a sofa; I groped out a pair of boots, which I substituted for those thin heelless shoes, in those days called “pumps,” without which a gentleman could not attend an evening party. I put on my hat, and lastly, I took a pair of loaded pistols which I had been advised were satisfactory companions in the then unsettled state of French society: swarms of disbanded soldiers, some of them alleged to be desperate characters, being everywhere to be met with. These preparations made, I confess I took a looking-glass to the window to see how I looked in the moonlight; and being satisfied, I replaced it, and ran downstairs.

===Chinese===
可是时光在飞，约定的时辰已经临近。我把斗篷抛在沙发上；摸出一双靴子换上——换下那双薄底无跟、当年唤作「浅口便鞋」（pumps）的鞋，没有它，绅士进不得晚会。我戴上帽子，末了，又取了一对上足弹药的手枪——有人劝我带上，说在那时法国社会动荡的情形下，它们是叫人放心的旅伴：遣散的兵痞成群结队，据说其中不乏亡命之徒，到处都碰得上。诸事齐备，我承认，我还把一面镜子拿到窗前，照照月光下的自己是个什么模样；照满意了，放回镜子，奔下楼去。

===Original===
In the hall I called for my servant.

“St. Clair,” said I; “I mean to take a little moonlight ramble, only ten minutes or so. You must not go to bed until I return. If the night is very beautiful, I may possibly extend my ramble a little.”

So down the steps I lounged, looking first over my right, and then over my left shoulder, like a man uncertain which direction to take, and I sauntered up the road, gazing now at the moon, and now at the thin white clouds in the opposite direction, whistling, all the time, an air which I had picked up at one of the theatres.

When I had got a couple of hundred yards away from the Dragon Volant, my minstrelsy totally ceased; and I turned about, and glanced sharply down the road that looked as white as hoarfrost under the moon, and saw the gable of the old inn, and a window, partly concealed by the foliage, with a dusky light shining from it.

No sound of footstep was stirring; no sign of human figure in sight. I consulted my watch, which the light was sufficiently strong to enable me to do. It now wanted but eight minutes of the appointed hour. A thick mantle of ivy at this point covered the wall and rose in a clustering head at top.

===Chinese===
到了门厅，我叫来仆人。

「圣克莱尔，」我说，「我想趁着月色散一小会儿步，十来分钟的样子。我回来之前你不许睡。夜色要是实在好，说不定我会多走一会儿。」

于是我慢悠悠踱下台阶，先朝右肩后望望，又朝左肩后望望，像个拿不定主意往哪边走的人，信步往大路上溜达，一会儿望望月亮，一会儿望望对面天上的薄薄白云，嘴里一路吹着从戏园子里学来的一支调子。

走出离飞龙旅馆二百来码，我的口哨便戛然而止；我转过身，朝月光下白得像霜的大路迅疾一扫，望见老客栈的山墙，和一扇被树叶半掩的窗，透出一点昏黄的光。

四下没有一点脚步声；望不见一个人影。我看了看表——光线还够我看。离约定时辰只差八分钟了。墙在这里被一层厚厚的常春藤盖满，顶上攒作一簇。

===Original===
It afforded me facilities for scaling the wall, and a partial screen for my operations, if any eye should chance to be looking that way. And now it was done. I was in the park of the Château de la Carque, as nefarious a poacher as ever trespassed on the grounds of unsuspicious lord!

Before me rose the appointed grove, which looked as black as a clump of gigantic hearse-plumes. It seemed to tower higher and higher at every step; and cast a broader and blacker shadow toward my feet. On I marched, and was glad when I plunged into the shadow which concealed me. Now I was among the grand old lime and chestnut trees﻿—my heart beat fast with expectation.

This grove opened, a little, near the middle; and in the space thus cleared, there stood with a surrounding flight of steps, a small Greek temple or shrine, with a statue in the centre. It was built of white marble with fluted Corinthian columns, and the crevices were tufted with grass; moss had shown itself on pedestal and cornice, and signs of long neglect and decay were apparent in its discoloured and weatherworn marble. A few feet in front of the steps a fountain, fed from the great ponds at the other side of the château, was making a constant tinkle and plashing in a wide marble basin, and the jet of water glimmered like a shower of diamonds in the broken moonlight. The very neglect and half-ruinous state of all this made it only the prettier, as well as sadder. I was too intently watching for the arrival of the lady, in the direction of the château, to study these things; but the half-noted effect of them was romantic, and suggested somehow the grotto and the fountain, and the apparition of Egeria.

===Chinese===
这正好方便我翻墙，还能替我的动作遮上一半——万一有谁的眼睛恰朝这边望。就这样，事成了。我已经身在拉卡尔克城堡的园林里，成了历来潜入毫无戒心的爵爷园地的不速之客里，最歹毒不过的偷猎贼！

约定的树丛耸立在前，黑得像一蓬巨大的丧礼羽饰。仿佛我每走一步，它便越升越高，朝我脚下投来越来越宽、越来越黑的影子。我继续前行，终于一头扎进那片把我也藏起来的阴影里，心里一阵欢喜。此刻我已置身于高大的老菩提树和栗树之间——心期待得怦怦直跳。

树丛在近中央处微微开敞；在那片空地上，围着一圈石阶，立着一座小小的希腊式神殿或圣祠，中央有一尊雕像。它以白色大理石筑成，立着带槽纹的科林斯石柱，石缝里丛生着草；基座和檐口生了苔，褪色风化的大理石上，处处是长久荒废的痕迹。台阶前几尺，有一眼泉水——引自城堡另一侧的大池塘——不断在一座宽阔的大理石水盘里叮咚作响、溅起水花，水柱在破碎的月光里闪动，像一阵钻石雨。这一切的荒废和半圮，反而使它更美，也更凄凉。我一心盯着城堡的方向，等那位女士到来，无暇细看这些东西；但它们那半入眼帘的气象自有一种浪漫的意味，不知怎么叫人想起洞窟和泉水，想起埃杰里亚（Egeria）显灵的地方。

===Original===
As I watched a voice spoke to me, a little behind my left shoulder. I turned, almost with a start, and the masque, in the costume of Mademoiselle de la Vallière stood there.

“The Countess will be here presently,” she said. The lady stood upon the open space, and the moonlight fell unbroken upon her. Nothing could be more becoming; her figure looked more graceful and elegant than ever. “In the meantime I shall tell you some peculiarities of her situation. She is unhappy; miserable in an ill-assorted marriage, with a jealous tyrant who now would constrain her to sell her diamonds, which are﻿—”

“Worth thirty thousand pounds sterling. I heard all that from a friend. Can I aid the Countess in her unequal struggle? Say but how, and the greater the danger or the sacrifice, the happier will it make me. *Can* I aid her?”

“If you despise a danger﻿—which, yet, is not a danger; if you despise, as she does, the tyrannical canons of the world; and, if you are chivalrous enough to devote yourself to a lady’s cause, with no reward but her poor gratitude; if you can do these things you can aid her, and earn a foremost place, not in her gratitude only, but in her friendship.”

At those words the lady in the mask turned away, and seemed to weep.

===Chinese===
正望着，一个声音在我左肩后不远的地方开了口。我几乎是一惊地转过身——那位德·拉瓦利埃小姐装束的假面女士就站在那里。

「伯爵夫人一会儿就到。」她说。女士站在空地上，月光无遮无拦地落在她身上。再没有比这更相称的光景了；她的身段显得比先前更优雅、更出众。「趁这工夫，我把她处境的几桩特别之处讲给你听。她不快活；这桩错配的婚姻叫她痛苦，丈夫是个妒忌的暴君，如今又要逼她卖掉她的钻石，那钻石是——」

「值三万镑。这些我都听一位朋友说过了。我能帮伯爵夫人打这场力量悬殊的仗么？只消说个办法，危险也好，牺牲也好，越大越叫我快活。*我能*帮她么？」

「如果你蔑视一样危险——其实那也算不得危险；如果你同她一样，蔑视世人那些专横的成规；如果你有足够的侠气，肯为一位女士的事业效命，除了她可怜的一点感激之外别无所图；你若做得到这些，你就能帮她，而且赢得一个首屈一指的位置——不止在她的感激里，更在她的友谊里。」

说这话时，假面女士转过脸去，似乎哭了。

===Original===
I vowed myself the willing slave of the Countess. “But,” I added, “you told me she would soon be here.”

“That is, if nothing unforeseen should happen; but with the eye of the Count de St. Alyre in the house, and open, it is seldom safe to stir.”

“Does she wish to see me?” I asked, with a tender hesitation.

“First, say have you really thought of *her*, more than once, since the adventure of the Belle Étoile.”

“She never leaves my thoughts; day and night her beautiful eyes haunt me; her sweet voice is always in my ear.”

“Mine is said to resemble hers,” said the mask.

“So it does,” I answered. “But it is only a resemblance.”

===Chinese===
我立誓甘为伯爵夫人的奴仆。「可是，」我又添上一句，「你告诉过我，她很快就来。」

「那是说，若不出岔子；可圣阿利尔伯爵的眼睛只要还在宅子里睁着，动一动都难得安全。」

「她想见我么？」我问，语声带着温柔的迟疑。

「先回答我：自美星旅馆那桩事之后，你当真想起过*她*，不止一次？」

「她从没离开过我的心；日日夜夜，她那双美丽的眼睛追着我；她的甜嗓子总在我耳边。」

「人家说，我的嗓子像她的。」假面说。

「确实像，」我答道，「不过只是像罢了。」

===Original===
“Oh! then mine is better?”

“Pardon me, Mademoiselle, I did not say *that*. Yours is a sweet voice, but I fancy a little higher.”

“A little shriller, you would say,” answered the De la Vallière, I fancied a good deal vexed.

“No, not shriller: your voice is not shrill, it is beautifully sweet; but not so pathetically sweet as her.”

“That is prejudice, Monsieur; it is not true.”

I bowed; I could not contradict a lady.

“I see, Monsieur, you laugh at me; you think me vain, because I claim in some points to be equal to the Countess de St. Alyre. I challenge you to say, my hand, at least, is less beautiful than hers.” As she thus spoke, she drew her glove off, and extended her hand, back upward, in the moonlight.

The lady seemed really nettled. It was undignified and irritating; for in this uninteresting competition the precious moments were flying, and my interview leading apparently to nothing.

===Chinese===
「哦！那么我的更好？」

「恕我直言，小姐，我可没*那么*说。您的嗓音也甜，只是我想，略微高一点。」

「您是想说，尖一点。」德·拉瓦利埃小姐答道，我看她恼得不轻。

「不，不是尖：您的嗓子不尖，甜得很美；只是不像她的那样，甜得叫人肠断。」

「那是偏心，先生；不是实话。」

我鞠了一躬；对女士是不能反驳的。

「我明白了，先生，你在笑话我；你觉得我虚荣，因为我居然在某些地方自比圣阿利尔伯爵夫人。那我倒要请你说说，至少这只手，哪里就比她的差了。」她一面说，一面褪下手套，手背朝上，伸到月光里。

这位女士看来当真动了气。这既失身份，又叫人恼火；因为在这场无谓的较量里，宝贵的时光正在飞逝，而我的会面显然一无所成。

===Original===
“You will admit, then, that my hand is as beautiful as hers?”

“I cannot admit it, Mademoiselle,” said I, with the honesty of irritation. “I will not enter into comparisons, but the Countess de St. Alyre is, in all respects, the most beautiful lady I ever beheld.”

The masque laughed coldly, and then, more and more softly, said, with a sigh, “I will prove all I say.” And as she spoke she removed the mask: and the Countess de St. Alyre, smiling, confused, bashful, more beautiful than ever, stood before me!

“Good Heavens!” I exclaimed. “How monstrously stupid I have been. And it was to Madame la Comtesse that I spoke for so long in the salon!” I gazed on her in silence. And with a low sweet laugh of good nature she extended her hand. I took it, and carried it to my lips.

“No, you must not do that,” she said, quietly, “we are not old enough friends yet. I find, although you were mistaken, that you do remember the Countess of the Belle Étoile, and that you are a champion true and fearless. Had you yielded to the claims just now pressed upon you by the rivalry of Mademoiselle de la Vallière, in her mask, the Countess de St. Alyre should never have trusted or seen you more. I now am sure that you are true, as well as brave. You now know that I have not forgotten you; and, also, that if you would risk your life for me, I, too, would brave some danger, rather than lose my friend forever. I have but a few moments more. Will you come here again tomorrow night, at a quarter past eleven? I will be here at that moment; you must exercise the most scrupulous care to prevent suspicion that you have come here, Monsieur. *You owe that to me.*”

===Chinese===
「那么您总得承认，我的手同她的一样美？」

「我不能承认，小姐，」我说，带着火气里的一股诚实。「我不愿比来比去；只是圣阿利尔伯爵夫人，无论哪一方面，都是我平生所见最美的女士。」

假面冷冷一笑，接着越笑越轻柔，叹了一口气道：「我说的，都要证明给你看。」说着，她摘下了假面：圣阿利尔伯爵夫人，含着笑，又羞又怯，比先前更美，就站在我面前！

「老天爷！」我喊道。「我蠢得也太骇人了！原来方才在沙龙里同我讲了半天的，是伯爵夫人本人！」我默默凝视着她。她发出一声低低的、好性儿的甜笑，伸出手来。我接过，送到唇边。

「不，这可不行，」她平静地说，「我们交情还没到这一步。我发觉，你虽然认错了人，却当真记得美星旅馆的伯爵夫人，是个真心而不畏缩的骑士。方才假面的德·拉瓦利埃小姐同你争，你若依了她那些强求，圣阿利尔伯爵夫人从此再也不会信任你、见你。现在我确知你真心，也确知你胆大。你也知道了，我没有忘记你；还知道了，你肯为我舍命，我也甘冒几分危险，不肯永远失去我的朋友。我只有几分钟了。明晚十一点一刻，你再来这里好么？我在那时到这里；你必须万分小心，别让人疑心你到过这里，先生。*这是你欠我的。*」

===Original===
She spoke these last words with the most solemn entreaty.

I vowed again and again, that I would die rather than permit the least rashness to endanger the secret which made all the interest and value of my life.

She was looking, I thought, more and more beautiful every moment. My enthusiasm expanded in proportion.

“You must come tomorrow night by a different route,” she said; “and if you come again, we can change it once more. At the other side of the château there is a little churchyard, with a ruined chapel. The neighbours are afraid to pass it by night. The road is deserted there, and a stile opens a way into these grounds. Cross it and you can find a covert of thickets, to within fifty steps of this spot.”

I promised, of course, to observe her instructions implicitly.

===Chinese===
最后这几句，她说得恳切至极。

我一再立誓，宁可死，也决不容半点轻率危及那个秘密——它是我全部生趣和命脉所系。

我觉得她一刻比一刻更美。我的热情也随之高涨。

「明晚你得换一条路来，」她说；「你若再来，咱们还可以再换。城堡另一侧有一座小教堂墓地，带一座破败的小礼拜堂。邻人夜里不敢打那里过。那段路荒僻，有一道踏级通进这片园地。跨过去，你就能找到一条灌木掩蔽的小径，一直通到离此地五十步之内。」

我自然言听计从，一一应承。

===Original===
“I have lived for more than a year in an agony of irresolution. I have decided at last. I have lived a melancholy life; a lonelier life than is passed in the cloister. I have had no one to confide in; no one to advise me; no one to save me from the horrors of my existence. I have found a brave and prompt friend at last. Shall I ever forget the heroic tableau of the hall of the Belle Étoile? Have you﻿—have you really kept the rose I gave you, as we parted? Yes﻿—you swear it. You need not; I trust you. Richard, how often have I in solitude repeated your name, learned from my servant. Richard, my hero! Oh! Richard! Oh, my king! I love you.”

I would have folded her to my heart﻿—thrown myself at her feet. But this beautiful and﻿—shall I say it﻿—inconsistent woman repelled me.

“No, we must not waste our moments in extravagances. Understand my case. There is no such thing as indifference in the married state. Not to love one’s husband,” she continued, “is to hate him. The Count, ridiculous in all else, is formidable in his jealousy. In mercy, then, to me, observe caution. Affect to all you speak to, the most complete ignorance of all the people in the Château de la Carque; and, if anyone in your presence mentions the Count or Countess de St. Alyre, be sure you say you never saw either. I shall have more to say to you tomorrow night. I have reasons that I cannot now explain, for all I do, and all I postpone. Farewell. Go! Leave me.”

===Chinese===
「一年多来，我活在无法决断的煎熬里。我终于下了决心。我过着愁惨的日子；比修道院里的日子还要孤单。无人可倾诉；无人可商量；无人救我跳出这处境的苦海。我终于找到了一个勇敢而爽快的朋友。美星旅馆门厅里那幅英雄画面，我怎能忘记？你——你当真留着我分手时送你的那朵玫瑰？是——你起誓了。不必起誓；我信你。理查德，我独自一人时，多少遍念着从仆人那里问来的你的名字。理查德，我的英雄！哦，理查德！哦，我的君王！我爱你。」

我真想把她搂进怀里——跪倒在她脚下。可这位美丽的、又该怎么说呢——反复无常的女人推开了我。

「不，我们不可把光阴耗费在痴狂上。要明白我的处境。婚姻里没有冷漠这回事。不爱自己的丈夫，」她接着说，「就是恨他。伯爵在一切事情上都可笑，唯独嫉妒起来可怕。那么，可怜我吧，务必小心。凡同你说话的人，你都要装作全然不识拉卡尔克城堡里的任何人；有人当着你的面提起圣阿利尔伯爵或伯爵夫人，你千万说从没见过这两位。明晚我还有话对你说。我做的一切、我推迟的一切，都有我此刻无法说明的缘由。别了。去吧！离开我。」

===Original===
She waved me back, peremptorily. I echoed her “farewell,” and obeyed.

This interview had not lasted, I think, more than ten minutes. I scaled the park-wall again, and reached the Dragon Volant before its doors were closed.

I lay awake in my bed, in a fever of elation. I saw, till the dawn broke, and chased the vision, the beautiful Countess de St. Alyre, always in the dark, before me.

===Chinese===
她断然挥手叫我退后。我应了声「别了」，从命而去。

这场会面，我想，前后不过十分钟。我又翻过园林的墙，赶在飞龙旅馆关门之前回到了店中。

我躺在床上，兴奋得睡不着，浑身发热。直到天色发白、驱散幻影之前，我一直望见美丽的圣阿利尔伯爵夫人，总在黑暗里，在我眼前。

## XVII The Tenant of the Palanquin / XVII 轿中占卜师

===Original===
The Marquis called on me next day. My late breakfast was still upon the table.

He had come, he said, to ask a favour. An accident had happened to his carriage in the crowd on leaving the ball, and he begged, if I were going into Paris, a seat in mine﻿—I was going in, and was extremely glad of his company. He came with me to my hotel; we went up to my rooms. I was surprised to see a man seated in an easy chair, with his back towards us, reading a newspaper. He rose. It was the Count de St. Alyre, his gold spectacles on his nose; his black wig, in oily curls, lying close to his narrow head, and showing, like carved ebony over a repulsive visage of boxwood. His black muffler had been pulled down. His right arm was in a sling. I don’t know whether there was anything unusual in his countenance that day, or whether it was but the effect of prejudice arising from all I had heard in my mysterious interview in his park, but I thought his countenance was more strikingly forbidding than I had seen it before.

I was not callous enough in the ways of sin to meet this man, injured at least in intent, thus suddenly, without a momentary disturbance.

He smiled.

===Chinese===
第二天，侯爵来拜访我。我那顿迟开的早饭还摆在桌上。

他说，他是来求个人情的。昨晚离开舞会时，他的马车在人群里出了岔子，所以他想求个座位——如果我进巴黎的话。我正要进城，极愿与他同行。他随我到了旅馆；我们上楼进了我的房间。我吃了一惊：安乐椅里坐着一个人，背朝着我们，正在读报。那人站起身来。是圣阿利尔伯爵，鼻梁上架着金丝眼镜；黑色假发打着油亮的卷儿，紧贴着他那窄窄的头颅，看上去就像乌木雕饰底下露出一张黄杨木刻的可憎面孔。黑围巾拉到了下巴底下。右臂吊着绷带。我说不清那天他的脸是否真有什么异样，也不知是不是夜里在他园林中那场神秘会谈、听了那许多话之后先入为主的成见作祟，只觉得他那副面相比我从前所见的更加阴森拒人。

在罪孽的门道上，我还没有练出足够硬的心肠，骤然见到这个至少在意图上受过我伤害的人，心头不由一阵慌乱。

他微微一笑。

===Original===
“I called, Monsieur Beckett, in the hope of finding you here,” he croaked, “and I meditated, I fear, taking a great liberty, but my friend the Marquis d’Harmonville, on whom I have perhaps some claim, will perhaps give me the assistance I require so much.”

“With great pleasure,” said the Marquis, “but not till after six o’clock. I must go this moment to a meeting of three or four people, whom I cannot disappoint, and I know, perfectly, we cannot break up earlier.”

“What am I to do?” exclaimed the Count, “an hour would have done it all. Was ever contretemps so unlucky!”

“I’ll give you an hour, with pleasure,” said I.

“How very good of you, Monsieur, I hardly dare to hope it. The business, for so gay and charming a man as Monsieur Beckett, is a little *funeste*. Pray read this note which reached me this morning.”

It certainly was not cheerful. It was a note stating that the body of his, the Count’s cousin, Monsieur de St. Amand, who had died at his house, the Château Clery, had been, in accordance with his written directions, sent for burial at Père La Chaise, and, with the permission of the Count de St. Alyre, would reach his house (the Château de la Carque), at about ten o’clock on the night following, to be conveyed thence in a hearse, with any member of the family who might wish to attend the obsequies.

===Chinese===
「我登门拜访，贝克特先生，正盼着您在这儿，」他哑着嗓子说，「我盘算着的，恕我冒昧，是一桩大大的叨扰；可我的朋友德·哈蒙维尔侯爵——我于他或许还有几分情分——大约肯给我眼下急需的帮衬。」

「非常乐意，」侯爵说，「不过得等到六点以后。我这刻就要去赴一个三四人的聚会，那些人我万不能爽约，而且我清楚得很，散会不会早。」

「那我可怎么办？」伯爵嚷道，「一个钟头本来就能把事全办完。天底下还有比这更倒霉的意外（contretemps）么！」

「我给您一个钟头，乐意效劳。」我说。

「先生真是太好心了，我简直不敢奢望。这桩差事，对贝克特先生这样风流有趣的人来说，未免有点*凶煞*（funeste）。请读读今早送到我手中的这张便条。」

这便条确实不轻松。上面说，伯爵的表亲德·圣阿芒先生（Monsieur de St. Amand），在他家克莱里城堡（Château Clery）病故，遵其书面遗嘱，遗体已启运拉雪兹神父公墓安葬；经圣阿利尔伯爵许可，灵柩将于次晚十点前后抵达他家（拉卡尔克城堡），再从那里由灵车载往墓地，家族中人愿送葬者可随行。

===Original===
“I did not see the poor gentleman twice in my life,” said the Count, “but this office, as he has no other kinsman, disagreeable as it is, I could scarcely decline, and so I want to attend at the office to have the book signed, and the order entered. But here is another misery. By ill luck, I have sprained my thumb, and can’t sign my name for a week to come. However, one name answers as well as another. Yours as well as mine. And as you are so good as to come with me, all will go right.”

Away, we drove. The Count gave me a memorandum of the Christian and surnames of the deceased, his age, the complaint he died of, and the usual particulars; also a note of the exact position in which a grave, the dimensions of which were described, of the ordinary simple kind, was to be dug, between two vaults belonging to the family of St. Amand. The funeral, it was stated, would arrive at half-past one o’clock a.m. (the next night but one); and he handed me the money, with extra fees, for a burial by night. It was a good deal; and I asked him, as he entrusted the whole affair to me, in whose name I should take the receipt.

“Not in mine, my good friend. They wanted me to become an executor, which I, yesterday, wrote to decline; and I am informed that if the receipt were in my name it would constitute me an executor in the eye of the law, and fix me in that position. Take it, pray, if you have no objection, in your own name.”

This, accordingly, I did.

“You will see, by-and-by, why I am obliged to mention all these particulars.”

===Chinese===
「这位可怜的先生，我生平统共没见过两面，」伯爵说，「可这桩差事，既然他再没有别的亲眷，虽说不情愿，我实在推辞不得。所以我要亲自到办事处去，把簿册签了，文书登了。可是又有一桩苦处：倒霉，我扭伤了拇指，往后一个礼拜签不得自己的名字。好在签谁的名字都是一样。您的同我的一样。既然先生肯赏光陪我走一遭，一切就都顺了。」

我们驱车而去。伯爵交给我一份备忘，写着死者的教名和姓氏、年龄、死因，以及照例应载的项目；还有一份说明，写明墓穴的确切位置——那墓的尺寸也有描述，是寻常朴素的一种——要挖在圣阿芒家（St. Amand）两座墓窖之间。丧仪据载将于（后天夜里）凌晨一点半抵达；他又把款项交到我手里，含着加倍的规费，为的是夜间下葬。数目不小；他把整个事情都托付给了我，我便问他，收据该开在谁的名下。

「不要开我的，我的好朋友。他们本要我做遗嘱执行人，我昨天已去信辞了；而且听说，收据若开了我的名字，在法律眼里就等于立我为执行人，把我就钉在那个位子上了。请您行个好，若不见怪，就开您自己的名字。」

我于是照办了。

「往后您自会明白，我为什么不得不把这些细节一一交代。」

===Original===
The Count, meanwhile, was leaning back in the carriage, with his black silk muffler up to his nose, and his hat shading his eyes, while he dozed in his corner; in which state I found him on my return.

Paris had lost its charm for me. I hurried through the little business I had to do, longed once more for my quiet room in the Dragon Volant, the melancholy woods of the Château de la Carque, and the tumultuous and thrilling influence of proximity to the object of my wild but wicked romance.

I was delayed some time by my stockbroker. I had a very large sum, as I told you, at my banker’s, uninvested. I cared very little for a few days’ interest﻿—very little for the entire sum, compared with the image that occupied my thoughts, and beckoned me with a white arm, through the dark, toward the spreading lime-trees and chestnuts of the Château de la Carque. But I had fixed this day to meet him, and was relieved when he told me that I had better let it lie in my banker’s hands for a few days longer, as the funds would certainly fall immediately. This accident, too, was not without its immediate bearing on my subsequent adventures.

===Chinese===
一路上，伯爵仰在车厢里，黑绸围巾一直掩到鼻子，帽子遮着眼睛，缩在角落里打盹；我回来时，他还是这副模样。

巴黎于我已失了魅力。我匆匆办完手头那几件小事，一心只盼回到飞龙旅馆那间安静的屋子，回到拉卡尔克城堡愁惨的树林，回到我那桩狂热而不光彩的罗曼司的对象近旁——那份近在咫尺的、骚动而令人战栗的魔力。

证券经纪人又耽搁了我一阵。如我告诉过你的，我在银行里存着一大笔款子，尚未投资。几天的利息，我全不放在心上——同占据我心头的那幅影像相比，整笔款子又算得了什么；那影像正伸出雪白的手臂，穿过黑暗，朝着拉卡尔克城堡舒展的菩提树和栗树向我招手。可我约的正是今天同他见面；他对我说，款项不妨再在银行里搁上几天，因为公债行情眼看要跌——这话叫我松了口气。这桩小小的意外，日后同我的奇遇也正有着直接的干系。

===Original===
When I reached the Dragon Volant, I found, in my sitting-room, a good deal to my chagrin, my two guests, whom I had quite forgotten. I inwardly cursed my own stupidity for having embarrassed myself with their agreeable society. It could not be helped now, however, and a word to the waiters put all things in train for dinner.

Tom Whistlewick was in great force; and he commenced almost immediately with a very odd story.

He told me that not only Versailles, but all Paris, was in a ferment, in consequence of a revolting, and all but sacrilegious, practical joke, played off on the night before.

The pagoda, as he persisted in calling the palanquin, had been left standing on the spot where we last saw it. Neither conjuror, nor usher, nor bearers had ever returned. When the ball closed, and the company at length retired, the servants who attended to put out the lights, and secure the doors, found it still there.

It was determined, however, to let it stand where it was until next morning, by which time, it was conjectured, its owners would send messengers to remove it.

===Chinese===
回到飞龙旅馆，我在起居室里发现了我的两位客人——着实懊恼，我竟把他们忘了个干净。我心里暗骂自己糊涂，竟让这两位好同伴搅了局。事已至此，无法可想，吩咐侍者一声，晚饭诸事便都安排停当。

汤姆·惠斯尔威克精神大振，几乎立刻就讲起一桩天大的怪事。

他告诉我，不但凡尔赛，连整个巴黎都沸腾了，为的是前一夜有人开了一个骇人听闻、几近亵渎的恶作剧。

那座「宝塔」——他咬定了要这么称呼那顶轿辇——一直搁在我们最后见到它的地方。术士也好，引座人也好，轿夫也好，一个都没有回来。舞会散场、宾客终于尽退之后，留下来熄灯锁门的仆役们，发现它还在那里。

于是决定，姑且让它立在原处，等第二天早晨；料想届时主人自会打发人来搬走。

===Original===
None arrived. The servants were then ordered to take it away; and its extraordinary weight, for the first time, reminded them of its forgotten human occupant. Its door was forced; and, judge what was their disgust, when they discovered, not a living man, but a corpse! Three or four days must have passed since the death of the burly man in the Chinese tunic and painted cap. Some people thought it was a trick designed to insult the Allies, in whose honour the ball was got up. Others were of opinion that it was nothing worse than a daring and cynical jocularity which, shocking as it was, might yet be forgiven to the high spirits and irrepressible buffoonery of youth. Others, again, fewer in number, and mystically given, insisted that the corpse was bona fide necessary to the exhibition, and that the disclosures and allusions which had astonished so many people were distinctly due to necromancy.

===Chinese===
谁也没来。仆役们这才奉命把它抬走；它那出奇的沉重，头一回叫人想起里面那位被遗忘了的活人。门被撬开了；想想他们恶心到什么地步——里面不是活人，而是一具死尸！那个穿中国袍、戴彩绘帽的魁梧汉子，死了总有三四天了。有人以为，这是一个存心侮辱同盟国的把戏——这场舞会本是为向同盟国致敬而办的。另一些人认为，这不过是放肆而玩世不恭的一手恶谑，虽则骇人，念在年轻人的血气和压不住的浑劲儿，尚可原谅。还有一些人，数目更少，心思偏向神秘一途，一口咬定这具尸体于这场展演实实在在（bona fide）必不可少，那些叫无数人惊诧的揭底和暗语，分明出自死灵法术。

===Original===
“The matter, however, is now in the hands of the police,” observed Monsieur Carmaignac, “and we are not the body we were two or three months ago, if the offenders against propriety and public feeling are not traced, and convicted, unless, indeed, they have been a great deal more cunning than such fools generally are.”

I was thinking within myself how utterly inexplicable was my colloquy with the conjuror, so cavalierly dismissed by Monsieur Carmaignac as a “fool;” and the more I thought the more marvellous it seemed.

“It certainly was an original joke, though not a very clear one,” said Whistlewick.

“Not even original,” said Carmaignac. “Very nearly the same thing was done, a hundred years ago or more, at a state ball in Paris; and the rascals who played the trick were never found out.”

In this Monsieur Carmaignac, as I afterwards discovered, spoke truly; for, among my books of French anecdote and memoirs, the very incident is marked, by my own hand.

While we were thus talking, the waiter told us that dinner was served; and we withdrew accordingly; my guests more than making amends for my comparative taciturnity.

===Chinese===
「不过，这案子如今已到警察手里，」卡马尼亚克先生说，「我们也不是两三个月前那个班子了；伤风败俗、搅扰公愤的家伙，若不能查出来定罪——除非他们比这类蠢货通常的情形狡猾得多。」

我心里正想着，我同那术士的一场对答，究竟如何地无从解释——卡马尼亚克先生却轻飘飘一句「蠢货」打发了；我越想越觉得神妙。

「这玩笑确属别开生面，只是不大明白。」惠斯尔威克说。

「连别开生面也谈不上，」卡马尼亚克说。「一百多年前，巴黎的一场宫廷舞会上，就有人干过几乎一模一样的事；那帮捣鬼的流氓，始终没有查出来。」

这一点，卡马尼亚克先生说的，我后来发觉属实；因为在我那些法国掌故和回忆录的书里，恰好记着这桩逸事，还是我亲手做的记号。

我们正这么说着，侍者来禀报晚饭已齐；我们便入席；两位客人的健谈，绰绰有余地补足了我相对的沉默。

## XVIII The Churchyard / XVIII 教堂墓地

===Original===
Our dinner was really good, so were the wines; better, perhaps, at this out-of-the-way inn, than at some of the more pretentious hotels in Paris. The moral effect of a really good dinner is immense﻿—we all felt it. The serenity and good nature that follow are more solid and comfortable than the tumultuous benevolences of Bacchus.

My friends were happy, therefore, and very chatty; which latter relieved me of the trouble of talking, and prompted them to entertain me and one another incessantly with agreeable stories and conversation, of which, until suddenly a subject emerged, which interested me powerfully, I confess, so much were my thoughts engaged elsewhere, I heard next to nothing.

===Chinese===
这顿晚饭实在精美，酒也是；在这家僻静的客栈里，恐怕比巴黎某些更讲究的大旅馆还要好些。一顿真正好饭的道德效用是巨大的——我们都感觉到了。饭后随之而来的安详与和气，比酒神那阵喧腾的善心更扎实、更受用。

朋友们于是兴致极高，话也极多；后者省了我说话的力气，只听凭他们讲个不停，用动听的故事和闲谈款待我，也款待彼此；直到忽然冒出一个叫我大感兴趣的话头为止——我承认，在那之前，我的心事全在别处，几乎一个字也没听进去。

===Original===
“Yes,” said Carmaignac, continuing a conversation which had escaped me, “there was another case, beside that Russian nobleman, odder still. I remembered it this morning, but cannot recall the name. He was a tenant of the very same room. By the by, Monsieur, might it not be as well,” he added, turning to me, with a laugh, half joke whole earnest, as they say, “if you were to get into another apartment, now that the house is no longer crowded? that is, if you mean to make any stay here.”

“A thousand thanks! no. I’m thinking of changing my hotel; and I can run into town so easily at night; and though I stay here, for this night, at least, I don’t expect to vanish like those others. But you say there is another adventure, of the same kind, connected with the same room. Do let us hear it. But take some wine first.”

The story he told was curious.

“It happened,” said Carmaignac, “as well as I recollect, before either of the other cases. A French gentleman﻿—I wish I could remember his name﻿—the son of a merchant, came to this inn (the Dragon Volant), and was put by the landlord into the same room of which we have been speaking. *Your* apartment, Monsieur. He was by no means young﻿—past forty﻿—and very far from good-looking. The people here said that he was the ugliest man, and the most good-natured, that ever lived. He played on the fiddle, sang, and wrote poetry. His habits were odd and desultory. He would sometimes sit all day in his room writing, singing, and fiddling, and go out at night for a walk. An eccentric man! He was by no means a millionaire, but he had a *modicum bonum* you understand﻿—a trifle more than half a million of francs. He consulted his stockbroker about investing this money in foreign stocks, and drew the entire sum from his banker. You now have the situation of affairs when the catastrophe occurred.”

“Pray fill your glass,” I said.

“Dutch courage, Monsieur, to face the catastrophe!” said Whistlewick, filling his own.

===Chinese===
「是的，」卡马尼亚克说，接着一段我没听着的谈话讲下去，「除了那位俄国贵族，还有一桩案子，更怪。我今天早上想起来了，只是记不起名字。他也是那间屋子的房客。对了，先生，」他转向我，笑着添了一句——正如人家说的，半是玩笑，全当正经，「如今店里既然不再挤了，您要不要换间房？——如果您打算在这里多住的话。」

「多谢，多谢！不必了。我正打算换个旅馆呢；夜里进城也便当；今晚我固然还要在这儿住——至少这一夜——可我不指望自己也像那几位一样失踪。不过你说还有一桩同类的奇事，也出在同一间屋里。快讲给我们听吧。不过先喝口酒。」

他讲的故事确实离奇。

「事情发生，」卡马尼亚克说，「据我记忆所及，还在前两桩案子之前。一位法国绅士——可惜我想不起他的名字——一个商人的儿子，来到这家客栈（飞龙旅馆），被店主安排进我们方才说的那间屋子。*您*住的那间，先生。他绝不年轻——四十开外——而且离好看极远。这里的人说，他是天下最丑的男人，也是天下脾气最好的男人。他拉小提琴，唱歌，还作诗。他的习性古怪而散漫。有时他会整日关在屋里，写字、唱歌、拉琴，到夜里才出门散步。一个怪人！他绝不是百万富翁，可他有一笔『可观的薄财』（modicum bonum），您懂的——五十几万法郎。他找证券经纪人商量，要把这笔钱投到外国证券上去，便从银行把全数提了出来。惨事发生时的情形，您现在知道了。」

「请把杯子斟满。」我说。

「借酒壮胆，先生，好对付那场大祸！」惠斯尔威克说着，斟满了自己的。

===Original===
“Now, that was the last that ever was heard of his money,” resumed Carmaignac. “You shall hear about himself. The night after this financial operation, he was seized with a poetic frenzy; he sent for the then landlord of this house, and told him that he long meditated an epic, and meant to commence that night, and that he was on no account to be disturbed until nine o’clock in the morning. He had two pairs of wax candles, a little cold supper on a side-table, his desk open, paper enough upon it to contain the entire *Henriade*, and a proportionate store of pens and ink.

“Seated at this desk he was seen by the waiter who brought him a cup of coffee at nine o’clock, at which time the intruder said he was writing fast enough to set fire to the paper﻿—that was his phrase; he did not look up, he appeared too much engrossed. But, when the waiter came back, half an hour afterwards, the door was locked; and the poet, from within, answered, that he must not be disturbed.

“Away went the garçon; and next morning at nine o’clock knocked at his door, and receiving no answer, looked through the keyhole; the lights were still burning, the window-shutters were closed as he had left them; he renewed his knocking, knocked louder, no answer came. He reported this continued and alarming silence to the innkeeper, who, finding that his guest had not left his key in the lock, succeeded in finding another that opened it. The candles were just giving up the ghost in their sockets, but there was light enough to ascertain that the tenant of the room was gone! The bed had not been disturbed; the window-shutter was barred. He must have let himself out, and, locking the door on the outside, put the key in his pocket, and so made his way out of the house. Here, however, was another difficulty, the Dragon Volant shut its doors and made all fast at twelve o’clock; after that hour no one could leave the house, except by obtaining the key and letting himself out, and of necessity leaving the door unsecured, or else by collusion and aid of some person in the house.

“Now it happened that, some time after the doors were secured, at half-past twelve, a servant who had not been apprized of his order to be left undisturbed, seeing a light shine through the keyhole, knocked at the door to inquire whether the poet wanted anything. He was very little obliged to his disturber, and dismissed him with a renewed charge that he was not to be interrupted again during the night. This incident established the fact that he was in the house after the doors had been locked and barred. The innkeeper himself kept the keys, and swore that he found them hung on the wall above his head, in his bed, in their usual place, in the morning; and that nobody could have taken them away without awakening him. That was all we could discover. The Count de St. Alyre, to whom this house belongs, was very active and very much chagrined. But nothing was discovered.”

===Chinese===
「好，他的钱从此音讯全无，」卡马尼亚克接着说，「他本人的事您听着。这场金钱交易之后的当夜，他诗兴大发；把那时这店的店主请了来，告诉他，说自己久想写一部史诗，就打算当夜动笔，明早九点以前，无论如何不许打扰。他备下两对蜡烛，一张小桌上摆了冷夜宵，书桌开着，桌上的纸足够誊完一整部《亨利亚特》（Henriade），笔墨也备得相称充足。

「九点钟，侍者给他端咖啡去，见他正坐在桌前；那位不速之客说，他写得飞快，快得能把纸点着——这是他的原话；他没有抬头，一副全神贯注的样子。可是半小时后侍者回来时，门锁上了；诗人在里面应声说，不许打扰。

「伙计去了；第二天早晨九点来敲门，无人应声，便从钥匙孔往里看：烛还亮着，窗板照他离开时那样关着；他又敲，敲得更响，还是没有回音。他把这持续而骇人的寂静报告了店主；店主见客人的钥匙没有留在锁上，另寻了一把，居然把门打开了。蜡烛正在烛台上咽气，可光线还够看清：房客不见了！床没有动过；窗板闩着。他一定是自己出去，从外面锁了门，把钥匙揣进衣袋，就这样离开了这所宅子。可这里又有一重难处：飞龙旅馆十二点锁门闭户，一切上闩；过了那个时辰，谁也别想出这宅子，除非拿到钥匙自己放自己出去——那门就势必不上闩——否则就得同宅子里什么人串通，得其相助。

「偏巧，门锁好之后不久，十二点半光景，一个不晓得他吩咐过不许打扰的仆人，看见钥匙孔里透出灯光，便去敲门，问诗人先生要不要什么。诗人对搅扰他的人很不领情，把他打发走，又郑重叮嘱，夜里再不许来打搅。这件事坐实了：锁门上闩之后，他还在宅子里。钥匙由店主亲自保管；他赌咒说，早晨钥匙还挂在床头墙壁上老地方；谁要想拿走，非惊醒他不可。我们查明的就这么多。这座宅子的主人圣阿利尔伯爵，奔走得很卖力，也懊恼得很。可什么也没有查出来。」

===Original===
“And nothing heard since of the epic poet?” I asked.

“Nothing﻿—not the slightest clue﻿—he never turned up again. I suppose he is dead; if he is not, he must have got into some devilish bad scrape, of which we have heard nothing, that compelled him to abscond with all the secrecy and expedition in his power. All that we know for certain is that, having occupied the room in which you sleep, he vanished, nobody ever knew how, and never was heard of since.”

“You have now mentioned three cases,” I said, “and all from the same room.”

“Three. Yes, all equally unintelligible. When men are murdered, the great and immediate difficulty the assassins encounter is how to conceal the body. It is very hard to believe that three persons should have been consecutively murdered, in the same room, and their bodies so effectually disposed of that no trace of them was ever discovered.”

From this we passed to other topics, and the grave Monsieur Carmaignac amused us with a perfectly prodigious collection of scandalous anecdote, which his opportunities in the police department had enabled him to accumulate.

===Chinese===
「那位史诗诗人，后来也毫无音信？」我问。

「毫无——连一丝线索也没有——他再没有露过面。我想他是死了；若没有死，那他一定是陷进了什么糟糕透顶而我们毫无所闻的烂事，逼得他竭尽所能地隐姓埋名、仓皇远遁。我们确知的只有一条：他住过您睡觉的那间屋子，然后失踪了，没人知道怎么失的踪，从此再无音信。」

「你如今讲了三桩案子了，」我说，「而且都出在同一间屋。」

「三桩。是的，全都同样无从索解。人被谋害了，凶手当下最大的难处，是如何藏尸。很难相信三个人接连在同一间屋里被杀，尸首又处理得那样干净，连一点痕迹都不曾留下。」

由此我们转到别的话头，严肃的卡马尼亚克先生拿出一大堆骇人听闻的秽闻轶事来给我们下酒——那全亏他在警务部门的机会，才积攒得起来，数量着实惊人。

===Original===
My guests happily had engagements in Paris, and left me about ten.

I went up to my room, and looked out upon the grounds of the Château de la Carque. The moonlight was broken by clouds, and the view of the park in this desultory light, acquired a melancholy and fantastic character.

The strange anecdotes recounted of the room in which I stood, by Monsieur Carmaignac, returned vaguely upon my mind, drowning in sudden shadows the gaiety of the more frivolous stories with which he had followed them. I looked round me on the room that lay in ominous gloom, with an almost disagreeable sensation. I took my pistols now with an undefined apprehension that they might be really needed before my return tonight. This feeling, be it understood, in nowise chilled my ardour. Never had my enthusiasm mounted higher. My adventure absorbed and carried me away; but it added a strange and stern excitement to the expedition.

===Chinese===
幸好两位客人在巴黎都有约，十点来钟便告辞了。

我上楼回房，朝拉卡尔克城堡的园林望去。月光被云割碎，园中的景色在这种断断续续的光里，平添一种愁惨而光怪陆离的气象。

卡马尼亚克先生讲述的、关于我立身这间屋子的种种怪谈，模模糊糊地涌回我心头，把他随后那些轻浮故事带来的欢乐，一下子沉进了阴影里。我环顾这间笼罩在不祥幽暗里的屋子，心头掠过一阵几乎算得上不快的滋味。这时我拿上手枪，怀着一种说不清的预感：今夜归来之前，它们也许当真用得着。这感觉——请务必明白——丝毫没有冷却我的热望。我的兴致从没有这样高过。这场奇遇吞没了我，携着我向前；只是给这趟夜行添了一种奇异的、冷峻的刺激。

===Original===
I loitered for a time in my room. I had ascertained the exact point at which the little churchyard lay. It was about a mile away; I did not wish to reach it earlier than necessary.

I stole quietly out, and sauntered along the road to my left, and thence entered a narrower track, still to my left, which, skirting the park wall, and describing a circuitous route, all the way, under grand old trees, passes the ancient cemetery. That cemetery is embowered in trees, and occupies little more than half an acre of ground, to the left of the road, interposing between it and the park of the Château de la Carque.

Here, at this haunted spot, I paused and listened. The place was utterly silent. A thick cloud had darkened the moon, so that I could distinguish little more than the outlines of near objects, and that vaguely enough; and sometimes, as it were, floating in black fog, the white surface of a tombstone emerged.

Among the forms that met my eye against the iron-grey of the horizon, were some of those shrubs or trees that grow like our junipers, some six feet high, in form like a miniature poplar, with the darker foliage of the yew. I do not know the name of the plant, but I have often seen it in such funereal places.

===Chinese===
我在房里消磨了一阵。小教堂墓地的确切位置我已探明。大约一英里开外；我不想到得比需要的更早。

我悄悄溜出去，沿左手边的大路信步走去，又折进左边一条更窄的小径；那小径贴着园林的墙，一路绕着弯，头顶尽是高大古老的树，从那古墓园边上经过。墓园隐在树丛里，占地不过半英亩挂零，在大路左手，介乎大路同拉卡尔克城堡的园林之间。

在这处闹鬼的地方，我停步倾听。四下死寂。一片浓云遮暗了月亮，我辨得出的，不过是近处东西的轮廓，而且影影绰绰；时而，仿佛浮在黑雾里似的，一方墓碑的白面探了出来。

在我眼里映着的、衬在铁灰色天际上的种种形影中间，有几丛灌木，或者说小树，长得像我们的刺柏，约六英尺高，形状像一株小小的白杨，叶子却比紫杉还深。这植物叫什么，我不知道，可我常在这种丧葬的地方见到它。

===Original===
Knowing that I was a little too early, I sat down upon the edge of a tombstone to wait, as, for aught I knew, the beautiful Countess might have wise reasons for not caring that I should enter the grounds of the château earlier than she had appointed. In the listless state induced by waiting, I sat there, with my eyes on the object straight before me, which chanced to be that faint black outline I have described. It was right before me, about half-a-dozen steps away.

The moon now began to escape from under the skirt of the cloud that had hid her face for so long; and, as the light gradually improved, the tree on which I had been lazily staring began to take a new shape. It was no longer a tree, but a man standing motionless. Brighter and brighter grew the moonlight, clearer and clearer the image became, and at last stood out perfectly distinctly. It was Colonel Gaillarde.

Luckily, he was not looking toward me. I could only see him in profile; but there was no mistaking the white moustache, the farouche visage, and the gaunt six-foot stature. There he was, his shoulder toward me, listening and watching, plainly, for some signal or person expected, straight in front of him.

If he were, by chance, to turn his eyes in my direction, I knew that I must reckon upon an instantaneous renewal of the combat only commenced in the hall of the Belle Étoile. In any case, could malignant fortune have posted, at this place and hour, a more dangerous watcher? What ecstasy to him, by a single discovery, to hit me so hard, and blast the Countess de St. Alyre, whom he seemed to hate.

===Chinese===
知道自己来得略早，我便在一方墓碑边上坐下等候；因为，据我所知，美丽的伯爵夫人或许自有她的道理，不愿我早于她指定的时辰踏进城堡的园地。在等候生出的倦怠里，我就那样坐着，眼睛望着正前方的那个东西——恰好就是我描写过的那道淡淡的黑影。它正在我面前，约莫六步开外。

这时，月亮开始从那片久久遮住她脸的云裙底下钻出来；光线渐渐亮起来，我懒懒盯着的那棵树开始变了形状。它不再是树，而是一个伫立不动的人。月光越来越亮，影像越来越清晰，最后完全分明地立了出来。是盖亚尔上校。

幸好他没朝我这边望。我只看得见他的侧影；可那白胡子、那凶悍的面孔、那六英尺的瘦长身量，错不了。他就在那里，肩膀朝着我，侧耳听着、盯着，明明白白在等什么信号或什么人，就在他正前方。

万一他碰巧把眼睛转向我这边，我知道，美星旅馆门厅里那场才开了个头的战斗，势必立时重开。况且，恶运岂能把一个更危险的监视者，恰恰安插在这个地点、这个时辰？只需一个发现，就能把我打个半死，又把圣阿利尔伯爵夫人——他似乎恨透了她——毁个干净，这对他是何等的狂喜。

===Original===
He raised his arm; he whistled softly; I heard an answering whistle as low; and, to my relief, the Colonel advanced in the direction of this sound, widening the distance between us at every step; and immediately I heard talking, but in a low and cautious key.

I recognized, I thought, even so, the peculiar voice of Gaillarde.

I stole softly forward in the direction in which those sounds were audible. In doing so, I had, of course, to use the extremest caution.

I thought I saw a hat above a jagged piece of ruined wall, and then a second﻿—yes, I saw two hats conversing; the voices came from under them. They moved off, not in the direction of the park, but of the road, and I lay along the grass, peeping over a grave, as a skirmisher might, observing the enemy. One after the other, the figures emerged full into view as they mounted the stile at the roadside. The Colonel, who was last, stood on the wall for awhile, looking about him, and then jumped down on the road. I heard their steps and talk as they moved away together, with their backs toward me, in the direction which led them farther and farther from the Dragon Volant.

===Chinese===
他抬起手臂，轻轻打了一声呼哨；我听见一声同样低的哨音应答；叫我松快的是，上校朝那声音的方向迎了上去，一步一步拉开我们之间的距离；随即我听见说话声，压得又低又小心。

即便这样，我想我还是听出了盖亚尔那副特别的嗓音。

我朝声音传来的方向轻轻挪过去。这样走，自然得打起十二分的小心。

我仿佛看见一堵残墙的锯齿上头有一顶帽子，接着又是第二顶——是的，两顶帽子在交谈；声音就从帽子底下出来。他们走开了，不是朝园林的方向，而是朝大路的方向；我伏在草上，从一座坟后探望，像个散兵侦察敌情似的。一个接一个，两个人影跨上路边的踏级时，全身显了出来。走在后头的上校，在墙头立了片刻，四下张望，然后跳下大路。我听着他们的脚步和谈话结伴远去，他们背朝着我，走的方向离飞龙旅馆越来越远。

===Original===
I waited until these sounds were quite lost in distance before I entered the park. I followed the instructions I had received from the Countess de St. Alyre, and made my way among brushwood and thickets to the point nearest the ruinous temple, and crossed the short intervening space of open ground rapidly.

I was now once more under the gigantic boughs of the old lime and chestnut trees; softly, and with a heart throbbing fast, I approached the little structure.

The moon was now shining steadily, pouring down its radiance on the soft foliage, and here and there mottling the verdure under my feet.

I reached the steps; I was among its worn marble shafts. She was not there, nor in the inner sanctuary, the arched windows of which were screened almost entirely by masses of ivy. The lady had not yet arrived.

===Chinese===
我等到那些声响完全消失在远处，才进园林。我遵照圣阿利尔伯爵夫人的嘱咐，从灌木和树丛间穿行，走到离那座破败神殿最近的地方，疾步掠过当中那一小段开阔地。

我又一次置身在老菩提树和老栗树的巨枝底下；我放轻脚步，心口怦怦地跳着，挨近那座小小的建筑。

这时月光稳定地照下来，清辉泻在柔嫩的叶子上，也把我脚下的绿茵点染得斑斑驳驳。

我走到台阶前，置身在那些磨旧的石柱之间。她不在那里，内殿里也没有——那殿的拱窗几乎全被大团大团的常春藤遮住了。女士还没有到。

## XIX The Key / XIX 钥匙

===Original===
I stood now upon the steps, watching and listening. In a minute or two I heard the crackle of withered sticks trod upon, and, looking in the direction, I saw a figure approaching among the trees, wrapped in a mantle.

I advanced eagerly. It was the Countess. She did not speak, but gave me her hand, and I led her to the scene of our last interview. She repressed the ardour of my impassioned greeting with a gentle but peremptory firmness. She removed her hood, shook back her beautiful hair, and, gazing on me with sad and glowing eyes, sighed deeply. Some awful thought seemed to weigh upon her.

“Richard, I must speak plainly. The crisis of my life has come. I am sure you would defend me. I think you pity me; perhaps you even love me.”

At these words I became eloquent, as young madmen in my plight do. She silenced me, however, with the same melancholy firmness.

===Chinese===
我此刻站在台阶上，观望着，聆听着。过了一两分钟，我听见踩断枯枝的脆响，循声望去，只见树影之间有个披着斗篷的身影正走近来。

我急切地迎上前去。是伯爵夫人。她没有说话，只把手伸给我，我便领她到我们上次相见的所在。她以一种温柔却不容分说的坚定，按住了我热烈问候的冲动。她掀开兜帽，甩开一头秀发，用忧伤而灼灼的目光凝视着我，深深叹了口气。似乎有什么可怕的念头沉甸甸压在她心头。

"理查德，我必须把话说透。我人生的危急关头到了。我确信你会护着我。我想你是怜惜我的；说不定，你甚至爱我。"

听了这话，我顿时变得雄辩滔滔，凡处在我这般境地的年轻疯子都是如此。然而她带着同样忧郁的坚定，止住了我的话头。

===Original===
“Listen, dear friend, and then say whether you can aid me. How madly I am trusting you; and yet my heart tells me how wisely! To meet you here as I do—what insanity it seems! How poorly you must think of me! But when you know all, you will judge me fairly. Without your aid I cannot accomplish my purpose. That purpose unaccomplished, I must die.
