I am chained to a man whom I despise—whom I abhor. I have resolved to fly. I have jewels, principally diamonds, for which I am offered thirty thousand pounds of your English money. They are my separate property by my marriage settlement; I will take them with me. You are a judge, no doubt, of jewels. I was counting mine when the hour came, and brought this in my hand to show you. Look.”

“It is magnificent!” I exclaimed, as a collar of diamonds twinkled and flashed in the moonlight, suspended from her pretty fingers. I thought, even at that tragic moment, that she prolonged the show, with a feminine delight in these brilliant toys.

===Chinese===
"听着，亲爱的朋友，然后告诉我你能否帮我。我这是何等疯狂地信任着你；可我的心又告诉我，这信任是何等明智！像我这样跑到这里来见你——看来是何等的癫狂！你心里该把我看得多么不庄重！可等你知道了全部，你会公正地评判我的。没有你的帮助，我无法达成我的心愿。心愿达不成，我就只有一死。我被锁链拴在一个我所鄙夷的男人身上——一个我所憎恶的男人身上。我已决意出逃。我有珠宝，主要是钻石，有人肯出三万英镑你们英国的钱来买。凭我的婚约，它们是我的特有财产；我要随身带走。你无疑是鉴赏珠宝的行家。约定的钟点到来时我正在清点我的珠宝，便顺手带了这一件在手里，来给你看。瞧。"

"真是华美夺目！"我叹道。一串钻石项圈从她纤美的指间垂下，在月光里闪烁生辉。即便在那样一个悲壮的时刻，我心里还想着：她把这场展示拉长了，对这些闪亮的玩物怀着一种属于女人的欢悦。

===Original===
“Yes,” she said, “I shall part with them all. I will turn them into money, and break, forever, the unnatural and wicked bonds that tied me, in the name of a sacrament, to a tyrant. A man young, handsome, generous, brave as you, can hardly be rich. Richard, you say you love me; you shall share all this with me. We will fly together to Switzerland; we will evade pursuit; my powerful friends will intervene and arrange a separation; and I shall, at length, be happy and reward my hero.”

You may suppose the style, florid and vehement, in which I poured forth my gratitude, vowed the devotion of my life, and placed myself absolutely at her disposal.

“Tomorrow night,” she said, “my husband will attend the remains of his cousin, Monsieur de St. Amand, to Père la Chaise. The hearse, he says, will leave this at half-past nine. You must be here, where we stand, at nine o’clock.”

I promised punctual obedience.

===Chinese===
"是的，"她说，"我要同它们尽数作别。我要把它们变成钱，永远斩断那以圣事之名把我缚在暴君身上的悖逆而邪恶的枷锁。一个像你这样年轻、英俊、慷慨、勇敢的人，是很难有钱的。理查德，你说你爱我；你就同我分享这一切吧。我们一起逃往瑞士；我们会躲开追捕；我有权势的朋友们会出面斡旋，安排分居；我终于会得到幸福，并酬报我的英雄。"

你可以想见我用怎样的辞藻——浓艳而激越——倾吐我的感激，起誓终身效忠，把自己全然交由她支配。

"明晚，"她说，"我丈夫要送他表兄德·圣阿芒先生的遗体去拉雪兹神父公墓。他说灵车九点半从这里出发。九点整，你必须到这儿来，就站在我们现在站的地方。"

我应承一定准时遵命。

===Original===
“I will not meet you here; but you see a red light in the window of the tower at that angle of the château?”

I assented.

“I placed it there, that, tomorrow night, when it comes, you may recognize it. So soon as that rose-coloured light appears at that window, it will be a signal to you that the funeral has left the château, and that you may approach safely. Come, then, to that window; I will open it, and admit you. Five minutes after a travelling-carriage, with four horses, shall stand ready in the porte-cochère. I will place my diamonds in your hands; and so soon as we enter the carriage, our flight commences. We shall have at least five hours’ start; and with energy, stratagem, and resource, I fear nothing. Are you ready to undertake all this for my sake?”

Again I vowed myself her slave.

“My only difficulty,” she said, “is how we shall quickly enough convert my diamonds into money; I dare not remove them while my husband is in the house.”

===Chinese===
"我不在这儿见你；你看得见城堡那一角的塔楼窗里透着一点红光么？"

我表示看到了。

"是我放在那儿的，好叫明晚灯亮起来的时候你认得它。那道玫瑰色的光一旦出现在那扇窗前，便是给你的信号：送葬的行列已离开城堡，你可以放心走近。那时你就到那扇窗下去；我会打开窗，放你进来。五分钟后，一辆四马旅行马车会在大门车廊里备好。我会把钻石交到你手上；我们一登上马车，便即刻启程逃亡。我们至少能抢先五个钟头；只要有魄力、有计谋、有手段，我什么都不怕。为了我，你愿意担起这一切吗？"

我再度起誓，甘为她的奴仆。

"我唯一的难处，"她说，"是如何把我的钻石足够快地换成钱；我丈夫在宅子里的时候，我不敢动它们。"

===Original===
Here was the opportunity I wished for. I now told her that I had in my banker’s hands no less a sum than thirty thousand pounds, with which, in the shape of gold and notes, I should come furnished, and thus the risk and loss of disposing of her diamonds in too much haste would be avoided.

“Good heaven!” she exclaimed, with a kind of disappointment. “You are rich, then? and I have lost the felicity of making my generous friend more happy. Be it so! since so it must be. Let us contribute, each, in equal shares, to our common fund. Bring you, your money; I, my jewels. There is a happiness to me even in mingling my resources with yours.”

On this there followed a romantic colloquy, all poetry and passion, such as I should, in vain, endeavour to reproduce.

Then came a very special instruction.

“I have come provided, too, with a key, the use of which I must explain.”

It was a double key—a long, slender stem, with a key at each end—one about the size which opens an ordinary room door; the other, as small, almost, as the key of a dressing-case.

===Chinese===
这正中我的下怀。我便告诉她，我存在银行家手里的款项足足有三万英镑之巨，届时我会带着金币和钞票前来，如此便可免掉她仓促变卖钻石的风险与折损。

"天哪！"她叫出声来，带着几分失望。"那么说你是富有的了？我竟失去了让我慷慨的朋友更加幸福的那份快乐。也罢！事已如此，只好这样。那就让我们对共同的资财各出相等的一份吧。你带上你的钱，我带上我的珠宝。把自己的财物同你的合在一处，这本身于我也是一种幸福。"

接着是一场罗曼蒂克的对话，通篇是诗与激情，我纵想复述也只是徒劳。

然后是一项极为特别的叮嘱。

"我此番还随身备了一把钥匙，它的用法我必须向你说明。"

那是一把双头钥匙——一根细长的杆，两头各有一个钥匙头——一头约莫是开普通房门用的那种大小；另一头几乎小得同梳妆匣的钥匙一样。

===Original===
“You cannot employ too much caution tomorrow night. An interruption would murder all my hopes. I have learned that you occupy the haunted room in the Dragon Volant. It is the very room I would have wished you in. I will tell you why—there is a story of a man who, having shut himself up in that room one night, disappeared before morning. The truth is, he wanted, I believe, to escape from creditors; and the host of the Dragon Volant, at that time, being a rogue, aided him in absconding. My husband investigated the matter, and discovered how his escape was made. It was by means of this key. Here is a memorandum and a plan describing how they are to be applied. I have taken them from the Count’s escritoire. And now, once more I must leave to your ingenuity how to mystify the people at the Dragon Volant. Be sure you try the keys first, to see that the locks turn freely. I will have my jewels ready. You, whatever we divide, had better bring your money, because it may be many months before you can revisit Paris, or disclose our place of residence to anyone; and our passports—arrange all that; in what names, and whither, you please. And now, dear Richard” (she leaned her arm fondly on my shoulder, and looked with ineffable passion in my eyes, with her other hand clasped in mine), “my very life is in your hands; I have staked all on your fidelity.”

As she spoke the last word, she, on a sudden, grew deadly pale, and gasped, “Good God! who is here?”

At the same moment she receded through the door in the marble screen, close to which she stood, and behind which was a small roofless chamber, as small as the shrine, the window of which was darkened by a clustering mass of ivy so dense that hardly a gleam of light came through the leaves.

===Chinese===
"明晚你怎样小心都不为过。一次打搅就会要了我全部希望的命。我听说你住的是飞龙旅馆那间闹鬼的房间。那正是我最愿意你住的房间。我告诉你为什么——有个传闻，说有个人夜里把自己关在那房间里，天亮之前就不见了。实情呢，我相信，是他想躲开债主；而飞龙旅馆当时的店主是个无赖，帮他逃走了。我丈夫查究过这桩案子，查出了他是怎么逃走的。靠的就是这把钥匙。这里有一份备忘录和一张图，说明它们如何施用。是我从伯爵的写字柜里取来的。还有，如何去迷惑飞龙旅馆那班人，就又要留给你的机变了。务必先试一试钥匙，确认锁芯转动灵光。我会把珠宝备好。你呢，无论我们如何分摊，最好把你的钱带来，因为也许要过好几个月你才能重返巴黎，或者把我们的住处透露给任何人；还有我们的护照——这些统统由你去安排，用什么名字、去什么地方，全凭你做主。好了，亲爱的理查德"（她深情地把一条手臂倚上我的肩头，另一只手与我十指相扣，眼里含着难以言说的激情望着我），"我的性命就攥在你手里；我把一切都押在了你的忠诚上。"

她最后一个字刚出口，突然间脸色煞白，倒抽一口气："天哪！谁在那儿？"

同一瞬间，她从大理石屏风上那道门里退了进去——她正站在门边，门后是一间没有屋顶的小室，同神龛一般狭小，窗子被一大丛密密匝匝的常春藤遮得严严实实，几乎透不进一丝光亮。

===Original===
I stood upon the threshold which she had just crossed, looking in the direction in which she had thrown that one terrified glance. No wonder she was frightened. Quite close upon us, not twenty yards away, and approaching at a quick step, very distinctly lighted by the moon, Colonel Gaillarde and his companion were coming. The shadow of the cornice and a piece of wall were upon me. Unconscious of this, I was expecting the moment when, with one of his frantic yells, he should spring forward to assail me.

I made a step backward, drew one of my pistols from my pocket, and cocked it. It was obvious he had not seen me.

I stood, with my finger on the trigger, determined to shoot him dead if he should attempt to enter the place where the Countess was. It would, no doubt, have been a murder; but, in my mind, I had no question or qualm about it. When once we engage in secret and guilty practices we are nearer other and greater crimes than we at all suspect.

“There’s the statue,” said the Colonel, in his brief discordant tones. “That’s the figure.”

“Alluded to in the stanzas?” inquired his companion.

“The very thing. We shall see more next time. Forward, Monsieur; let us march.”

===Chinese===
我站在她刚刚跨过的门槛上，朝她惊恐一瞥的方向望去。难怪她吓坏了。离我们极近，不出二十码远，盖亚尔上校正带着他的同伴快步走来，月光把他们照得清清楚楚。飞檐和一角墙的阴影正罩着我。他们对此毫无察觉；我却兀自等着他发出一声狂吼、纵身扑上来袭击我的那一刻。

我向后退了一步，从口袋里拔出一支手枪，扳开了枪机。显然，他没有看见我。

我手指扣着扳机站在那里，打定了主意：他若敢闯进伯爵夫人藏身的地方，就一枪毙了他。那无疑将是谋杀；可我心里对此没有半点疑问或不安。人一旦陷入隐秘而罪孽的勾当，离别的、更大的罪行，就比我们自己料想的近得多。

"雕像在那儿，"上校用他那简短刺耳的嗓音说，"就是那个像。"

"诗节里提到的那座？"他的同伴问。

"正是它。下回我们再看个仔细。开路，先生；走吧。"

===Original===
And, much to my relief, the gallant Colonel turned on his heel, and marched through the trees, with his back toward the château, striding over the grass, as I quickly saw, to the park wall, which they crossed not far from the gables of the Dragon Volant.

I found the Countess trembling in no affected, but a very real terror. She would not hear of my accompanying her toward the château. But I told her that I would prevent the return of the mad Colonel; and upon that point, at least, that she need fear nothing. She quickly recovered, again bid me a fond and lingering good night, and left me, gazing after her, with the key in my hand, and such a phantasmagoria floating in my brain as amounted very nearly to madness.

There was I, ready to brave all dangers, all right and reason, plunge into murder itself, on the first summons, and entangle myself in consequences inextricable and horrible (what cared I?) for a woman of whom I knew nothing, but that she was beautiful and reckless!

I have often thanked heaven for its mercy in conducting me through the labyrinths in which I had all but lost myself.

===Chinese===
令我大大松了一口气的是，这位勇武的上校转过脚跟，背朝城堡穿过树林，大步踏过草地——我很快看出——直奔园墙而去，就在离飞龙旅馆山墙不远处翻了出去。

我找到伯爵夫人时，她正抖个不停——不是做作的战栗，而是实实在在的惊怖。她不许我送她回城堡。但我告诉她，我会拦住那疯上校，不叫他折回来；至少在这一点上，她尽可放心。她很快定下神来，又同我缠绵不舍地互道了晚安，然后离我而去。我目送着她的背影，手里攥着那把钥匙，脑中浮动着的一连串幻影，简直与疯狂无异。

瞧瞧我吧：随时准备豁出一切危险、一切道义与理智，一声召唤便可投身谋杀之中，把自己缠进解不开的可怕后果里（我在乎什么呢？）——只为一个女人，一个除了美丽与不顾一切之外我一无所知的女人！

我后来常常感谢上天的仁慈，是它领着我走出了那座我几乎迷失其中的迷宫。

## XX A High-Cauld-Cap / XX 高顶睡帽

===Original===
I was now upon the road, within two or three hundred yards of the Dragon Volant. I had undertaken an adventure with a vengeance! And by way of prelude, there not improbably awaited me, at my inn, another encounter, perhaps, this time, not so lucky, with the grotesque sabreur.

I was glad I had my pistols. I certainly was bound by no law to allow a ruffian to cut me down, unresisting.

Stooping boughs from the old park, gigantic poplars on the other side, and the moonlight over all, made the narrow road to the inn-door picturesque.

I could not think very clearly just now; events were succeeding one another so rapidly, and I, involved in the action of a drama so extravagant and guilty, hardly knew myself or believed my own story, as I slowly paced towards the still open door of the Flying Dragon.

===Chinese===
这时我已上了大路，离飞龙旅馆不过两三百码。我这是结结实实地揽下了一场冒险！而作为序曲，旅馆里多半还等着我另一场遭遇——这回恐怕不会再那么走运了——对手正是那个怪模怪样的马刀客。

幸好我带着手枪。总没有任何一条法律要求我束手站立，任凭一个恶棍把我砍翻。

老园林里垂下的枝柯，路那边高耸入云的巨杨，再加上遍洒的月光，把通往旅馆门口的窄路映照得如画一般。

此刻我的思绪难以清明；事变一桩接着一桩来得太快，而我身陷一出如此荒唐而罪孽的戏剧之中，几乎认不得自己，也不敢相信自己亲历的故事，就这样缓步走向飞龙旅馆那扇尚未关闭的大门。

===Original===
No sign of the Colonel, visible or audible, was there. In the hall I inquired. No gentleman had arrived at the inn for the last half hour. I looked into the public room. It was deserted. The clock struck twelve, and I heard the servant barring the great door. I took my candle. The lights in this rural hostelry were by this time out, and the house had the air of one that had settled to slumber for many hours. The cold moonlight streamed in at the window on the landing, as I ascended the broad staircase; and I paused for a moment to look over the wooded grounds to the turreted château, to me, so full of interest. I bethought me, however, that prying eyes might read a meaning in this midnight gazing, and possibly the Count himself might, in his jealous mood, surmise a signal in this unwonted light in the stair-window of the Dragon Volant.

On opening my room door, with a little start, I met an extremely old woman with the longest face I ever saw; she had what used to be termed, a high-cauld-cap, on, the white border of which contrasted with her brown and yellow skin, and made her wrinkled face more ugly. She raised her curved shoulders, and looked up in my face, with eyes unnaturally black and bright.

“I have lighted a little wood, Monsieur, because the night is chill.”

I thanked her, but she did not go. She stood with her candle in her tremulous fingers.

“Excuse an old woman. Monsieur,” she said; “but what on earth can a young English *milord*, with all Paris at his feet, find to amuse him in the Dragon Volant?”

===Chinese===
上校的踪影，看不见也听不见。我在大厅里打听。过去半个钟头没有一位绅士到店。我朝公共休息室里张望，里面空无一人。时钟敲了十二下，我听见仆人在闩大门。我取了蜡烛。这乡间客栈的灯火此刻都已熄尽，整座房子一副已安睡了多个时辰的模样。我登上宽阔的楼梯时，清冷的月光从楼梯口的窗子里流泻进来；我停了片刻，越过林木森森的庭园，望向那座塔楼耸立的城堡——在我眼里，它是那样牵动人心。不过我随即想到，窥探的眼睛也许会从这午夜的凝望里读出深意，说不定善妒的伯爵本人就会疑心：飞龙旅馆楼梯窗口这盏反常的灯光是个信号。

打开房门时，我不由小小一怔：眼前是个极其老迈的妇人，脸长得我生平仅见；她头上戴着一种旧时所谓的高顶睡帽（high-cauld-cap），白色的帽边衬着她黄褐的皮肤，把她那张布满皱纹的脸衬得愈发丑陋。她耸起佝偻的肩，仰头直视着我的脸，一双眼睛黑亮得不自然。

"我给先生点了一小炉柴火，夜里凉。"

我道了谢，可她并不走。她站在那里，颤抖的手指间举着蜡烛。

"恕老婆子多嘴，先生，"她说，"一位年轻的英国爵爷（milord），整个巴黎都任他玩，到底能在飞龙旅馆寻着什么乐子呢？"

===Original===
Had I been at the age of fairy tales, and in daily intercourse with the delightful Countess d’Aulnois, I should have seen in this withered apparition, the genius loci, the malignant fairy, at the stamp of whose foot, the ill-fated tenants of this very room had, from time to time, vanished. I was past that, however; but the old woman’s dark eyes were fixed on mine, with a steady meaning that plainly told me that my secret was known. I was embarrassed and alarmed; I never thought of asking her what business that was of hers.

“These old eyes saw you in the park of the château tonight.”

“*I!*” I began, with all the scornful surprise I could affect.

“It avails nothing, Monsieur; I know why you stay here; and I tell you to begone. Leave this house tomorrow morning, and never come again.”

She lifted her disengaged hand, as she looked at me with intense horror in her eyes.

“There is nothing on earth—I don’t know what you mean,” I answered; “and why should you care about me?”

“I don’t care about you, Monsieur—I care about the honour of an ancient family, whom I served in their happier days, when to be noble, was to be honoured. But my words are thrown away, Monsieur; you are insolent. I will keep my secret, and you, yours; that is all. You will soon find it hard enough to divulge it.”

===Chinese===
倘若我还处在听童话的年纪，又天天同那位可人的奥努瓦伯爵夫人（Countess d'Aulnois）打交道，我准会把这个干瘪的幽灵认作此地的守护精怪（genius loci）、那害人的妖婆——她脚跟一顿，这间屋子里命途多舛的房客便会时不时地凭空消失。不过我早过了那个年纪；可老妇人的黑眼睛直盯着我，那目光里坚定的意味分明告诉我：我的秘密已被识破。我又窘又慌；却压根儿没想到要质问她，这与她何干。

"今晚在城堡园林里瞧见你的，就是这双老眼。"

"我！"我开口道，装出满副轻蔑的惊诧。

"装模作样没有用，先生；我知道你为什么赖在这里；我劝你走。明天一早离开这屋子，永远别再来。"

她举起空着的那只手，眼里带着极度的惊怖望着我。

"根本没有的事——我不明白你是什么意思，"我答道，"再说你何必操心我？"

"我不是操心你，先生——我操心的是一个古老家族的名誉。我在他们过好日子的时候伺候过他们，那时候，出身贵族还意味着受人敬重。可我的话是白说了，先生；你放肆。我守住我的秘密，你守好你的；就这么回事。用不了多久，你就会发现，想把它说出去都难了。"

===Original===
The old woman went slowly from the room and shut the door, before I had made up my mind to say anything. I was standing where she had left me, nearly five minutes later. The jealousy of Monsieur the Count, I assumed, appears to this old creature about the most terrible thing in creation. Whatever contempt I might entertain for the dangers which this old lady so darkly intimated, it was by no means pleasant, you may suppose, that a secret so dangerous should be so much as suspected by a stranger, and that stranger a partisan of the Count de St. Alyre.

Ought I not, at all risks, to apprize the Countess, who had trusted me so generously, or, as she said herself, so madly, of the fact that our secret was, at least, suspected by another? But was there not greater danger in attempting to communicate? What did the beldame mean by saying, “Keep your secret, and I’ll keep mine?”

I had a thousand distracting questions before me. My progress seemed like a journey through the Spessart, where at every step some new goblin or monster starts from the ground or steps from behind a tree.

===Chinese===
不等我拿定主意说什么，老妇人已缓缓走出房间，带上了门。将近五分钟后，我还站在她撂下我的地方。我暗自推断，伯爵先生的醋意，在这老东西眼里只怕是天地间顶顶可怕的事。不论我对这老妇人阴森森暗示的危险报以怎样的轻蔑，你可想而知：一个如此凶险的秘密，居然让一个外人起了疑，而且这外人还是圣阿利尔伯爵一边的人，这终究不是什么愉快的事。

我难道不该不顾一切风险，告知伯爵夫人——她如此慷慨地信任我，或如她自己所说，如此疯狂地信任我——我们的秘密至少已被旁人怀疑？可试图传信，岂不是风险更大？这老虔婆说"我守住我的秘密，你守好你的"，究竟是什么意思？

千百个乱人心神的问题摆在我面前。我的行程好比穿越施佩萨特森林（Spessart）——每走一步，就有新的妖精或怪物破土而出，或从树后闪身而出。

===Original===
Peremptorily I dismissed these harassing and frightful doubts. I secured my door, sat myself down at my table, and with a candle at each side, placed before me the piece of vellum which contained the drawings and notes on which I was to rely for full instructions as to how to use the key.

When I had studied this for awhile, I made my investigation. The angle of the room at the right side of the window was cut off by an oblique turn in the wainscot. I examined this carefully, and, on pressure, a small bit of the frame of the woodwork slid aside, and disclosed a keyhole. On removing my finger, it shot back to its place again, with a spring. So far I had interpreted my instructions successfully. A similar search, next the door, and directly under this, was rewarded by a like discovery. The small end of the key fitted this, as it had the upper keyhole; and now, with two or three hard jerks at the key, a door in the panel opened, showing a strip of the bare wall, and a narrow, arched doorway, piercing the thickness of the wall; and within which I saw a screw-staircase of stone.

===Chinese===
我断然撵开这些折磨人的可怕疑虑。我闩好房门，在桌前坐定，左右各点一支蜡烛，把那张羊皮纸摊在面前——上面的图样和说明，正是我使用那把钥匙所依据的完整指南。

研究了一阵之后，我便动手查探。房间在窗子右侧的那个屋角，被护壁板一道斜向的转折切去了。我仔细检视那里，一按之下，木工框架的一小块滑向一旁，露出一个锁孔。手指一松，它又凭借弹簧弹回原位。到此为止，我对说明的解读都很顺利。在房门旁边、正对着这处的地方，同样的搜寻也有了同样的收获。钥匙的小头正合这锁孔，一如它合上面那个；接着，使劲拧了两三下钥匙，壁板上的一扇门开了，露出一线光秃秃的墙壁，和一道狭窄的拱形门洞，穿透墙的厚度；门洞里面，我看到一道石头旋梯。

===Original===
Candle in hand I stepped in. I do not know whether the quality of air, long undisturbed, is peculiar; to me it has always seemed so, and the damp smell of the old masonry hung in this atmosphere. My candle faintly lighted the bare stone wall that enclosed the stair, the foot of which I could not see. Down I went, and a few turns brought me to the stone floor. Here was another door, of the simple, old, oak kind, deep sunk in the thickness of the wall. The large end of the key fitted this. The lock was stiff; I set the candle down upon the stair, and applied both hands; it turned with difficulty, and as it revolved, uttered a shriek that alarmed me for my secret.

For some minutes I did not move. In a little time, however, I took courage, and opened the door. The night-air floating in, puffed out the candle. There was a thicket of holly and underwood, as dense as a jungle, close about the door. I should have been in pitch-darkness, were it not that through the topmost leaves, there twinkled, here and there, a glimmer of moonshine.

Softly, lest anyone should have opened his window, at the sound of the rusty bolt, I struggled through this, till I gained a view of the open grounds. Here I found that the brushwood spread a good way up the park, uniting with the wood that approached the little temple I have described.

===Chinese===
我举烛跨了进去。长年不扰动的空气是否真有一种特别的味道，我说不准；在我感觉里向来如此，而古老砖石那股潮湿的气味，正弥漫在这片空气里。烛光幽幽地照着围拢旋梯的光石壁，梯底我望不见。我一路下去，转了几折便到了石造的地面。这里又有一扇门，是那种古旧简朴的橡木门，深深嵌在厚墙里。钥匙的大头正配这把锁。锁很滞涩；我把蜡烛搁在梯级上，双手齐上；锁艰难地转动着，转动的当口发出一声锐叫，惊得我为自己的秘密捏了一把汗。

有好几分钟我一动不动。不过稍过片刻，我鼓起勇气，推开了门。夜气涌入，把蜡烛扑灭了。门四周密密地围着一丛冬青和矮树，密得像片丛林。若不是透过最高处的枝叶，这儿那儿还闪着几缕月光，我便是置身于漆黑一片之中了。

我轻手轻脚地摸索着穿过树丛——生怕生锈的门闩一响，有人会推开窗户张望——直到望见开阔的园地。这时我发现，这片灌木一路蔓延上园林深处，与我先前描写过的那座小庙附近逼近的树林连成了一片。

===Original===
A general could not have chosen a more effectually-covered approach from the Dragon Volant to the trysting-place where hitherto I had conferred with the idol of my lawless adoration.

Looking back upon the old inn, I discovered that the stair I descended was enclosed in one of those slender turrets that decorate such buildings. It was placed at that angle which corresponded with the part of the paneling of my room indicated in the plan I had been studying.

Thoroughly satisfied with my experiment, I made my way back to the door, with some little difficulty, remounted to my room, locked my secret door again; kissed the mysterious key that her hand had pressed that night, and placed it under my pillow, upon which, very soon after, my giddy head was laid, not, for some time, to sleep soundly.

===Chinese===
从飞龙旅馆到那处幽会之地——我至今仍不时前去朝拜我那无法无天的偶像的地方——即使让一位将军来选，也挑不出一条遮蔽得如此严实的路径。

回望老旅馆，我发现方才下去的那道楼梯，就藏在装点这类建筑的那些细塔楼中的一座里。塔楼的位置正处于那个角落，同我研究的那张图上标示的我房间壁板的那一部分恰好吻合。

对这次试验心满意足，我颇费了些周折摸回门口，重新上楼回到房间，锁好我的暗门；在那把今夜被她的手握过的神秘钥匙上亲了一口，把它放到枕头底下。不久，我晕晕乎乎的脑袋便落在了这只枕头上，好一阵子都睡不安稳。

## XXI I See Three Men in a Mirror / XXI 我在镜中看见三个人

===Original===
I awoke very early next morning, and was too excited to sleep again. As soon as I could, without exciting remark, I saw my host. I told him that I was going into town that night, and thence to ————, where I had to see some people on business, and requested him to mention my being there to any friend who might call. That I expected to be back in about a week, and that in the meantime my servant, St. Clair, would keep the key of my room, and look after my things.

Having prepared this mystification for my landlord, I drove into Paris, and there transacted the financial part of the affair. The problem was to reduce my balance, nearly thirty thousand pounds, to a shape in which it would be not only easily portable, but available, wherever I might go, without involving correspondence, or any other incident which would disclose my place of residence, for the time being. All these points were as nearly provided for as they could be. I need not trouble you about my arrangements for passports. It is enough to say that the point I selected for our flight was, in the spirit of romance, one of the most beautiful and sequestered nooks in Switzerland.

Luggage, I should start with none. The first considerable town we reached next morning, would supply an extemporized wardrobe. It was now two o’clock; *only* two! How on earth was I to dispose of the remainder of the day?

===Chinese===
第二天清晨我醒得很早，兴奋得再睡不着。一等到不惹人注目的时辰，我便去见店主。我告诉他，我当晚要进城，再从那里前往某地，有事要见几个人；并请他转告任何可能来访的朋友，说我在那边。还说预计一周左右回来，此间我的仆人圣克莱尔会保管我房间的钥匙，照看我的东西。

给店主布下这套迷魂阵之后，我便驱车进了巴黎，在那里办妥了这桩事的银钱部分。难题在于把我名下将近三万英镑的存款，换成一个不仅便于携带、而且无论我走到哪里都能随时取用的形制，既不牵涉书信往来，也不出任何会暴露我临时居所的岔子。这些要点都已尽可能周全地办妥了。护照的安排不必赘述，免得烦扰诸位。只需说一句：我为我们出逃选定的地点，本着罗曼蒂克的精神，是瑞士最美丽、最幽静的角落之一。

行李，我将一件不带。明早我们到达的第一座像样的城镇，自会置办出一套应急的行头。这时是两点钟；才两点！这一天的剩余时光，我到底该怎么打发？

===Original===
I had not yet seen the cathedral of Notre Dame; and thither I drove. I spent an hour or more there; and then to the Conciergerie, the Palais de Justice, and the beautiful Sainte Chapelle. Still there remained some time to get rid of, and I strolled into the narrow streets adjoining the cathedral. I recollect seeing, in one of them, an old house with a mural inscription stating that it had been the residence of Canon Fulbert, the uncle of Abelard’s Eloise. I don’t know whether these curious old streets, in which I observed fragments of ancient gothic churches fitted up as warehouses, are still extant. I lighted, among other dingy and eccentric shops, upon one that seemed that of a broker of all sorts of old decorations, armour, china, furniture. I entered the shop; it was dark, dusty, and low. The proprietor was busy scouring a piece of inlaid armour, and allowed me to poke about his shop, and examine the curious things accumulated there, just as I pleased. Gradually I made my way to the farther end of it, where there was but one window with many panes, each with a bull’s-eye in it, and in the dirtiest possible state. When I reached this window, I turned about, and in a recess, standing at right angles with the side wall of the shop, was a large mirror in an old-fashioned dingy frame. Reflected in this I saw, what in old houses I have heard termed an “alcove,” in which, among lumber, and various dusty articles hanging on the wall, there stood a table, at which three persons were seated, as it seemed to me, in earnest conversation. Two of these persons I instantly recognized; one was Colonel Gaillarde, the other was the Marquis d’Harmonville. The third, who was fiddling with a pen, was a lean, pale man, pitted with the smallpox, with lank black hair, and about as mean-looking a person as I had ever seen in my life. The Marquis looked up, and his glance was instantaneously followed by his two companions. For a moment I hesitated what to do. But it was plain that I was not recognized, as indeed I could hardly have been, the light from the window being behind me, and the portion of the shop immediately before me, being very dark indeed.

===Chinese===
我还没见过圣母院大教堂；便驱车前往。我在那里流连了一个多钟头；然后去了巴黎古监狱（Conciergerie）、司法宫（Palais de Justice）和美丽的圣礼拜堂（Sainte Chapelle）。仍旧剩下些时光需要打发，我便信步走进大教堂旁边那些窄巷。我记得在其中一条巷子里见到一座老房子，墙上嵌着铭文，说它曾是阿伯拉尔的爱洛依丝的舅舅、教士富尔贝尔的故居。我不知道这些奇妙的老街如今是否还在——我曾看见古老哥特式教堂的残段被改作仓库。在几家昏暗而古怪的铺子之间，我撞上一家，看样子是个经营各色旧装饰品、盔甲、瓷器、家具的经纪铺。我走进店里；铺内昏暗、积尘、低矮。店主正忙着擦拭一件嵌花盔甲，任由我在他店里东翻西看，随意检视那里堆集的种种古怪物事。我渐渐挪到店铺最里头，那儿只有一扇多格的窗子，每块玻璃中央都带一个牛眼形圆泡，脏得不能再脏。走到这扇窗前，我回过身，只见一个凹室里——与店铺侧墙成直角的位置——立着一面老式暗框的大镜子。镜中映出的，是我听人说旧宅里那种「凹室」（alcove）的所在：破烂家什之间，墙上挂着各样蒙尘的物件，立着一张桌子，桌边坐着三个人，看样子正在认真地交谈。其中两人我一眼便认出：一个是盖亚尔上校，另一个是德·哈蒙维尔侯爵。第三个正摆弄着一支笔，是个瘦削苍白的男人，脸上有天花麻子，稀疏的黑发耷拉着，模样之猥琐，是我生平仅见。侯爵抬起头来，他目光一扫，两个同伴立刻随之望来。我一时踌躇不知如何是好。但显然他们没有认出我——事实上也几乎不可能认出：窗光在我背后，而我面前那片店堂实在暗得很。

===Original===
Perceiving this, I had presence of mind to affect being entirely engrossed by the objects before me, and strolled slowly down the shop again. I paused for a moment to hear whether I was followed, and was relieved when I heard no step. You may be sure I did not waste more time in that shop, where I had just made a discovery so curious and so unexpected.

It was no business of mine to inquire what brought Colonel Gaillarde and the Marquis together, in so shabby, and even dirty a place, or who the mean person, biting the feather end of his pen, might be. Such employments as the Marquis had accepted sometimes make strange bedfellows.

I was glad to get away, and just as the sun set, I had reached the steps of the Dragon Volant, and dismissed the vehicle in which I arrived, carrying in my hand a strong box, of marvellously small dimensions considering all it contained, strapped in a leather cover, which disguised its real character.

===Chinese===
看出这一点，我沉得住气，装出一副全然沉溺在眼前物件里的样子，又慢慢向店堂那头踱去。我停了片刻，听听有没有人跟上来，没听见脚步声，才松了口气。你尽可相信，在那家刚刚让我有了如此离奇、如此意外之发现的铺子里，我没有再多耽搁一刻。

盖亚尔上校和侯爵是何缘由聚在这样一处破败、甚至肮脏的地方，那个咬着笔杆羽毛末端的猥琐人物又是谁，都不是我该打听的。侯爵大人接受的这类差事，有时会把八竿子打不着的人凑作一处。

我庆幸自己脱身出来。日落时分，我已到了飞龙旅馆门前的台阶上，打发走来时的车，手里提着一只钱箱——以它装下的东西而论，小得惊人——外面用皮罩束着，掩住了它的真容。

===Original===
When I got to my room, I summoned St. Clair. I told him nearly the same story I had already told my host. I gave him fifty pounds, with orders to expend whatever was necessary on himself, and in payment for my rooms till my return. I then ate a slight and hasty dinner. My eyes were often upon the solemn old clock over the chimneypiece, which was my sole accomplice in keeping tryst in this iniquitous venture. The sky favoured my design, and darkened all things with a sea of clouds.

The innkeeper met me in the hall, to ask whether I should want a vehicle to Paris? I was prepared for this question, and instantly answered that I meant to walk to Versailles, and take a carriage there. I called St. Clair.

“Go,” said I, “and drink a bottle of wine with your friends. I shall call you if I should want anything; in the meantime, here is the key of my room; I shall be writing some notes, so don’t allow anyone to disturb me, for at least half an hour. At the end of that time you will probably find that I have left this for Versailles; and should you not find me in the room, you may take that for granted; and you take charge of everything, and lock the door, you understand?”

St. Clair took his leave, wishing me all happiness and no doubt promising himself some little amusement with my money. With my candle in my hand, I hastened upstairs. It wanted now but five minutes to the appointed time. I do not think there is anything of the coward in my nature; but I confess, as the crisis approached, I felt something of the suspense and awe of a soldier going into action. Would I have receded? Not for all this earth could offer.

===Chinese===
回到房里，我唤来圣克莱尔。我对他说了与对店主所说几乎相同的一套话。我给了他五十英镑，吩咐他该花在自己身上的尽管花，并代付我到回来为止的房钱。然后我匆匆吃了顿简便的晚饭。我的目光不时落在壁炉架上那座神情肃穆的老钟上——在这桩伤天害理的冒险里，它是唯一与我共守约会的东西。天色也成全我的图谋，一片云海把万物都罩暗了。

店主在大厅里迎住我，问我要不要雇车去巴黎？这个问题我早有准备，立刻答道：我打算步行到凡尔赛，在那里搭车。我唤来圣克莱尔。

"去吧，"我说，"同你的朋友们喝瓶酒去。我要什么自会叫你；眼下，这是我房间的钥匙；我要写几封信，所以至少半个钟头之内，别让任何人来打搅我。半个钟头之后，你多半会发现我已经动身去凡尔赛了；你要是发现我不在房里，就这么认定好了；你把一切照管起来，把门锁上，明白吗？"

圣克莱尔告退了，祝愿我万事如意，无疑也盘算着拿我的钱给自己寻点小小的乐趣。我手执蜡烛，赶紧上楼。此刻离约定时间只差五分钟了。我不认为自己天性里有半点怯懦；但我承认，随着关键时刻临近，我尝到了几分士兵临阵时的紧张与肃然。我会退缩吗？把全世界能给的都给我，也不会。

===Original===
I bolted my door, put on my great coat, and placed my pistols, one in each pocket. I now applied my key to the secret locks; drew the wainscot-door a little open, took my strong box under my arm, extinguished my candle, unbolted my door, listened at it for a few moments to be sure that no one was approaching, and then crossed the floor of my room swiftly, entered the secret door, and closed the spring lock after me. I was upon the screw-stair in total darkness, the key in my fingers. Thus far the undertaking was successful.

===Chinese===
我闩上房门，穿上大衣，两支手枪一边口袋里插一支。接着我把钥匙探进暗锁；将壁板门拉开一条缝，钱箱夹在腋下，吹灭蜡烛，拉开房门的闩，在门边听了片刻，确信没有人走近，然后疾步穿过房间地板，钻进暗门，把弹簧锁在身后扣上。我立于全然漆黑的旋梯上，钥匙攥在指间。到这一步为止，行动是成功的。

## XXII Rapture / XXII 狂喜

===Original===
Down the screw-stair I went in utter darkness; and having reached the stone floor, I discerned the door and groped out the keyhole. With more caution, and less noise than upon the night before, I opened the door, and stepped out into the thick brushwood. It was almost as dark in this jungle.

Having secured the door, I slowly pushed my way through the bushes, which soon became less dense. Then, with more ease, but still under thick cover, I pursued in the track of the wood, keeping near its edge.

At length, in the darkened air, about fifty yards away, the shafts of the marble temple rose like phantoms before me, seen through the trunks of the old trees. Everything favoured my enterprise. I had effectually mystified my servant and the people of the Dragon Volant, and so dark was the night, that even had I alarmed the suspicions of all the tenants of the inn, I might safely defy their united curiosity, though posted at every window of the house.

Through the trunks, over the roots of the old trees, I reached the appointed place of observation. I laid my treasure, in its leathern case, in the embrasure, and leaning my arms upon it, looked steadily in the direction of the château. The outline of the building was scarcely discernible, blending dimly, as it did, with the sky. No light in any window was visible. I was plainly to wait; but for how long?

===Chinese===
我在一片漆黑中顺旋梯而下；到了石地面，我辨认出那扇门，摸索出锁孔。我比前一夜更加小心、更少声息地打开门，跨进了密密的灌木丛。这片丛林里几乎一样黑。

锁好门后，我缓缓地从灌木间挤过去，灌木很快变得稀疏了。接着我走得较为从容，但仍在浓密的遮蔽之下，沿着树林的踪迹前行，贴近林缘。

终于，在暗下来的空气里，约五十码开外，那座大理石小庙的柱身透过老树的树干，像幽灵一般立在我眼前。一切都利于我的图谋。我已把我的仆人和飞龙旅馆的人着实迷惑住了，而夜又这么黑，哪怕我惹得客栈全体房客都起了疑心，纵然他们守在这座屋子的每一扇窗后，我也尽可以不怕他们合起来的好奇心。

穿过树干，跨过老树的虬根，我到了约定的守望地点。我把皮套裹着的财宝搁在窗龛里，双臂倚在上面，目不转睛地朝城堡的方向望去。建筑的轮廓几乎分辨不出，只是昏昏地与天色融作一处。哪扇窗里也不见灯火。显然我还得等；可要等多久呢？

===Original===
Leaning on my box of treasure, gazing toward the massive shadow that represented the château, in the midst of my ardent and elated longings, there came upon me an odd thought, which you will think might well have struck me long before. It seemed on a sudden, as it came, that the darkness deepened, and a chill stole into the air around me.

Suppose I were to disappear finally, like those other men whose stories I had listened to! Had I not been at all the pains that mortal could, to obliterate every trace of my real proceedings, and to mislead everyone to whom I spoke as to the direction in which I had gone?

This icy, snake-light thought stole through my mind, and was gone.

It was with me the full-blooded season of youth, conscious strength, rashness, passion, pursuit, the adventure! Here were a pair of double-barrelled pistols, four lives in my hands? What could possibly happen? The Count—except for the sake of my dulcinea, what was it to me whether the old coward whom I had seen, in an ague of terror before the brawling Colonel, interposed or not? I was assuming the worst that could happen. But with an ally so clever and courageous as my beautiful Countess, could any such misadventure befall? Bah! I laughed at all such fancies.

As I thus communed with myself, the signal light sprang up. The rose-coloured light, *couleur de rose*, emblem of sanguine hope, and the dawn of a happy day.

===Chinese===
我倚着我的宝箱，凝望着代表城堡的那团巨大的暗影，在热烈而飞扬的渴望之中，一个古怪的念头忽然袭上心头——你会认为这念头早该在我脑中闪过了。它来的时候，黑暗仿佛骤然加深，一股寒意悄悄钻进我四周的空气里。

万一我也像那些人一样永远消失了呢——那些人的故事我可都是亲耳听来的！我岂不是费尽了一个凡人所能费的心机，抹去自己真实行藏的一切痕迹，把每一个同我讲过话的人都引错了方向？

这条冰冷的、蛇一般滑溜的念头从我心里游过，随即消散了。

那时我正当青春血气方刚的时节：自觉有力、莽撞、激情、追逐、冒险！这里是一对双筒手枪，四条性命在我掌中？还能出什么事？伯爵——若不是为了我的杜尔西内娅（dulcinea），那个我亲眼见过的老家伙——在大吵大嚷的上校面前吓得像打摆子一样发抖的胆小鬼——他挡不挡道，与我何干？我已把可能发生的最坏情形都算计进去了。可有这样一位又聪慧又勇毅的盟友——我美丽的伯爵夫人——那样的祸事又怎会落到头上？呸！我对这类胡思乱想一笑置之。

正当我这样自忖，信号灯亮了起来。那道玫瑰色的光，法语里的玫瑰色（couleur de rose），乐观希望的象征，快乐一天的黎明。

===Original===
Clear, soft, and steady, glowed the light from the window. The stone shafts showed black against it. Murmuring words of passionate love as I gazed upon the signal, I grasped my strong box under my arm, and with rapid strides approached the Château de la Carque. No sign of light or life, no human voice, no tread of foot, no bark of dog, indicated a chance of interruption. A blind was down; and as I came close to the tall window, I found that half-a-dozen steps led up to it, and that a large lattice, answering for a door, lay open.

A shadow from within fell upon the blind; it was drawn aside, and as I ascended the steps, a soft voice murmured—“Richard, dearest Richard, come, oh! come! how I have longed for this moment?”

Never did she look so beautiful. My love rose to passionate enthusiasm. I only wished there were some real danger in the adventure worthy of such a creature. When the first tumultuous greeting was over, she made me sit beside her on a sofa. There we talked for a minute or two. She told me that the Count had gone, and was by that time more than a mile on his way, with the funeral, to Père la Chaise. Here were her diamonds. She exhibited, hastily, an open casket containing a profusion of the largest brilliants.

===Chinese===
窗里的光清亮、柔和而稳定地燃着。石柱衬着它显出黑色的轮廓。我一面凝望信号，一面喃喃说着炽热的情话，把钱箱夹在腋下，大步流星地走向拉卡尔克城堡。没有一点灯火或人气的迹象，没有人声，没有脚步，没有犬吠，显示有被打搅之虞。一幅窗帘垂着；走近那扇高窗时，我发现有六级台阶直上窗前，一扇权当门用的大格子窗敞开着。

里面的一道影子投在窗帘上；窗帘拉开了，我拾级而上时，一个温柔的声音喃喃道——"理查德，最亲爱的理查德，来呀，哦！来呀！我盼这一刻盼得多么苦啊？"

她从没有像此刻这样美。我的爱意涨成炽烈的狂热。我只恨这场冒险里没有一个配得上这样一位尤物的真实危险。最初那阵激动的问候过后，她让我在她身边的沙发上坐下。我们在那里谈了一两分钟。她告诉我，伯爵已经动身，此刻已随着送葬的队伍，在去拉雪兹神父公墓的路上走出一英里开外了。她的钻石在这里。她匆匆展示了一只敞开的珠匣，里面满满盛着成堆最大颗的闪亮钻石。

===Original===
“What is this?” she asked.

“A box containing money to the amount of thirty thousand pounds,” I answered.

“What! all that money?” she exclaimed.

“Every sou.”

“Was it not unnecessary to bring so much, seeing all these,” she said, touching her diamonds. “It would have been kind of you, to allow me to provide for both for a time, at least. It would have made me happier even than I am.”

“Dearest, generous angel!” Such was my extravagant declamation. “You forget that it may be necessary, for a long time, to observe silence as to where we are, and impossible to communicate safely with anyone.”

“You have then here this great sum—are you certain; have you counted it?”

“Yes, certainly; I received it today,” I answered, perhaps showing a little surprise in my face, “I counted it, of course, on drawing it from my bankers.”

===Chinese===
"这是什么？"她问。

"一只箱子，装着三万英镑的钱，"我答道。

"什么！这么多钱？"她叫道。

"一个苏也不少。"

"有这些在这儿，带这么多来岂不是多余，"她说着，抚了抚她的钻石。"你若肯让我供养我们两个一阵子，哪怕是暂时的，那才是你的体贴呢。那会让我比现在还幸福。"

"最亲爱的、慷慨的天使！"我的夸张台词便是如此。"你忘了，我们可能得长久地隐瞒行踪，也未必能与任何人安全地通信。"

"那么这大笔钱就在这里了——你确定吗；你点过数吗？"

"是的，当然；我今天才拿到手，"我答道，脸上也许露出一点诧异，"从银行家那里提出来的时候，我自然点过了。"

===Original===
“It makes me feel a little nervous, travelling with so much money; but these jewels make as great a danger; *that* can add but little to it. Place them side by side; you shall take off your great coat when we are ready to go, and with it manage to conceal these boxes. I should not like the drivers to suspect that we were conveying such a treasure. I must ask you now to close the curtains of that window, and bar the shutters.”

I had hardly done this when a knock was heard at the room-door.

“I know who this is,” she said, in a whisper to me.

I saw that she was not alarmed. She went softly to the door, and a whispered conversation for a minute followed.

“My trusty maid, who is coming with us. She says we cannot safely go sooner than ten minutes. She is bringing some coffee to the next room.”

She opened the door and looked in.

“I must tell her not to take too much luggage. She is so odd! Don’t follow—stay where you are—it is better that she should not see you.”

She left the room with a gesture of caution.

===Chinese===
"带着这么多钱赶路，我心里有点发慌；可这些珠宝招来的风险也一样大；这点钱添上去也多不了几分。把它们并排放好；等我们动身时你把大衣脱下来，用它把这几只匣子掩起来。我可不愿叫车夫们疑心我们运着这样一笔财宝。现在我要请你放下那扇窗的窗帘，闩上百叶窗。"

我刚做完这些，房门上便响起了叩门声。

"我知道这是谁，"她悄声对我说。

我看她并不惊慌。她轻步走到门边，跟着是一分钟的窃窃私语。

"是我那可靠的贴身女仆，她同我们一起走。她说不到十分钟以后走不保险。她正往隔壁房间送咖啡来。"

她推开门往里看。

"我得嘱咐她别带太多行李。她这人就是古怪！别跟来——站在原地——最好别让她瞧见你。"

她做了个示意小心的手势，离开了房间。

===Original===
A change had come over the manner of this beautiful woman. For the last few minutes a shadow had been stealing over her, an air of abstraction, a look bordering on suspicion. Why was she pale? Why had there come that dark look in her eyes? Why had her very voice become changed? Had anything gone suddenly wrong? Did some danger threaten?

This doubt, however, speedily quieted itself. If there had been anything of the kind, she would, of course, have told me. It was only natural that, as the crisis approached, she should become more and more nervous. She did not return quite so soon as I had expected. To a man in my situation absolute quietude is next to impossible. I moved restlessly about the room. It was a small one. There was a door at the other end. I opened it, rashly enough. I listened, it was perfectly silent. I was in an excited, eager state, and every faculty engrossed about what was coming, and in so far detached from the immediate present. I can’t account, in any other way, for my having done so many foolish things that night, for I was, naturally, by no means deficient in cunning. About the most stupid of those was, that instead of immediately closing that door, which I never ought to have opened, I actually took a candle and walked into the room.

There I made, quite unexpectedly, a rather startling discovery.

===Chinese===
这位美人的神态起了变化。过去几分钟里，一片阴影悄悄爬上她，一种心不在焉的神气，一种近乎猜疑的表情。她为什么发白？她眼里为什么浮起那层阴翳？她的声音为什么都变了？可是突然出了什么岔子？有什么危险在逼近？

不过这疑虑很快自己平息了。真要有那种事，她当然会告诉我。随着时刻临近，她越来越紧张，原也是人之常情。她没有像我预想的那么快回来。处在我这种境地的人，要做到绝对的安静几乎不可能。我在房里不安地来回走动。这是个小房间。另一头有一扇门。我鲁莽地把它推开了。我听了听，里面鸦雀无声。我当时正处于亢奋急切的状态，全部心神都被将要来临的事占满，因而对眼下的当下反倒心不在焉。那晚我干了那么多蠢事，我只能这样解释，因为我天生绝非缺乏狡黠。其中最蠢的一桩是：那扇我本不该开的门，我非但没有立刻关上，反而真的端起一支蜡烛，走进了那个房间。

在那里，我全然出乎意料地有了一个相当惊人的发现。

## XXIII A Cup of Coffee / XXIII 一杯咖啡

===Original===
The room was carpetless. On the floor were a quantity of shavings, and some score of bricks. Beyond these, on a narrow table, lay an object, which I could hardly believe I saw aright.

I approached and drew from it a sheet which had very slightly disguised its shape. There was no mistake about it. It was a coffin; and on the lid was a plate, with the inscription in French:

> Pierre de la Roche St. Amand.
> Agée de XXIII ans.

I drew back with a double shock. So, then, the funeral after all had not yet left! Here lay the body. I had been deceived. This, no doubt, accounted for the embarrassment so manifest in the Countess’s manner. She would have done more wisely had she told me the true state of the case.

I drew back from this melancholy room, and closed the door. Her distrust of me was the worst rashness she could have committed. There is nothing more dangerous than misapplied caution. In entire ignorance of the fact I had entered the room, and there I might have lighted upon some of the very persons it was our special anxiety that I should avoid.

===Chinese===
这房间没有铺地毯。地板上是一堆刨花，还有二十来块砖。在这些东西那头，一张窄桌上躺着一件东西，我几乎不敢相信自己的眼睛。

我走上前去，从它上面掀开一层仅仅稍稍掩住其形状的布单。没有看错。那是一口棺材；盖板上钉着一块铭牌，刻着法文的碑铭：

> 皮埃尔·德·拉·罗什·圣阿芒（Pierre de la Roche St. Amand）。
> 享年二十三岁（Agée de XXIII ans）。

我带着双重的震惊退了回来。这么说，送葬的队伍终究还没有出发！遗体就停在这里。我被骗了。这无疑解释了伯爵夫人神态间那般明显的局促。她若把实情告诉我，才是更聪明的做法。

我从这个阴惨惨的房间退出来，关上了门。她对我的不信任，是她所能犯下的最糟的轻率。没有什么比用错了地方的谨慎更危险。我全然不知实情，闯进了那个房间，在那儿我极可能撞上几个恰恰是我们最担心要避开的人。

===Original===
These reflections were interrupted, almost as soon as begun, by the return of the Countess de St. Alyre. I saw at a glance that she detected in my face some evidence of what had happened, for she threw a hasty look towards the door.

“Have you seen anything—anything to disturb you, dear Richard? Have you been out of this room?”

I answered promptly, “Yes,” and told her frankly what had happened.

“Well, I did not like to make you more uneasy than necessary. Besides, it is disgusting and horrible. The body *is* there; but the Count had departed a quarter of an hour before I lighted the coloured lamp, and prepared to receive you. The body did not arrive till eight or ten minutes after he had set out. He was afraid lest the people at Père la Chaise should suppose that the funeral was postponed. He knew that the remains of poor Pierre would certainly reach this tonight although an unexpected delay has occurred; and there are reasons why he wishes the funeral completed before tomorrow. The hearse with the body must leave this in ten minutes. So soon as it is gone, we shall be free to set out upon our wild and happy journey. The horses are to the carriage in the porte-cochère. As for this *funeste* horror (she shuddered very prettily), let us think of it no more.”

===Chinese===
这些念头刚起，便被德·圣阿利尔伯爵夫人的归来打断了。我一眼便看出，她从我脸上察觉了事情的一些痕迹，因为她朝那扇门仓促地瞥了一眼。

"你看见什么了吗——什么让你不安的东西，亲爱的理查德？你出过这间屋子吗？"

我立刻答了声"是"，并把发生的事坦白告诉了她。

"唉，我不想让你担不必要的更多心事。再说，这事又恶心又可怕。遗体确实在那里；可是在我点亮彩色灯、准备接你进来之前一刻钟，伯爵就已经动身走了。遗体是在他出发之后八九分钟才送到的。他怕拉雪兹神父公墓那边的人以为葬礼改期了。他知道可怜的皮埃尔的遗骸今晚必定会送到，尽管出了个意料之外的耽搁；而他自有缘故，希望葬礼赶在明天之前办完。载遗体的灵车十分钟后必须离开这里。它一走，我们就可以动身，踏上我们这场狂放而快乐的旅程了。马已经套上车，停在门廊车廊里了。至于这桩凶煞事（funeste）的恐怖"（她颤得煞是动人），"我们再别去想它了。"

===Original===
She bolted the door of communication, and when she turned, it was with such a pretty penitence in her face and attitude, that I was ready to throw myself at her feet.

“It is the last time,” she said, in a sweet sad little pleading, “I shall ever practise a deception on my brave and beautiful Richard—my hero? Am I forgiven?”

Here was another scene of passionate effusion, and lovers’ raptures and declamations, but only murmured, lest the ears of listeners should be busy.

At length, on a sudden, she raised her hand, as if to prevent my stirring, her eyes fixed on me, and her ear toward the door of the room in which the coffin was placed, and remained breathless in that attitude for a few moments. Then, with a little nod towards me, she moved on tiptoe to the door, and listened, extending her hand backward as if to warn me against advancing; and, after a little time, she returned, still on tiptoe, and whispered to me, “They are removing the coffin—come with me.”

I accompanied her into the room from which her maid, as she told me, had spoken to her. Coffee and some old china cups, which appeared to me quite beautiful, stood on a silver tray; and some liqueur glasses, with a flask, which turned out to be noyau, on a salver beside it.

===Chinese===
她闩上连通两屋的门，转过身来时，脸上和姿态里带着那样楚楚动人的悔意，我几乎要拜倒在她脚下。

"这是最后一次，"她用甜甜的、忧伤的口吻小声恳求道，"我再也不同我勇敢英俊的理查德——我的英雄——耍半点欺瞒了？我能得到原谅吗？"

接着又是一幕激情奔涌的场面，恋人间的狂喜与剖白，只是都压在唇间喃喃低语，生怕隔墙有耳。

终于，她蓦地举起手，像是要止住我的动作，眼睛紧盯着我，耳朵偏向停放着棺材的那间房的门，屏住呼吸保持着这个姿势，片刻不动。然后，她朝我微微颔首，踮着脚走到门边听了听，一只手向后伸着，仿佛警告我别上前；过了一小会儿，她仍踮着脚回来，对我悄声道："他们在抬棺材了——跟我来。"

我随她走进那个房间——据她说，她的女仆方才就是在那儿同她说话的。一只银托盘上摆着咖啡和几只旧瓷杯，在我看来美极了；旁边另一只托盘上是几只利口酒杯和一只细颈瓶，后来知道盛的是努瓦约酒（noyau）。

===Original===
“I shall attend you. I’m to be your servant here; I am to have my own way; I shall not think myself forgiven by my darling if he refuses to indulge me in anything.” She filled a cup with coffee, and handed it to me with her left hand, her right arm she fondly, passed over my shoulder, and with her fingers through my curls caressingly, she whispered, “Take this, I shall take some just now.”

It was excellent; and when I had done she handed me the liqueur, which I also drank.

“Come back, dearest, to the next room,” she said. “By this time those terrible people must have gone away, and we shall be safer there, for the present, than here.”

“You shall direct, and I obey; you shall command me, not only now, but always, and in all things, my beautiful queen!” I murmured.

My heroics were unconsciously, I daresay, founded upon my ideal of the French school of lovemaking. I am, even now, ashamed as I recall the bombast to which I treated the Countess de St. Alyre.

“There, you shall have another miniature glass—a fairy glass—of noyau,” she said, gaily. In this volatile creature, the funereal gloom of the moment before, and the suspense of an adventure on which all her future was staked, disappeared in a moment. She ran and returned with another tiny glass, which, with an eloquent or tender little speech, I placed to my lips and sipped.

===Chinese===
"我来侍候你。这一回我做你的仆人；一切要依我的意思；我的心肝若不肯样样纵容我，我就不认为自己得到了原谅。"她斟了一杯咖啡，用左手递给我，右手臂柔情地搭上我的肩，手指插进我的鬈发间轻抚着，悄声说："把它喝了，我马上也喝一点。"

咖啡好极了；我喝完后，她又递来利口酒，我也喝了。

"回隔壁房间去吧，最亲爱的，"她说，"这会儿那伙可怕的人必定已经走了，眼下我们在那边要比在这里安全。"

"你来指点，我来服从；你只管命令我，不单是现在，而是永远，在一切事情上，我美丽的王后！"我喃喃道。

我敢说，我的这套英雄腔，是无意间师法了我理想中法国式的谈情路数。即使现在，一想起自己对德·圣阿利尔伯爵夫人发过的那通浮夸言辞，我还臊得慌。

"来，再给你一小杯——一小杯精灵杯里的努瓦约酒，"她欢快地说。在这个轻灵活泼的人身上，方才那阵丧仪的阴郁，和一场押上她全部前程的冒险的悬心，顷刻间都无影无踪了。她跑开去又端来一小杯，我伴着一句动听而温柔的小话，把它举到唇边啜了一口。

===Original===
I kissed her hand, I kissed her lips, I gazed in her beautiful eyes, and kissed her again unresisting.

“You call me Richard, by what name am I to call my beautiful divinity?” I asked.

“You call me Eugenie, it is my name. Let us be quite real; that is, if you love as entirely as I do.”

“Eugenie!” I exclaimed, and broke into a new rapture upon the name.

It ended by my telling her how impatient I was to set out upon our journey; and, as I spoke, suddenly an odd sensation overcame me. It was not in the slightest degree like faintness. I can find no phrase to describe it, but a sudden constraint of the brain; it was as if the membrane in which it lies, if there be such a thing, contracted, and became inflexible.

“Dear Richard! what is the matter?” she exclaimed, with terror in her looks. “Good Heavens! are you ill? I conjure you, sit down; sit in this chair.” She almost forced me into one; I was in no condition to offer the least resistance. I recognised but too truly the sensations that supervened. I was lying back in the chair in which I sat without the power, by this time, of uttering a syllable, of closing my eyelids, of moving my eyes, of stirring a muscle. I had in a few seconds glided into precisely the state in which I had passed so many appalling hours when approaching Paris, in my night-drive with the Marquis d’Harmonville.

Great and loud was the lady’s agony. She seemed to have lost all sense of fear. She called me by my name, shook me by the shoulder, raised my arm and let it fall, all the time imploring of me, in distracting sentences, to make the slightest sign of life, and vowing that if I did not, she would make away with herself.

These ejaculations, after a minute or two, suddenly subsided. The lady was perfectly silent and cool. In a very businesslike way she took a candle and stood before me, pale indeed, very pale, but with an expression only of intense scrutiny with a dash of horror in it. She moved the candle before my eyes slowly, evidently watching the effect. She then set it down, and rang a hand-bell two or three times sharply. She placed the two cases (I mean hers containing the jewels) and my strong box, side by side on the table; and I saw her carefully lock the door that gave access to the room in which I had just now sipped my coffee.

===Chinese===
我吻了她的手，吻了她的唇，凝望她美丽的眼睛，又一次吻了她，她顺从地任我亲吻。

"你唤我理查德，那我该用什么名字称呼我美丽的女神呢？"我问。

"你就唤我欧仁妮，这是我的名字。让我们完全以真心相待；当然，前提是你爱得同我一样彻底。"

"欧仁妮！"我叫出声来，对着这个名字又生出一阵新的狂喜。

最后，我对她说，我多么迫不及待地想踏上我们的旅程；说话间，一种奇异的感觉突然攫住了我。它丝毫也不像晕眩。我找不到词句形容它，只觉得脑中猛地一阵拘紧；仿佛包裹着大脑的那层膜——如果真有这么一层膜——缩紧了，变得僵直。

"亲爱的理查德！怎么了？"她叫道，眼里满是惊怖。"天哪！你病了吗？我求你坐下；坐在这把椅子上。"她几乎是强把我按进椅子里；我已无力作丝毫反抗。接着袭来的那些感觉，我认得太真切了。我仰靠在椅子里，这时已发不出一个音节，阖不上眼皮，转不动眼球，动不了一丝肌肉。几秒钟之间，我已滑入了那种状态——同我随德·哈蒙维尔侯爵夜车驶近巴黎时熬过那许多骇人时光的状态，一模一样。

这位女士的悲恸激烈而喧腾。她似乎全然忘了恐惧。她喊着我的名字，摇我的肩，举起我的手臂又任它落下，一面用撕心裂肺的话句句恳求我，求我作出最微小的一丝生命迹象，并发誓若我理也不理，她就自寻短见。

这阵呼天抢地，过了一两分钟便骤然平息。这位女士完全安静下来，冷静得很。她拿起一支蜡烛，用十足办公事的架势站在我面前——脸确实白，白得很，但那表情只是凝神细致的审视，掺着一丝惊骇。她让蜡烛在我眼前缓缓移动，分明在观察效果。然后她放下蜡烛，把一只手铃急促地摇了两三下。她把两只匣子（我是说她装珠宝的那只）同我的钱箱并排放在桌上；我还看见她仔细锁上了通向那间房——我方才在其中啜饮咖啡的那间——的门。

## XXIV Hope / XXIV 希望

===Original===
She had scarcely set down my heavy box, which she seemed to have considerable difficulty in raising on the table, when the door of the room in which I had seen the coffin, opened, and a sinister and unexpected apparition entered.

It was the Count de St. Alyre, who had been, as I have told you, reported to me to be, for some considerable time, on his way to Père la Chaise. He stood before me for a moment, with the frame of the doorway and a background of darkness enclosing him, like a portrait. His slight, mean figure was draped in the deepest mourning. He had a pair of black gloves in his hand, and his hat with crape round it.

When he was not speaking his face showed signs of agitation; his mouth was puckering and working. He looked damnably wicked and frightened.

“Well, my dear Eugenie? Well, child—eh? Well, it all goes admirably?”

“Yes,” she answered, in a low, hard tone. “But you and Planard should not have left that door open.”

This she said sternly. “He went in there and looked about wherever he liked; it was fortunate he did not move aside the lid of the coffin.”

“Planard should have seen to that,” said the Count, sharply. “*Ma foi!* I can’t be everywhere!” He advanced half-a-dozen short quick steps into the room toward me, and placed his glasses to his eyes.

“Monsieur Beckett,” he cried sharply, two or three times, “Hi! don’t you know me?”

He approached and peered more closely in my face; raised my hand and shook it, calling me again, then let it drop, and said—“It has set in admirably, my pretty mignonne. When did it commence?”

The Countess came and stood beside him, and looked at me steadily for some seconds.

You can’t conceive the effect of the silent gaze of those two pairs of evil eyes.

The lady glanced to where, I recollected, the mantelpiece stood, and upon it a clock, the regular click of which I sharply heard.

“Four—five—six minutes and a half,” she said slowly, in a cold hard way.

“Brava! Bravissima! my beautiful queen! my little Venus! my Joan of Arc! my heroine! my paragon of women!”

He was gloating on me with an odious curiosity, smiling, as he groped backward with his thin brown fingers to find the lady’s hand; but she, not (I dare say) caring for his caresses, drew back a little.

===Chinese===
她刚把那只沉重的箱子放下——搬上桌时她似乎相当吃力——我见到棺材的那间房的门就开了，一个凶险而意外的幽灵走了进来。

来者是圣阿利尔伯爵——如我告诉过你的，相当一段时间以来，据他们向我通报，他都正在去拉雪兹神父公墓的路上。他在我面前站了片刻，门框和身后的一片黑暗把他框在其中，宛如一幅肖像。他瘦小寒碜的身形裹在一身重孝里。手里捏着一副黑手套，帽子上一圈黑纱。

他不说话的时候，脸上露出激动不安的迹象；嘴巴一皱一皱地抽动。他看上去邪恶得要命，又吓得不轻。

"怎么样，我亲爱的欧仁妮？怎么样，孩子——呃？一切进展得漂亮吧？"

"是，"她用低沉、生硬的口吻答道，"可你和普拉纳尔不该让那扇门开着。"

这话说得很严厉。"他进去了，爱往哪儿看就往哪儿看；万幸他没去掀棺材盖。"

"那该普拉纳尔操心，"伯爵尖声说，"说真的（Ma foi）！我又不能分身处处都在！"他迈着碎步，急急地朝我这边走了六小步，把眼镜架到眼前。

"贝克特先生，"他尖声喊了两三遍，"喂！你不认得我了吗？"

他凑近细看我的脸；举起我的手摇了摇，又叫了我几声，随后任它垂下，说道——"发作得漂亮极了，我俊俏的小宝贝（mignonne）。什么时候开始的？"

伯爵夫人走过来站在他身旁，直勾勾地看了我几秒钟。

你无法想象那两双恶眼睛默默注视着人的效果。

那位夫人朝壁炉架的方向瞥了一眼——我记得那上面有一座钟，它规律的滴答声我听得分明。

"四分——五分——六分半钟，"她缓慢地说，语气冰冷生硬。

"好极了！妙极了（Brava! Bravissima）！我美丽的王后！我的小维纳斯！我的圣女贞德！我的女英雄！我的巾帼完人！"

他带着一种可憎的好奇盯着我看，脸上带笑，一面用细瘦的黄手指向身后摸索，去找那位夫人的手；可她——多半（我敢说）不稀罕他的爱抚——微微退开了些。

===Original===
“Come, *ma chère*, let us count these things. What is it? Pocketbook? Or—or—*what*?”

“It is *that*!” said the lady, pointing with a look of disgust to the box, which lay in its leather case on the table.

“Oh! Let us see—let us count—let us see,” he said, as he was unbuckling the straps with his tremulous fingers. “We must count them—we must see to it. I have pencil and pocketbook—but—where’s the key? See this cursed lock! My ———! What is it? Where’s the key?”

He was standing before the Countess, shuffling his feet, with his hands extended and all his fingers quivering.

“I have not got it; how could I? It is in his pocket, of course,” said the lady.

In another instant the fingers of the old miscreant were in my pockets: he plucked out everything they contained, and some keys among the rest.

===Chinese===
"来，亲爱的（ma chère），咱们来清点这些东西。是什么？皮夹？还是——还是——什么？"

"是那个！"那位夫人说，一脸嫌恶地指向桌上皮套里装着的那只箱子。

"哦！看看——数数——看看，"他说着，用颤抖的手指解皮带扣。"一定得数清——一定得弄明白。我有铅笔和记事本——可是——钥匙呢？瞧这该死的锁！我的——！怎么回事？钥匙在哪里？"

他站在伯爵夫人面前，两脚蹭来蹭去，双手前伸，指头全在哆嗦。

"我没拿；我怎么拿？当然在他口袋里，"那位夫人说。

转眼间，那老恶棍的手指就伸进了我的口袋：他把里面的东西统统掏了出来，其中就有几把钥匙。

===Original===
I lay in precisely the state in which I had been during my drive with the Marquis to Paris. This wretch I knew was about to rob me. The whole drama, and the Countess’s role in it, I could not yet comprehend. I could not be sure—so much more presence of mind and histrionic resource have women than fall to the lot of our clumsy sex—whether the return of the Count was not, in truth, a surprise to her; and this scrutiny of the contents of my strong box, an extempore undertaking of the Count’s. But it was clearing more and more every moment: and I was destined, very soon, to comprehend minutely my appalling situation.

I had not the power of turning my eyes this way or that, the smallest fraction of a hair’s breadth. But let anyone, placed as I was at the end of a room, ascertain for himself by experiment how wide is the field of sight, without the slightest alteration in the line of vision, he will find that it takes in the entire breadth of a large room, and that up to a very short distance before him; and imperfectly, by a refraction, I believe, in the eye itself, to a point very near indeed. Next to nothing that passed in the room, therefore, was hidden from me.

The old man had, by this time, found the key. The leather case was open. The box cramped round with iron was next unlocked. He turned out its contents upon the table.

===Chinese===
我躺在那里，正处于同随侯爵驱车去巴黎时一模一样的状态。我知道这个恶棍就要抢劫我。可这整出戏，以及伯爵夫人在其中扮演的角色，我还参不透。我说不准——女人的沉着和做戏的本事，远非我们这些笨手笨脚的男性分内所能有——伯爵的归来是否其实也出乎她的意料；对我钱箱内容的清点，是否也是伯爵的临时起意。但事情一刻比一刻清楚：而且我命中注定，很快就要细细致致地明白我这骇人的处境了。

我连一丝一发地转动眼球的能力都没有。但谁只要像我当时那样处在房间一头，亲自试验一下：视线丝毫不改方向，视野能有多宽——他会发现，这样也能望尽一间大房间的整个宽度，一直到身前极近的地方；而且我相信，凭着眼睛本身的一种折射，甚至能隐约看到近在咫尺的一点。因此，房间里发生的事，几乎没有一件瞒得过我。

这时老头儿已找到了钥匙。皮套打开了。接着，那只铁条箍着的箱子也开了锁。他把里面的东西全倒在桌上。

===Original===
“Rouleaux of a hundred Napoleons each. One, two, three. Yes, quick. Write down a thousand Napoleons. One, two; yes, right. Another thousand, *write*!” And so, on and on till the gold was rapidly counted. Then came the notes.

“Ten thousand francs. *Write.* Ten thousand francs again: is it written? Another ten thousand francs: is it down? Smaller notes would have been better. They should have been smaller. These are horribly embarrassing. Bolt that door again; Planard would become unreasonable if he knew the amount. Why did you not tell him to get it in smaller notes? No matter now—go on—it can’t be helped—*write*—another ten thousand francs—another—another.” And so on, till my treasure was counted out, before my face, while I saw and heard all that passed with the sharpest distinctness, and my mental perceptions were horribly vivid. But in all other respects I was dead.

He had replaced in the box every note and rouleau as he counted it, and now having ascertained the sum total, he locked it, replaced it, very methodically, in its cover, opened a buffet in the wainscoting, and, having placed the Countess’ jewel-case and my strong box in it, he locked it; and immediately on completing these arrangements he began to complain, with fresh acrimony and maledictions of Planard’s delay.

===Chinese===
"一卷卷的拿破仑金币，每卷一百枚。一、二、三。好，快点。记下一千枚拿破仑。一、二；对，没错。再一千，记上！"就这样一卷接一卷，金币很快点完了。然后是钞票。

"一万法郎。记下。又一万法郎：记了没有？再一万法郎：落笔了吗？小面额的钞票本来更好。该换小票的。这些大票麻烦死人。再把那扇门闩上；普拉纳尔要是知道数目，会蛮不讲理的。你怎么不叫他换成小票？罢了——接着来——没法子了——记——又一万法郎——再来——再来。"就这样下去，直到我的财宝当着我的面点清。这一切我看得清清楚楚，听得真真切切，心智清明得可怕。可在其他一切方面，我已经是个死人了。

他点一张钞票、一卷金币，就往箱子里放回一张、一卷，待确知了总数，便锁上箱子，有条不紊地把它装回皮套，又打开护壁板里的一口柜子，把伯爵夫人的珠匣和我的钱箱放了进去，上了锁；这些安排刚一做完，他立刻又抱怨起来，语调愈发刻毒，咒骂普拉纳尔迟迟不来。

===Original===
He unbolted the door, looked in the dark room beyond, and listened. He closed the door again, and returned. The old man was in a fever of suspense.

“I have kept ten thousand francs for Planard,” said the Count, touching his waistcoat pocket.

“Will that satisfy him?” asked the lady.

“Why—curse him!” screamed the Count. “Has he no conscience! I’ll swear to him it’s half the entire thing.”

He and the lady again came and looked at me anxiously for awhile, in silence; and then the old Count began to grumble again about Planard, and to compare his watch with the clock. The lady seemed less impatient; she sat no longer looking at me, but across the room, so that her profile was toward me—and strangely changed, dark and witch-like it looked. My last hope died as I beheld that jaded face from which the mask had dropped. I was certain that they intended to crown their robbery by murder. Why did they not despatch me at once? What object could there be in postponing the catastrophe which would expedite their own safety. I cannot recall, even to myself, adequately the horrors unutterable that I underwent. You must suppose a real nightmare—I mean a nightmare in which the objects and the danger are real, and the spell of corporal death appears to be protractable at the pleasure of the persons who preside at your unearthly torments. I could have no doubt as to the cause of the state in which I was.

In this agony, to which I could not give the slightest expression, I saw the door of the room where the coffin had been, open slowly, and the Marquis d’Harmonville entered the room.

===Chinese===
他拉开门闩，朝外面那间黑屋子望了望，听了听，又关上门，走了回来。老头儿正焦灼得如热锅上的蚂蚁。

"我给普拉纳尔留了一万法郎，"伯爵说着，拍了拍马甲口袋。

"那能满足他吗？"那位夫人问。

"嘿——该死的！"伯爵尖声叫道，"他还有没有良心！我会对他赌咒发誓，说这就是全部的一半。"

他和那位夫人又走过来，默默而焦虑地看了我一阵；然后老伯爵又开始咕哝起普拉纳尔来，把自己的怀表同那座钟对了对。那位夫人似乎不那么心急；她坐着，不再看我，而是望着房间的另一头，只把侧脸对着我——那张脸变得出奇，阴沉，像女巫一般。眼看那张卸下了假面具的倦怠面孔，我最后一线希望熄灭了。我确信他们打算在抢劫之后，再以凶杀来收场。他们为什么不立时结果了我？把这场能保他们自身平安的灾祸往后拖，又能有什么好处？我经受的那些难以言说的恐怖，连对我自己都无法充分回想。你只能把它想成一个真实的梦魇——我是说那样一种梦魇：其中的物件与危险都是真的，而肉身死亡的法咒，仿佛可以听凭主持你这场非人折磨的人随意延长。至于我何以落到这般境地，缘由何在，我已没有丝毫怀疑。

在这种连一丝表情都无法表露的极度痛苦之中，我看见原先停放棺材的那间房的门缓缓开了，德·哈蒙维尔侯爵走了进来。

## XXV Despair / XXV 绝望

===Original===
A moment’s hope, hope violent and fluctuating, hope that was nearly torture, and then came a dialogue, and with it the terrors of despair.

“Thank heaven, Planard, you have come at last,” said the Count, taking him, with both hands, by the arm and clinging to it, and drawing him toward me. “See, look at him. It has all gone sweetly, sweetly, sweetly up to this. Shall I hold the candle for you?”

My friend d’Harmonville, Planard, whatever he was, came to me, pulling off his gloves, which he popped into his pocket.

“The candle, a little this way,” he said, and stooping over me he looked earnestly in my face. He touched my forehead, drew his hand across it, and then looked in my eyes for a time.

“Well, doctor, what do you think?” whispered the Count.

“How much did you give him?” said the Marquis, thus suddenly stunted down to a doctor.

“Seventy drops,” said the lady.

“In the hot coffee?”

“Yes; sixty in a hot cup of coffee and ten in the liqueur.”

Her voice, low and hard, seemed to me to tremble a little. It takes a long course of guilt to subjugate nature completely, and prevent those exterior signs of agitation that outlive all good.

===Chinese===
希望存在了一瞬——剧烈起伏的希望，近乎酷刑的希望；随后对话开始了，随之而来的是绝望的恐怖。

"谢天谢地，普拉纳尔，你总算来了，"伯爵说着，双手抓住他的手臂紧紧挽住，把他拉向我这边。"瞧，看看他。到这一步为止，一切顺顺当当、顺顺当当、顺顺当当。要我给你举蜡烛吗？"

我的朋友德·哈蒙维尔——或者说普拉纳尔，随他究竟是谁——朝我走来，一面脱下手套，塞进口袋。

"蜡烛，往这边偏一点，"他说着，俯下身来，恳切地端详我的脸。他碰了碰我的额头，用手在上面抹了一下，然后盯着我的眼睛看了一阵。

"怎么样，医生，你看如何？"伯爵悄声问。

"你们给他用了多少？"侯爵说——就这样骤然缩成了一名医生。

"七十滴，"那位夫人说。

"掺在热咖啡里？"

"是；六十滴在一杯热咖啡里，十滴在利口酒里。"

她那低沉生硬的声音，在我听来微微发颤。非得经过漫长的作恶生涯，才能彻底制服天性，压下那些在一切善念消亡之后仍不肯散去的激动表象。

===Original===
The doctor, however, was treating me as coolly as he might a subject which he was about to place on the dissecting-table for a lecture.

He looked into my eyes again for awhile, took my wrist, and applied his fingers to the pulse.

“That action suspended,” he said to himself.

Then again he placed something that, for the moment I saw it, looked like a piece of gold-beater’s leaf, to my lips, holding his head so far that his own breathing could not affect it.

“Yes,” he said in soliloquy, very low.

Then he plucked my shirt-breast open and applied the stethoscope, shifted it from point to point, listened with his ear to its end, as if for a very far off sound, raised his head, and said, in like manner, softly to himself, “All appreciable action of the lungs has subsided.”

Then turning from the sound, as I conjectured, he said:

“Seventy drops, allowing ten for waste, ought to hold him fast for six hours and a half—that is ample. The experiment I tried in the carriage was only thirty drops, and showed a highly sensitive brain. It would not do to kill him, you know. You are certain you did not exceed *seventy*?”

“Perfectly,” said the lady.

“If he were to die the evaporation would be arrested, and foreign matter, some of it poisonous, would be found in the stomach, don’t you see? If you are doubtful, it would be well to use the stomach-pump.”

“Dearest Eugenie, be frank, be frank, do be frank,” urged the Count.

“I am *not* doubtful, I am *certain*,” she answered.

“How long ago, exactly? I told you to observe the time.”

“I did; the minute-hand was exactly there, under the point of that Cupid’s foot.”

“It will last, then, probably for seven hours. He will recover then; the evaporation will be complete, and not one particle of the fluid will remain in the stomach.”

===Chinese===
而那位医生摆弄我的那份冷静，就像摆弄一具即将放上解剖台示教的尸体。

他又对着我的眼睛端详了一阵，捏起我的手腕，把手指搭上脉搏。

"搏动止了，"他自言自语。

接着他又把一样东西贴到我唇边——那一瞥之间，它像是片金箔——头偏得远远的，让自己的呼吸不至于影响它。

"好，"他低低地自语。

然后他扯开我的衬衫前襟，放上听诊器，一处一处地挪，耳朵贴着听头听，像在听一个极遥远的声音，随后抬起头，同样轻声自语道："肺部的活动已全然止息，无从察觉了。"

然后他不再听诊，转过身来，说道：

"七十滴，除去十滴的耗损，应当能把他牢牢钉住六个半钟头——绰绰有余。我在马车里试的那一次只有三十滴，已显出他的脑子极其敏感。要知道，可不能把他弄死。你肯定没有超过七十滴？"

"万分肯定，"那位夫人说。

"他若是死了，蒸发作用就会中止，胃里便会验出异物，有些还是毒性的，明白吗？你要是心里没底，最好用洗胃器。"

"最亲爱的欧仁妮，坦白些，坦白些，千万坦白些，"伯爵催促道。

"我不是没底，我是有底，"她答道。

"多久之前，确切说？我叮嘱过你看时间。"

"我看了；分针恰好在那儿，正对着那丘比特的脚尖底下。"

"那么药力大概能撑七个钟头。到时候他会缓过来；药液将蒸发殆尽，胃里一星半点也不会残留。"

===Original===
It was reassuring, at all events, to hear that there was no intention to murder me. No one who has not tried it knows the terror of the approach of death, when the mind is clear, the instincts of life unimpaired, and no excitement to disturb the appreciation of that entirely new horror.

The nature and purpose of this tenderness was very, very peculiar, and as yet I had not a suspicion of it.

“You leave France, I suppose?” said the ex-Marquis.

“Yes, certainly, tomorrow,” answered the Count.

“And where do you mean to go?”

“That I have not yet settled,” he answered quickly.

“You won’t tell a friend, eh?”

“I can’t till I know. This has turned out an unprofitable affair.”

“We shall settle that by-and-by.”

“It is time we should get him lying down, eh?” said the Count, indicating me with one finger.

“Yes, we must proceed rapidly now. Are his nightshirt and nightcap—you understand—here?”

“All ready,” said the Count.

“Now, Madame,” said the doctor, turning to the lady, and making her, in spite of the emergency, a bow, “it is time you should retire.”

The lady passed into the room, in which I had taken my cup of treacherous coffee, and I saw her no more.

===Chinese===
无论如何，听说他们无意害我性命，总归让人宽心。除非亲身领受，谁也不知道死神步步逼近时的那种恐怖——那时头脑清明，生命的本能完好无损，又没有丝毫亢奋来搅乱你对这全然陌生的新恐怖的体察。

这份体贴的性质与用意，非常、非常特别，而我当时对此没有半点疑心。

"你们就要离开法国了吧，我想？"前侯爵说。

"是，当然，明天就走，"伯爵答道。

"打算去哪里？"

"这个还没定下来，"他答得很快。

"不肯告诉朋友，嗯？"

"我自己还不知道呢，怎么告诉。这桩买卖结果没赚头。"

"这个我们回头再算。"

"该让他躺下了吧，呃？"伯爵说，一根手指指了指我。

"是，现在得抓紧办了。他的睡衣睡帽——你懂的——都在这儿吗？"

"都备好了，"伯爵说。

"好了，夫人，"医生说着转向那位夫人，尽管事态紧急，仍向她鞠了一躬，"您该退下了。"

那位夫人走进了我喝下那杯奸计咖啡的房间，从此我再没见过她。

===Original===
The Count took a candle, and passed through the door at the further end of the room, returning with a roll of linen in his hand. He bolted first one door, then the other.

They now, in silence, proceeded to undress me rapidly. They were not many minutes in accomplishing this.

What the doctor had termed my nightshirt, a long garment which reached below my feet, was now on, and a cap, that resembled a female nightcap more than anything I had ever seen upon a male head, was fitted upon mine, and tied under my chin.

And now, I thought, I shall be laid in a bed, to recover how I can, and, in the meantime, the conspirators will have escaped with their booty, and pursuit be in vain.

This was my best hope at the time; but it was soon clear that their plans were very different.

The Count and Planard now went, together, into the room that lay straight before me. I heard them talking low, and a sound of shuffling feet; then a long rumble; it suddenly stopped; it recommenced; it continued; side by side they came in at the door, their backs toward me. They were dragging something along the floor that made a continued boom and rumble, but they interposed between me and it, so that I could not see it until they had dragged it almost beside me; and then, merciful heaven! I saw it plainly enough. It was the coffin I had seen in the next room. It lay now flat on the floor, its edge against the chair in which I sat. Planard removed the lid. The coffin was empty.

===Chinese===
伯爵拿起一支蜡烛，穿过房间尽头的门出去，回来时手里拿着一卷亚麻布。他先闩上一扇门，又闩上另一扇。

接着，他们默不作声地动手，飞快地剥去我的衣裳。没几分钟就办完了。

医生称作睡衣的那件东西——一件长过脚面的衣裳——已经穿在我身上；一顶帽子也扣上了我的头，系在了下巴底下；它比我见过的任何男人头上的东西都更像一顶女人睡帽。

这时我想，他们要把我放上一张床，随我自生自灭地缓过来，而这期间，阴谋家们早已携赃远遁，追之莫及。

这是我当时最好的指望；可很快便见分晓，他们的打算完全是另一回事。

伯爵和普拉纳尔这时一同走进正对着我视线的那间房。我听见他们低声说话，还有脚步拖沓之声；接着是长长的一阵隆隆；戛然而止；又响起来；继续着；两人并肩从门口走进来，背朝着我。他们在地板上拖着一件东西，发出连绵的轰响，可他们身子挡在我和它之间，直到把它拖到我近旁，我才看见它；那时——仁慈的上天啊！我看得再清楚不过了。那正是我在隔壁房间见过的棺材。此刻它平躺在地板上，边沿抵着我坐的椅子。普拉纳尔掀开棺盖。棺材是空的。

## XXVI Catastrophe / XXVI 惨变

===Original===
“Those seem to be good horses, and we change on the way,” said Planard. “You give the men a Napoleon or two; we must do it within three hours and a quarter. Now, come; I’ll lift him, upright, so as to place his feet in their proper berth, and you must keep them together, and draw the white shirt well down over them.”

In another moment I was placed, as he described, sustained in Planard’s arms, standing at the foot of the coffin, and so lowered backward, gradually, till I lay my length in it. Then the man, whom he called Planard, stretched my arms by my sides, and carefully arranged the frills at my breast, and the folds of the shroud, and after that, taking his stand at the foot of the coffin, made a survey which seemed to satisfy him.

The Count, who was very methodical, took my clothes, which had just been removed, folded them rapidly together and locked them up, as I afterwards heard, in one of the three presses which opened by doors in the panel.

===Chinese===
"这几匹马看着不错，路上还可以换马，"普拉纳尔说，"赏车夫们一两枚拿破仑金币；我们必须在三个钟头一刻之内赶到。来吧；我把他抱直了，好把他的脚安放到该在的位置，你得把他的双脚并拢，把白衬衫好好拉下来盖住它们。"

转眼间，我便如他所说的那样，被普拉纳尔抱在臂弯里，立在棺材脚端，然后被缓缓向后放低，直到我全身平躺在里面。接着，这个被他唤作普拉纳尔的人，把我的双臂抻直放在身体两侧，细心理好我胸前的褶边和裹尸布的褶皱，随后站到棺材脚端，打量了一番，似乎颇为满意。

伯爵是个极有条理的人，他把我刚被剥下的衣裳拿去，飞快叠好，锁了起来——后来我听说，锁进了壁板里三扇柜门后的一口柜子里。

===Original===
I now understood their frightful plan. This coffin had been prepared for *me*; the funeral of St. Amand was a sham to mislead inquiry; I had myself given the order at Père la Chaise, signed it, and paid the fees for the interment of the fictitious Pierre de St. Amand, whose place I was to take, to lie in his coffin, with his name on the plate above my breast, and with a ton of clay packed down upon me; to waken from this catalepsy, after I had been for hours in the grave, there to perish by a death the most horrible that imagination can conceive.

If, hereafter, by any caprice of curiosity or suspicion, the coffin should be exhumed, and the body it enclosed examined, no chemistry could detect a trace of poison, nor the most cautious examination the slightest mark of violence.

I had myself been at the utmost pains to mystify inquiry, should my disappearance excite surmises, and had even written to my few correspondents in England to tell them that they were not to look for a letter from me for three weeks at least.

In the moment of my guilty elation death had caught me, and there was no escape. I tried to pray to God in my unearthly panic, but only thoughts of terror, judgment, and eternal anguish, crossed the distraction of my immediate doom.

I must not try to recall what is indeed indescribable—the multiform horrors of my own thoughts. I will relate, simply, what befell, every detail of which remains sharp in my memory as if cut in steel.

===Chinese===
我如今明白了他们那可怕的计划。这口棺材是为我备下的；圣阿芒的葬礼是一场掩人耳目的骗局；在拉雪兹神父公墓，下葬的命令是我亲自下的、亲自签的字、亲自付的钱——葬的是那个虚构的皮埃尔·德·圣阿芒，而我就是要顶替他的人：躺进他的棺材，胸前上方的铭牌钉着他的名字，一吨黏土压下来夯在我身上；待我在坟墓里待过几个钟头，从这场僵住症中醒来，就在那里，以想象所能构思的最恐怖的死法，断了性命。

将来，倘若出于什么好奇或疑心的古怪念头，这口棺材被掘出来，里面的尸身受到检验，任何化学手段都验不出一丝毒迹，最细致的查验也找不出半点暴力的痕迹。

而我自己也曾竭尽全力布下迷阵，以防我的失踪引起猜疑，甚至还写信给在英国寥寥几位与我通信的人，告诉他们至少三个星期内不必指望收到我的信。

在我罪孽的得意忘形之际，死亡攫住了我，再无脱逃之路。我在这非人的惊惶中试图向上帝祈祷，可掠过心头的，只有恐怖、审判与永刑的念头，在我眼前死期的纷乱里穿来穿去。

我自己种种念头那千形百状的恐怖，实在无法形容，我也不敢再去回想。我只平铺直叙地讲述所发生的事，其中每一个细节都锋利地留在我记忆里，如同刻在钢上。

===Original===
“The undertaker’s men are in the hall,” said the Count.

“They must not come till this is fixed,” answered Planard. “Be good enough to take hold of the lower part while I take this end.” I was not left long to conjecture what was coming, for in a few seconds more something slid across, a few inches above my face, and entirely excluded the light, and muffled sound, so that nothing that was not very distinct reached my ears henceforward; but very distinctly came the working of a turnscrew, and the crunching home of screws in succession. Than these vulgar sounds, no doom spoken in thunder could have been more tremendous.

The rest I must relate, not as it then reached my ears, which was too imperfectly and interruptedly to supply a connected narrative, but as it was afterwards told me by other people.

===Chinese===
"殡葬的人在大厅里了，"伯爵说。

"这事没弄好之前不能让他们进来，"普拉纳尔答道，"劳驾，你抬那头，我抬这头。"没容我猜想多久接下来是什么，只几秒钟，一样东西便滑了过来，在我脸上方几英寸处，把光完全遮死了，也闷住了声响，从此凡不是特别清晰的动静，都到不了我耳中；但那螺丝刀的转动声，还有螺丝一颗接一颗咬紧到位的嘎吱声，却听得清清楚楚。比起这些粗鄙的声音，雷霆之中宣判的死刑也不会更惊心动魄。

接下来的事，我不能按当时传到我耳中的样子来讲——那太破碎、太断续，串不成连贯的叙述——只能按旁人后来告诉我的样子讲。

===Original===
The coffin-lid being screwed down, the two gentlemen arranged the room, and adjusted the coffin so that it lay perfectly straight along the boards, the Count being specially anxious that there should be no appearance of hurry or disorder in the room, which might have suggested remark and conjecture.

When this was done, Doctor Planard said he would go to the hall to summon the men who were to carry the coffin out and place it in the hearse. The Count pulled on his black gloves, and held his white handkerchief in his hand, a very impressive chief-mourner. He stood a little behind the head of the coffin, awaiting the arrival of the persons who accompanied Planard, and whose fast steps he soon heard approaching.

Planard came first. He entered the room through the apartment in which the coffin had been originally placed. His manner was changed; there was something of a swagger in it.

===Chinese===
棺盖钉严之后，两位先生收拾了房间，把棺材摆正，让它沿着地板放得笔直；伯爵格外在意，屋里不能露出丝毫匆促杂乱的样子，免得惹人议论和猜疑。

这些做完后，普拉纳尔医生说要去大厅叫抬棺的人，好把棺材抬出去装上灵车。伯爵戴上黑手套，手里执着白手帕，一副极像样的丧主气派。他站在棺材头端稍后的地方，等着随普拉纳尔一同来的人；很快，他便听见了他们急促逼近的脚步声。

普拉纳尔走在前头。他从原先停放棺材的那个套间走进屋来。他的气派变了，带上几分昂首阔步的神气。

===Original===
“Monsieur le Comte,” he said, as he strode through the door, followed by half-a-dozen persons. “I am sorry to have to announce to you a most unseasonable interruption. Here is Monsieur Carmaignac, a gentleman holding an office in the police department, who says that information to the effect that large quantities of smuggled English and other goods have been distributed in this neighbourhood, and that a portion of them is concealed in your house. I have ventured to assure him, of my own knowledge, that nothing can be more false than that information, and that you would be only too happy to throw open for his inspection, at a moment’s notice, every room, closet, and cupboard in your house.”

“Most assuredly,” exclaimed the Count, with a stout voice, but a very white face. “Thank you, my good friend, for having anticipated me. I will place my house and keys at his disposal, for the purpose of his scrutiny, so soon as he is good enough to inform me, of what specific contraband goods he comes in search.”

“The Count de St. Alyre will pardon me,” answered Carmaignac, a little dryly. “I am forbidden by my instructions to make that disclosure; and that I *am* instructed to make a general search, this warrant will sufficiently apprise Monsieur le Comte.”

“Monsieur Carmaignac, may I hope,” interposed Planard, “that you will permit the Count de St. Alyre to attend the funeral of his kinsman, who lies here, as you see—” (he pointed to the plate upon the coffin)—“and to convey whom to Père la Chaise, a hearse waits at this moment at the door.”

===Chinese===
"伯爵先生，"他跨进门来，身后跟着六七个人，说道，"很抱歉，我不得不向您通报一桩极不合时宜的搅扰。这位是卡马尼亚克先生，警务部门的一位官员。他说，据密报，大批走私的英国货和其他货物散匿在本乡一带，其中一部分就藏在您府上。我冒昧地以我所知向他担保，这情报再虚假不过；还说您必定乐于随时把府上每一间房、每一格小间、每一口橱柜都敞开任他查验。"

"那是自然，"伯爵叫道，嗓音硬气，脸色却白得很。"谢谢你，我的好朋友，替我抢先说了。只要他肯行好告诉我，他究竟来搜的是什么具体的走私货，我马上就把宅子和钥匙都交由他查个遍。"

"圣阿利尔伯爵会原谅我的，"卡马尼亚克答道，语气有点干巴巴，"我的指令不许我透露这一层；至于我奉命作全面搜查这一点，这张搜查状足以让伯爵先生知悉了。"

"卡马尼亚克先生，我可否指望，"普拉纳尔插话道，"您允许圣阿利尔伯爵送一送他亲人的葬礼——他就在这里躺着，如您所见——"（他指了指棺材上的铭牌）"——为送他去拉雪兹神父公墓，一辆灵车此刻正在门口候着。"

===Original===
“That, I regret to say, I cannot permit. My instructions are precise; but the delay, I trust, will be but trifling. Monsieur le Comte will not suppose for a moment that I suspect him; but we have a duty to perform, and I must act as if I did. When I am ordered to search, I search; things are sometimes hid in such *bizarre* places. I can’t say, for instance, what that coffin may contain.”

“The body of my kinsman, Monsieur Pierre de St. Amand,” answered the Count, loftily.

“Oh! then you’ve seen him?”

“Seen him? Often, too often?” The Count was evidently a good deal moved.

“I mean the body?”

The Count stole a quick glance at Planard.

“N—no, Monsieur—that is, I mean only for a moment.” Another quick glance at Planard.

“But quite long enough, I fancy, to recognize him?” insinuated that gentleman.

“Of course—of course; instantly—perfectly. What! Pierre de St. Amand? Not know him at a glance? No, no, poor fellow, I know him too well for that.”

===Chinese===
"那个嘛，很遗憾，我不能允许。我的指令一清二楚；不过耽搁，我相信只是片刻的事。伯爵先生千万不要以为我怀疑他；但我们有职责在身，我行事就只得装作怀疑。奉命搜查，我就搜查；东西有时藏在些古怪（bizarre）得出奇的地方。比如说，那口棺材里装着什么，我就说不好。"

"我亲人的遗体，皮埃尔·德·圣阿芒先生，"伯爵居高临下地答道。

"哦！那么您见过他了？"

"见过？常见，太常见了！"伯爵显然颇为动情。

"我是说遗体？"

伯爵飞快地瞟了普拉纳尔一眼。

"没——没有，先生——就是说，只见过一小会儿。"又飞快地瞟了普拉纳尔一眼。

"不过我想，要认出他来，这点时间总还是够的吧？"那位先生旁敲侧击道。

"当然——当然；一眼便认得——认得真切。什么话！皮埃尔·德·圣阿芒？看了一眼还认不出？不，不，可怜的人，我同他太熟了，不会认不出。"

===Original===
“The things I am in search of,” said Monsieur Carmaignac, “would fit in a narrow compass—servants are so ingenious sometimes. Let us raise the lid.”

“Pardon me, Monsieur,” said the Count, peremptorily, advancing to the side of the coffin, and extending his arm across it. “I cannot permit that indignity—that desecration.”

“There shall be none, sir—simply the raising of the lid; you shall remain in the room. If it should prove as we all hope, you shall have the pleasure of one other look, really the last, upon your beloved kinsman.”

“But, sir, I can’t.”

“But, Monsieur, I must.”

“But, besides, the thing, the turnscrew, broke when the last screw was turned; and I give you my sacred honour there is nothing but the body in this coffin.”

“Of course Monsieur le Comte believes all that; but he does not know so well as I the legerdemain in use among servants, who are accustomed to smuggling. Here, Philippe, you must take off the lid of that coffin.”

===Chinese===
"我要找的东西，"卡马尼亚克先生说，"占地很小——仆人们有时手巧得很。咱们把棺盖掀开吧。"

"恕难从命，先生，"伯爵斩钉截铁地说，抢步到棺材边上，伸出一条手臂横在棺上，"我不能容许那般羞辱——那般亵渎。"

"不会有任何亵渎的，先生——不过是掀一掀盖子；您可以留在房里。倘若结果如我们所愿，您还能有幸再看一眼您亲爱的亲人——真正是最后一眼。"

"可是，先生，我不能。"

"可是，先生，我必须。"

"再说，那家伙——螺丝刀——拧最后一颗螺丝的时候断掉了；我以我神圣的名誉担保，这棺材里除了遗体，什么也没有。"

"伯爵先生自然信得过这一切；只是他不像我这样深知那些惯于走私的仆人们惯用的戏法。菲利普，来，你把那口棺材的盖子卸下来。"

===Original===
The Count protested; but Philippe—a man with a bald head, and a smirched face, looking like a working blacksmith—placed on the floor a leather bag of tools, from which, having looked at the coffin, and picked with his nail at the screw-heads, he selected a turnscrew, and, with a few deft twirls at each of the screws, they stood up like little rows of mushrooms, and the lid was raised. I saw the light, of which I thought I had seen my last, once more; but the axis of vision remained fixed. As I was reduced to the cataleptic state in a position nearly perpendicular, I continued looking straight before me, and thus my gaze was now fixed upon the ceiling. I saw the face of Carmaignac leaning over me with a curious frown. It seemed to me that there was no recognition in his eyes. Oh, heaven! that I could have uttered were it but one cry! I saw the dark, mean mask of the little Count staring down at me from the other side; the face of the pseudo-marquis also peering at me, but not so full in the line of vision; there were other faces also.

“I see, I see,” said Carmaignac, withdrawing. “Nothing of the kind there.”

===Chinese===
伯爵还在抗议；可是菲利普——一个秃头、花脸、模样像个打铁匠的人——把一只皮革工具袋放在地上，看了看棺材，用指甲剔了剔螺头，从中挑出一把螺丝刀，对着每颗螺丝利落地拧了几转，螺丝便像一排排小蘑菇般立了起来，棺盖掀开了。我又见到了光——我原以为已与它永诀的光；可视线仍僵直不动。因为我陷入僵住状态时，身子近乎笔直，我便一直径直望着前方；于是一躺进棺材，目光便落在了天花板上。我看见卡马尼亚克的脸俯视着我，眉头好奇地皱着。我看他眼里似乎没有认出谁来。哦，上天！哪怕能喊出一声也好啊！我看见小个子伯爵那张阴沉猥琐的假面，从另一侧死死盯着我；那位假侯爵的脸也探过来端详我，只是不那么正对视线；此外还有别的面孔。

"看见了，看见了，"卡马尼亚克一面退开一面说，"那里头没有这类东西。"

===Original===
“You will be good enough to direct your man to readjust the lid of the coffin, and to fix the screws,” said the Count, taking courage; “and—and—really the funeral *must* proceed. It is not fair to the people who have but moderate fees for night-work, to keep them hour after hour beyond the time.”

“Count de St. Alyre, you shall go in a very few minutes. I will direct, just now, all about the coffin.”

The Count looked toward the door, and there saw a gendarme; and two or three more grave and stalwart specimens of the same force were also in the room. The Count was very uncomfortably excited; it was growing insupportable.

“As this gentleman makes a difficulty about my attending the obsequies of my kinsman, I will ask you, Planard, to accompany the funeral in my stead.”

“In a few minutes,” answered the incorrigible Carmaignac. “I must first trouble you for the key that opens that press.”

He pointed direct at the press, in which the clothes had just been locked up.

“I—I have no objection,” said the Count—“none, of course; only they have not been used for an age. I’ll direct someone to look for the key.”

“If you have not got it about you, it is quite unnecessary. Philippe, try your skeleton-keys with that press. I want it opened. Whose clothes are these?” inquired Carmaignac when, the press having been opened, he took out the suit that had been placed there scarcely two minutes since.
