===Chinese===
"有劳您吩咐您的人，把棺盖重新盖好，把螺丝拧上，"伯爵壮起胆子说，"还有——还有——葬礼实在必须进行了。人家夜里当差挣的不过区区几文，把他们一个钟头一个钟头地拖过时辰，太不公平了。"

"圣阿利尔伯爵，再过几分钟您就走。棺材的事，我马上吩咐。"

伯爵朝门口望去，看见一名宪兵；房间里还有两三名同样庄重魁伟的宪兵。伯爵烦躁难安；这局面越来越叫人受不住了。

"既然这位先生对我送亲人下葬诸多为难，那就请你，普拉纳尔，代我随灵车走一趟吧。"

"再过几分钟，"那个不可救药的卡马尼亚克答道，"我得先劳驾您交出开那口柜子的钥匙。"

他直指着那口柜子——我的衣裳刚被锁进去的那口。

"我——我没有异议，"伯爵说，"当然没有；只是那些柜子多年没动用了。我吩咐人去找钥匙。"

"您若没随身带着，那大可不必。菲利普，拿你的万能钥匙开那口柜子。我要它打开。这是谁的衣服？"柜门一开，卡马尼亚克取出那套放进去还不到两分钟的衣裳，问道。

===Original===
“I can’t say,” answered the Count. “I know nothing of the contents of that press. A roguish servant, named Lablais, whom I dismissed about a year ago, had the key. I have not seen it open for ten years or more. The clothes are probably his.

“Here are visiting cards, see, and here a marked pocket-handkerchief—‘R. B.’ upon it. He must have stolen them from a person named Beckett—R. Beckett. ‘Mr. Beckett, Berkley Square,’ the card says; and, my faith! here’s a watch and a bunch of seals; one of them with the initials ‘R. B.’ upon it. That servant, Lablais, must have been a consummate rogue!”

“So he was; you are right, sir.”

“It strikes me that he possibly stole these clothes,” continued Carmaignac, “from the man in the coffin, who, in that case, would be Monsieur Beckett, and not Monsieur de St. Amand. For, wonderful to relate, Monsieur, the watch is still going! That man in the coffin, I believe, is not dead, but simply drugged. And for having robbed and intended to murder him, I arrest you, Nicolas de la Marque, Count de St. Alyre.”

===Chinese===
"我说不上，"伯爵答道，"那口柜子里有什么我一概不知。有个刁滑的仆人，名叫拉布莱，约一年前被我辞退了，钥匙在他手里。我有十年开外没见这柜子开过。这衣裳多半是他的。"

"这儿有名片，瞧，还有一条做了记号的手帕——上面绣着'R. B.'。他准是偷自一个姓贝克特的人——R. 贝克特。名片上写着：'贝克特先生，伯克利广场'；乖乖！还有一块怀表和一串图章；其中一枚上就刻着'R. B.'的起首字母。那个仆人拉布莱，一定是个彻头彻尾的惯贼！"

"他正是；您说得对，先生。"

"依我看，这些衣裳，他多半是偷自棺材里的那位，"卡马尼亚克继续道，"果真如此，那人便是贝克特先生，而不是德·圣阿芒先生了。因为，说来奇怪，先生，这块表还在走呢！棺材里那个人，我相信并没有死，只是被下了药。而您，因抢劫了他并图谋害他性命，我逮捕您，尼古拉·德·拉马尔克，圣阿利尔伯爵。"

===Original===
In another moment the old villain was a prisoner. I heard his discordant voice break quaveringly into sudden vehemence and volubility; now croaking—now shrieking, as he oscillated between protests, threats, and impious appeals to the God who will “judge the secrets of men!” And thus lying and raving, he was removed from the room, and placed in the same coach with his beautiful and abandoned accomplice, already arrested; and, with two gendarmes sitting beside them, they were immediately driving at a rapid pace towards the Conciergerie.

There were now added to the general chorus two voices, very different in quality; one was that of the gasconading Colonel Gaillarde, who had with difficulty been kept in the background up to this; the other was that of my jolly friend Whistlewick, who had come to identify me.

===Chinese===
转眼之间，那老恶棍已成阶下囚。我听见他那刺耳的声音哆嗦着爆发出突兀的激切与滔滔不绝；时而嘶嘎——时而尖嚎，在抗辩、威胁和亵渎的哀告之间来回摇摆——哀告那位将要「审判人隐秘事」的上帝！他就这样一路撒着谎、发着狂，被带出房间，塞进同一辆马车，和他那位美貌而无耻的同伙——她已被先期拿获；两名宪兵分坐在他们身旁，马车当即疾驰而去，直奔巴黎古监狱（Conciergerie）。

这时，大合唱里又添了两副音色迥异的嗓音：一副属于吹牛皮的盖亚尔上校——到这时为止，好不容易才把他按在台后；另一副属于我那位快活的朋友惠斯尔威克，他是来认领我的。

===Original===
I shall tell you, just now, how this project against my property and life, so ingenious and monstrous, was exploded. I must first say a word about myself. I was placed in a hot bath, under the direction of Planard, as consummate a villain as any of the gang, but now thoroughly in the interests of the prosecution. Thence I was laid in a warm bed, the window of the room being open. These simple measures restored me in about three hours; I should otherwise, probably, have continued under the spell for nearly seven.

The practices of these nefarious conspirators had been carried on with consummate skill and secrecy. Their dupes were led, as I was, to be themselves auxiliary to the mystery which made their own destruction both safe and certain.

A search was, of course, instituted. Graves were opened in Père la Chaise. The bodies exhumed had lain there too long, and were too much decomposed to be recognized. One only was identified. The notice for the burial, in this particular case, had been signed, the order given, and the fees paid, by Gabriel Gaillarde, who was known to the official clerk, who had to transact with him this little funereal business. The very trick, that had been arranged for me, had been successfully practised in his case. The person for whom the grave had been ordered, was purely fictitious; and Gabriel Gaillarde himself filled the coffin, on the cover of which that false name was inscribed as well as upon a tombstone over the grave. Possibly, the same honour, under my pseudonym, may have been intended for me.

===Chinese===
这桩针对我财产和性命的阴谋，如此精巧又如此狠毒，是如何败露的，我稍后就讲。先得交代一句我自己。人们把我放进一个热水浴缸——由普拉纳尔指导操办；此人与那伙人里任何一个一样，都是个十恶不赦的坏蛋，不过如今已彻底站在控方一边。随后我被安置在一张温暖的床上，房间的窗子敞开着。这些简单的法子，大约三个钟头就让我缓了过来；不然的话，我很可能会在那道魔咒下继续躺上近七个钟头。

这伙恶毒的阴谋家的勾当，一向办得滴水不漏、隐秘至极。受骗的人像我一样，被引得亲身为那团迷雾出力，而这团迷雾，恰恰使他们自己的毁灭既稳妥又必然。

当然，随后进行了搜查。拉雪兹神父公墓的坟墓被掘开了。掘出的尸体入土太久，腐烂得无法辨认。只有一具验明了身份。那一例的下葬通知，是加布里埃尔·盖亚尔亲笔签署、亲口下令、亲手付费的——经手这桩小小丧事的公墓职员认识他。为我安排的那同一套诡计，在他身上早已得逞。申请墓穴时用的那个人纯属虚构；躺在棺材里的，正是加布里埃尔·盖亚尔本人；那口棺材的盖板上、墓穴上方的墓碑上，刻的都是那个假名字。说不定，同样的「哀荣」，原也打算用我的假名赐给我呢。

===Original===
The identification was curious. This Gabriel Gaillarde had had a bad fall from a runaway horse, about five years before his mysterious disappearance. He had lost an eye and some teeth, in this accident, besides sustaining a fracture of the right leg, immediately above the ankle. He had kept the injuries to his face as profound a secret as he could. The result was, that the glass eye which had done duty for the one he had lost, remained in the socket, slightly displaced, of course, but recognizable by the “artist” who had supplied it.

More pointedly recognizable were the teeth, peculiar in workmanship, which one of the ablest dentists in Paris had himself adapted to the chasms, the cast of which, owing to peculiarities in the accident, he happened to have preserved. This cast precisely fitted the gold plate found in the mouth of the skull. The mark, also, above the ankle, in the bone, where it had reunited, corresponded exactly with the place where the fracture had knit in the limb of Gabriel Gaillarde.

The Colonel, his younger brother, had been furious about the disappearance of Gabriel, and still more so about that of his money, which he had long regarded as his proper keepsake, whenever death should remove his brother from the vexations of living. He had suspected for a long time, for certain adroitly discovered reasons, that the Count de St. Alyre and the beautiful lady, his companion, countess, or whatever else she was, had pigeoned him. To this suspicion were added some others of a still darker kind; but in their first shape, rather the exaggerated reflections of his fury, ready to believe anything, than well-defined conjectures.

At length an accident had placed the Colonel very nearly upon the right scent; a chance, possibly lucky for himself, had apprized the scoundrel Planard that the conspirators—himself among the number—were in danger. The result was that he made terms for himself, became an informer, and concerted with the police this visit made to the Château de la Carque, at the critical moment when every measure had been completed that was necessary to construct a perfect case against his guilty accomplices.

===Chinese===
身份的辨认颇有趣。这位加布里埃尔·盖亚尔，在他神秘失踪之前约五年，曾从一匹惊马上重重摔下，丢了一只眼睛和几颗牙，右腿踝骨上方还断了一截。他把自己的面部伤情瞒得密不透风。结果，那只顶替失去的眼球的玻璃假眼，还留在眼窝里，当然略有偏移，但经手制配的「艺术家」一眼便认了出来。

更确凿可辨的是那副牙齿，做工特殊，是巴黎一位最能干的牙医亲手为他的缺牙配的；又因那次事故的种种特别之处，牙医恰巧保存着缺口的模型。这模型，同颅骨口中找到的金质牙板严丝合缝。踝骨上方骨骼上愈合的痕迹，也同加布里埃尔·盖亚尔腿上骨折接合的位置分毫不差。

上校是加布里埃尔的弟弟，对哥哥的失踪暴跳如雷，对他的钱的失踪更是怒不可遏——那笔钱，他早已视为自己名正言顺的纪念品，只等死神把哥哥从人生的烦恼里接走，便归他所有。很久以来，凭着某些机巧探得的缘由，他就疑心圣阿利尔伯爵和那位美人——他的同伙、伯爵夫人，或者随便她是什么——合伙坑了他。这疑心之外，还添了些更阴森的猜疑；只是那些猜疑起初更像是他的怒火放大的影子，什么都肯信，还算不得轮廓分明的推断。

终于，一次意外让上校几乎摸到了正路；又是一个契机——对他自己来说或许是走运——让恶棍普拉纳尔得知，阴谋家们——他也在其列——大祸临头了。结果，他为自己讨价还价，成了告密者，同警方密谋安排了这趟拉卡尔克城堡之行，时机掐在每一个必要环节都已齐备、足以给他的同伙们织成一条完整铁案的节骨眼上。

===Original===
I need not describe the minute industry or forethought with which the police agents collected all the details necessary to support the case. They had brought an able physician, who, even had Planard failed, would have supplied the necessary medical evidence.

My trip to Paris, you will believe, had not turned out quite so agreeably as I had anticipated. I was the principal witness for the prosecution in this cause célèbre, with all the *agrémens* that attend that enviable position. Having had an escape, as my friend Whistlewick said, “with a squeak” for my life, I innocently fancied that I should have been an object of considerable interest to Parisian society; but, a good deal to my mortification, I discovered that I was the object of a good-natured but contemptuous merriment. I was a *balourd*, a *benêt*, *un âne*, and figured even in caricatures. I became a sort of public character, a dignity,

> “Unto which I was not born,”

and from which I fled as soon as I conveniently could, without even paying my friend the Marquis d’Harmonville a visit at his hospitable château.

===Chinese===
警方人员为搜集支撑本案的一切细节所下的细致功夫和先见之明，无须我赘述。他们还带了一位干练的医生，就算普拉纳尔到时变卦，他也能提供必要的医学证据。

你可以想见，我的巴黎之行，结局并不像我预想的那般惬意。在这桩轰动一时的名案（cause célèbre）里，我是控方的主要证人，享受着那个令人艳羡的位置带来的一切妙处（agrémens）。用我朋友惠斯尔威克的话说，我这条命是「险之又险」捡回来的；我天真地以为自己会成为巴黎社会大为关注的对象；可是叫我颇没面子的是，我发现我成了一场善意却轻蔑的哄笑的对象。我是个傻瓜（balourd）、笨蛋（benêt）、一头驴（un âne），甚至上了漫画。我成了个人物，一种尊贵的身份，

> 「这样的尊荣，我生来便无份，」

一有机会脱身，我便从这身份里逃了出来，连我那位朋友德·哈蒙维尔侯爵好客的城堡，都没去拜访。

===Original===
The Marquis escaped scot-free. His accomplice, the Count, was executed. The fair Eugenie, under extenuating circumstances—consisting, so far as I could discover of her good looks—got off for six years’ imprisonment.

Colonel Gaillarde recovered some of his brother’s money, out of the not very affluent estate of the Count and soi-disant Countess. This, and the execution of the Count, put him in high good humour. So far from insisting on a hostile meeting, he shook me very graciously by the hand, told me that he looked upon the wound on his head, inflicted by the knob of my stick, as having been received in an honourable, though irregular duel, in which he had no disadvantage or unfairness to complain of.

I think I have only two additional details to mention. The bricks discovered in the room with the coffin, had been packed in it, in straw, to supply the weight of a dead body, and to prevent the suspicions and contradictions that might have been excited by the arrival of an empty coffin at the château.

Secondly, the Countess’s magnificent brilliants were examined by a lapidary, and pronounced to be worth about five pounds to a tragedy-queen, who happened to be in want of a suite of paste.

The Countess had figured some years before as one of the cleverest actresses on the minor stage of Paris, where she had been picked up by the Count and used as his principal accomplice.

===Chinese===
侯爵全身而退，毫发无损。他的同伙伯爵被处决了。美丽的欧仁妮，获酌情减刑——就我所能查知的，减刑的情节全在于她的美貌——落了个六年监禁。

盖亚尔上校从伯爵和那位所谓伯爵夫人并不宽裕的家产里，追回了哥哥的一部分钱。这件事，加上伯爵伏法，让他心情大好。非但没有坚持要跟我决斗一场，反而和颜悦色地同我握手，说他头上那道伤——我手杖头敲的——就当是在一场虽不正规却光明正大的决斗中受的，其中他没有吃到半点亏，也没有什么不公可抱怨。

我想还有两个细节要交代。棺材旁房间里发现的那堆砖，原先是用稻草垫着装在棺材里的，用来充作死人的分量，免得一口空棺材运进城堡，惹人起疑、对不上口径。

其次，伯爵夫人那些华美夺目的钻石，经宝石匠验看，断言大约值五英镑——正好送给哪位碰巧缺一套人造玻璃首饰的悲剧女王。

伯爵夫人几年前曾是巴黎小舞台上最伶俐的女演员之一，在那里被伯爵物色到手，成了他的头号同伙。

===Original===
She it was who, admirably disguised, had rifled my papers in the carriage on my memorable night-journey to Paris. She also had figured as the interpreting magician of the palanquin at the ball at Versailles. So far as I was affected by that elaborate mystification it was intended to reanimate my interest, which, they feared, might flag in the beautiful Countess. It had its design and action upon other intended victims also; but of them there is, at present, no need to speak. The introduction of a real corpse—procured from a person who supplied the Parisian anatomists—involved no real danger, while it heightened the mystery and kept the prophet alive in the gossip of the town and in the thoughts of the noodles with whom he had conferred.

I divided the remainder of the summer and autumn between Switzerland and Italy.

As the well-worn phrase goes, I was a sadder if not a wiser man. A great deal of the horrible impression left upon my mind was due, of course, to the mere action of nerves and brain. But serious feelings of another and deeper kind remained. My after life was ultimately formed by the shock I had then received. Those impressions led me—but not till after many years—to happier though not less serious thoughts; and I have deep reason to be thankful to the all-merciful Ruler of events, for an early and terrible lesson in the ways of sin.

===Chinese===
在我那难忘的夜车赴巴黎途中，在车厢里翻检我文件的，正是她——化装得妙极了。凡尔赛舞会上轿辇里那位解语的神巫，也是她扮的。就我受那场精心设计的迷局影响而言，其用意是重新点燃我的兴致——他们担心，我对那位美丽伯爵夫人的兴趣也许会冷却。这套把戏对其他预定的受害人也自有其算计和效用；不过那些人眼下不必多讲。引进一具真正的尸体——从一个给巴黎的解剖学家们供货的人那里弄来的——并无真正的风险，反倒加深了神秘，让那位先知在城里的闲谈中、在他接见过的那些傻子们的脑海里，一直活灵活现。

那年夏天剩下的时光和秋天，我一半在瑞士，一半在意大利度过。

套用一句用旧了的话说：我即便没有变得更聪明，也变得更沉痛了。留在我心头的那份恐怖印象，固然大半只是神经和大脑的作用。但另一种更深沉的严肃心绪留存了下来。我的后半生，归根到底是被那一次打击塑成的。那些印象引我走向——虽是在许多年之后——更幸福却同样严肃的思绪；而我深深有理由感谢那统御万事的全然仁慈的主，感谢他及早给我上了罪之道路的可怕一课。
